# D2.6 closure: large fields (review dispositions: WILSON_IT1_D26_TRIAGE_R20_2026_10_25.md to …_R28_2026_10_25.md)

Companion of WILSON_IT1_D2_STEP_MAP_2026_10_22.md ("the map"). D2.6 was never written as one item; it is the scatter of obligations listed in §0. Each is derived below or stated as OPEN in §8. Sources: the map; WILSON_IT1_SW_CLOSURE_2026_10_24.md ("SW closure"); RC_PAYMENT_PASS_2026_10_24.md; WILSON_IT1_LINE_DEFECT_2026_10_21.md ("IT-1 note"); the transcriptions Balaban\tex\<paper>\parts\pNNN_corrected.tex of CRE (pp.245, 255–278), LFR I (pp.177, 183), LFR II (p.380, pp.384–389), ER2 (p.59), RG1 (pp.253, 256–257, 266, 299), RG2 (pp.3–5, 9–13, 15), PBG (pp.416, 427–428), VAR (pp.277–281, 308), CMP98 (pp.23–24, 33, 42), REG (pp.79, 87); V3 (WILSON_FIRST_RG_STEP_V3.tex, lines cited where used); Balaban\ONE_LOOP_PROOFS.md (Cor. 8.2).

Structural statement first. After the step k → k+1 the loop is a dressed loop of step k+1 (map D2.0(3)). Its insertions of attached species (att(j, Z), att(j, coll), lw(Z), wrap(j)) are carried without Bałaban's contraction L^{−1}: they are bounded once, at production, and charged to the fundamental factor exp(−κ_j(Z) − 2p₀(g_{j(Z)})) of the large-field component Z they are attached to (LFR II p.384). So D2.6 has to show three things: (A) every insertion produced at step k+1 that is not dev has an attached species with a bound at the scale of its production step; (B) carrying the attached insertions through later steps does not multiply their total by a per-step factor; (C) the per-component total is paid by Z's fundamental factor over Z's lifetime, through Bałaban's induction pp.384–389 with an enlarged summand. (A) is §2–§4, (B) is §5, (C) is §7; §6 is the leading-word telescoping lw(Z).

## §1. Scope and the objects

- *Steps.* k+1 ≤ N: no R-operation has acted (R-h, derived, map D2.0(3) l.326–327). Every large-field component Z of Z_{k+1} = Λ_{k+1}ᶜ (2.0) carries, on each labelled term, the fundamental factor exp(−κ_{k+1}(Z) − 2p₀(g_{j(Z)})) of LFR II p.384, j(Z) the step at which the first large-field region inside Z was created. At k+1 > N the R-operation acts on the components that satisfy p.177 (i)–(ii); the observable's part of that is D2.7's. Every bound below is stated for k+1 ≤ N and is used by D2.7 unchanged for the components R does not touch.
- *Units.* Step-(k+1) units: the π_{k+1}-cube has side M in the scale of T_{L^{−(k+1)}}, i.e. LM in the scale of T_{L^{−k}} (RG2 p.13); throughout, a π_n-side is M in step-n units and an LMR_{n+1}-cube of CRE's step-(n+1) construction is LR_{n+1} π_n-sides. The MR_{k+1}-cubes of LFR are the cubes of Ω_{k+1}'s construction (CRE p.256); the layers added on pp.264–265 are layers of LMR_{k+1}-cubes, LR_{k+1} π_k-sides each. M ≥ 2LM₁ + 2R₁M₁ is SW §5's margin (C-M) (map D2.2(3)), used only where cited; its R₁, M₁ are VAR's, fixed before the coupling (VAR p.277), so M is a constant (not CRE's growing R₁; O5).
- *The layers.* Ω_n∖Ω_{n+1}, n ≤ k, are the older layers; Ω_{k+1} the current small-field region; Λ_{k+1} ⊆ Ω_{k+1} the fluctuation region of the step (CRE p.269). Geometry used throughout (derived from the transcriptions):
  - (G1) CRE (3.5), p.265: Ω_{k+1} = (Q′_{k+1}^{∼2} ∪ (B^{k+1}(P¹_{k+1})ᶜ)^{∼3})ᶜ, and (B^{k+1}(P¹_{k+1}))ᶜ ⊇ P′_{k+1}^∼ ∪ Z_k′^{∼5}, where Z_k′ is the cover of Z_k by LMR_{k+1}-cubes and Z_k ⊇ Λ_kᶜ (p.265: the step starts from Z_k′^{∼4}). So dist(Ω_{k+1}, Λ_kᶜ) ≥ 8 layers of LMR_{k+1}-cubes ((Z_k′^{∼5})^{∼3} = Z_k′^{∼8}); 6 are used: every point within 6 such layers of Λ_kᶜ lies in Ω_{k+1}ᶜ. At the next step, with k+1 for k: dist(Ω_{k+2}, Λ_{k+1}ᶜ) ≥ 6 layers of LMR_{k+2}-cubes.
  - (G2) CRE p.256: "The domains Ω_j, Λ_j, which are determined by the j-th renormalization transformation" have boundaries at distance at least 2MR_j (the RG sequence; l.21–24), and the domains of the class 𝐁_j(Ω) satisfy dist(Ω_n, Ω_{n−1}ᶜ) ≥ L^nξM₁ (l.61); VAR (1), p.277: (L^mη)^{−1}dist(Ω_mᶜ, Ω_{m+1}) > RM₁ (map D2.2(6)(e) l.945).
  - (G3) Ω_{k+2} ⊆ Λ_{k+1} (CRE pp.264–265; map l.935). So the slot variable σ_{k+1} = A_{k+1} on Ω_{k+2}∩Λ_{k+2}ᶜ lies in the layer Ω_{k+2}∖Ω_{k+3}, and σ_k lives on Ω_{k+1}∩Λ_{k+1}ᶜ ⊆ Ω_{k+1}∖Ω_{k+2}.
- *The collar.* Coll_{k+1} := the π_{k+1}-cubes within d₀ = 6 layers of Λ_{k+1}ᶜ ∪ S_{k+1} ∪ Ω_{k+1}ᶜ (SW closure §1(a3)). By (G1) at step k+2, Coll_{k+1} ∩ Ω_{k+2} = ∅ once a π_{k+1}-cube is not larger than an LMR_{k+2}-cube (a π_{k+1}-cube is one π_{k+1}-side, an LMR_{k+2}-cube is LR_{k+2} of them, so this holds since L, R ≥ 1): every collar cube of step k+1 lies in Ω_{k+2}ᶜ, hence in the layer Ω_{k+1}∖Ω_{k+2} or in Ω_{k+1}ᶜ, from step k+2 on. This is the geometric fact behind §5.
- *What is carried, and what is bounded.* On a labelled term the loop's non-scalar parts near large-field data cannot be bounded in absolute value and summed over the contour (map D2.0(5)(f): the available bound of the summed absolute values grows with the contour). They are carried as insertions of attached species, bounded per large-field component by D(k)(ii′), and the per-component totals are paid by the fundamental factors (§7). The non-scalar large-field parts of IT-1 note §6(d) (R13) are, for k ≤ N, exactly these attached species: at step 1 they are §3a(4)'s att(1, Z) and wrap(1) parts and §3a(2)'s att(1, coll) parts (map D2.0(3) l.278); at step k+1 they are the species of §2. Nothing else of the observable is attached to large fields: the scalar parts near large-field data are the thin terms E^∂ (R11, §7.4), numbers given the labels.
- *Per-component statements.* All bounds are per component Z of Z_{k+1} = Λ_{k+1}ᶜ (species att(j, Z)) or per assigned mark/input (species att(j, coll), split by component through B_j(Z)), never global. This is the form D2.0(5)(f)'s payment needs and the form IT-1 note §3b, §3i use at step 1.

## §2. The attached species made at step k+1 (R1–R3, R5, R12)

*2.0 Components and the collar.* CRE p.269: Λ_{k+1} = ((Ω_{k+1}ᶜ)^{∼2} ∪ R′_{k+1})ᶜ = Ω_{k+1}^{∼−2} ∩ (R′_{k+1}^∼)ᶜ, components of Λ_{k+1}ᶜ inside a cube of size 100LMR_{k+1} replaced by the smallest parallelepiped containing them. CRE p.264: "The term has a large field region Z_k = Λ_kᶜ". **Reading R-Z (logged, for review):** LFR's components Z of Z_{k+1}, which carry the fundamental factors, are the components of Λ_{k+1}ᶜ (p.264's identification applied at k+1; LFR II p.384 states the factors per component of the large field region). Each contains the 2-layer thickening of a component of Ω_{k+1}ᶜ or a component of R′_{k+1}. **Convention (replaces "component of Ω_{k+1}ᶜ" in D2.0(3)'s rules).** Every att(k+1, ·) and reclassification rule attaches to a component Z of Z_{k+1} = Λ_{k+1}ᶜ; D2.0(3)'s "first component of Ω_{k+1}ᶜ that the box meets" is read as the component of Λ_{k+1}ᶜ containing it. This is coarser, and every per-component bound below is stated first in the form C·#cubes_{k+1}(·): bounds C·#cubes(Z₀) for the components Z₀ ⊆ Z of Ω_{k+1}ᶜ add to C·#cubes(Z) (the Z₀ are disjoint subsets of Z). The conversion to the linear size is p.384 l.32, #cubes(Z) ≤ 3·2^{d−1}d′(Z) for d′(Z) ≠ 0; for d′(Z) = 0 the cubes of Z share a point (d′ is the length of a shortest tree meeting all the cubes, in units of the MR-cube side: RG1 p.257 with LFR II p.380 l.28), so #cubes(Z) ≤ 2^d. Together #cubes(Z) ≤ 3·2^{d−1}(d′(Z)+1). The payment of §7 is per component of Z_{k+1}, as LFR II p.384 needs. Coll_{k+1} (SW §1(a3)) = the π_{k+1}-cubes within 6 layers of Λ_{k+1}ᶜ, the π_{k+1}-cubes of Λ_{k+1}ᶜ included, so that "meets Coll_{k+1}" is the single species test of 2.2–2.4 (S_{k+1} ⊆ Λ_{k+1}ᶜ and Ω_{k+1}ᶜ ⊆ Λ_{k+1}ᶜ, so the three sets of (a3) reduce to one). Collar marks: 𝔪̂^{(k+1)}_Δ := 1 for every Δ ∈ Coll_{k+1}, 0 otherwise; each Δ is assigned to the component of Λ_{k+1}ᶜ nearest to it (ties by D2.0(3)'s order). So #{Δ → Z} ≤ 13^d·R_{k+1}^d·#cubes_{k+1}(Z) (a π_{k+1}-cube has side M in T_{L^{−(k+1)}}, an MR_{k+1}-cube R_{k+1}^d of them; 6 layers on each side give the factor 13^d).

*2.1 The rule (SW §1(a3), made exact here).* A step-(k+1) group 𝒢 (D2.2(6)(d), after the three merging rules) is dev iff (i) ω′ = 0 on it, (ii) every component of its polymers lies in Ω_k and every traversal of its arc I(𝒢) (the hull with allowance) lies in Ω_k, (iii) none of its polymers carries a boundary-type potential, (iv) its comparison region lies in Ω_{k+1} (SW §5(e)). (i) is read structurally: ω′ = 0 on 𝒢 means that no polymer domain and no member's domain of 𝒢 meets a bond of ω′. Otherwise:
- (a) if (iv) alone fails: species att(k+1, Z), Z the component of Λ_{k+1}ᶜ containing the component of Ω_{k+1}ᶜ met by the R_{t+1}-ball. Size: the group is of kind (α)/(β) with ω′ = 0, so D2.4(10)–(11) with (f)'s parity give O(ρ_{k+1}) per group, and (S-w) (D2.4(12)(f); its count does not use (iv), so these groups are inside it) gives Σ_{𝒢∋n}e^{θ + ν_rσ(𝒢) + ν_e·ext_{k+1}(𝒢)}‖Y_𝒢‖ ≤ C_S′ρ̂_{k+1}·pl, with (12)(c)'s interval extent ext_{k+1}(𝒢) (map l.1529: the least R with X(𝒢) ∪ Q^#_𝒢 in the ℓ∞-ball of radius R π_{k+1}-sides about every traversal of 𝒢's interval). Two extents are used below: ext_{k+1} (the step's, inside (S-w)) and the lineage extent ext^{coll}(𝒢) := max_{□∈Y_𝒢}dist(□, Coll_{k+1}) in π_{k+1}-sides (carried, §5.0). *The distance.* Let n′ be the traversal whose comparison region, the R_{t+1}-ball (R_t = C_boxM(t+1), t = t_{n′}(𝒢) the least t with X(𝒢) ⊂ B_∞(n′, R_t), map l.1316), meets Ω_{k+1}ᶜ. For t ≥ 2 minimality gives X(𝒢) ⊄ B_∞(n′, R_{t−1}), so R_{t−1} < M(ext_{k+1}+1) and R_{t+1} = R_{t−1} + 2C_boxM; for t = 1, R_2 = 3C_boxM. With dist(n, n′) ≤ M·ext_{k+1} (n and n′ in X(𝒢) ∪ I(𝒢)), d^Z_{k+1}(n) ≤ dist(n, n′) + R_{t+1} ≤ 2M·ext_{k+1}(𝒢) + (3C_box+2)M in both cases, and likewise every □ ∈ Y_𝒢 has dist(□, Coll_{k+1}) ≤ M·ext_{k+1} + R_{t+1}, so ext^{coll}(𝒢) ≤ 2ext_{k+1}(𝒢) + 3C_box + 2. *The split.* e^{ν_e·ext_{k+1}} = e^{(ν_e/4)ext_{k+1}}e^{(3/4)ν_e·ext_{k+1}}: the first factor pays the distance, e^{−(ν_e/4)ext_{k+1}} ≤ e^{(ν_e/8)(3C_box+2)}e^{−(ν_e/8)d^Z_{k+1}(n)/M}; the second is ≥ e^{−(3/8)ν_e(3C_box+2)}e^{(3/8)ν_e·ext^{coll}(𝒢)}, the carried weight of §5.0. So Σ_{𝒢∋n, (a)}e^{ν_rσ(𝒢)}e^{(3/8)ν_e·ext^{coll}(𝒢)}e^{θ}‖Y_𝒢‖ ≤ Cρ̂_{k+1}e^{−c_*d^Z_{k+1}(n)/M}, c_* := ν_e/8, C := C_S′·pl·e^{(ν_e/2)(3C_box+2)}: the (ii)-form, from the extent weight alone (no w_t). **Derived** given (S-w) (D2.4(12)(e)–(f), for review there). These groups fail only (iv), so they lie in (S-w)'s scope as SW §1(a1) states it (the groups satisfying (i)–(iii); (iv) is used only by the dev bound).
- (b) if (ii) fails: kind (γ), species att(k+1, coll), §4.
- (c) if (i) or (iii) fails: species att(k+1, coll), §2.2–§2.3, with §4.2's bounds where it is of kind (γ).
- (d) the parts of the reference extraction D2.2(6)(c) (the factors e^{ε_TδΨ_≥(T)} − 1, e^{−δΨ(M)} − 1) whose clusters meet Coll_{k+1} (2.0's single test; it covers the clusters meeting Λ_{k+1}ᶜ and those depending on ω′): att(k+1, coll), §2.4.
- (e) wrap(k+1): a group whose set X(𝒢) does not lift to the cover of the torus (map l.276–278: torus wrapping, decaying from no component) is wrap(k+1). Its set wraps some direction μ, so every traversal of the group has a cube of X(𝒢) at ℓ∞-distance ≥ N^{(k+1)}_min/2 − 1 π_{k+1}-sides, N^{(k+1)}_min the least torus period in π_{k+1}-sides: ext_{k+1}(𝒢) ≥ N^{(k+1)}_min/2 − 1, and (S-w) gives Σ_{𝒢∋n, wrap}e^{θ}‖Y_𝒢‖ ≤ C_S′e^{ν_e}ρ̂_{k+1}·pl·e^{−(ν_e/2)N^{(k+1)}_min}. As in the map, wrap(k+1) stays in (ii) (D2.0(3), (5)(c)(ε)), with the growth L per later step, summed under (5)(g)'s torus condition; it is not carried by §5. This is where R1's wrap(k+1) is produced. *Precedence and units.* After the inherited-species rule of *Merged groups*, the wrap test comes before (i)–(iv): a group whose set does not lift is wrap(k+1) whatever (i)–(iv), since neither the comparison region of (iv) nor the distance to a component is defined for it on the cover; if it also fails (i) or (iii), the bound is the same with C_S′ρ̂_{k+1} replaced by 2.2's C_S″b_k (the re-run; at real σ_k, over the ω-polydiscs at complex σ_k it rests on O6), and if it is of kind (γ), with amplitude C_S″b_k and §4.2's per-slot bounds whether or not it fails (i) or (iii), as 4.3 states; the factor e^{−(ν_e/2)N^{(k+1)}_min} is unchanged. N^{(k+1)}_min is in π_{k+1}-sides; in the map's step-(k+1) units (bonds) the period is M·N^{(k+1)}_min, so the factor reads e^{−(ν_e/(2M))N_min^{bonds}} in (5)(g)'s torus condition. Since a wrap(k+1) group failing (i) or (iii), or of kind (γ), has amplitude C_S″b_k, D(k)(ii)'s wrap display carries max(C_S′ρ̂_j, C_S″b_j) at each j; the sum over j is ≤ 2max(C_S′, C_S″)sup_j max(ρ̂_j, b_j) times the same torus factor, so (5)(g)'s condition holds with b_j in place of ρ̂_j, b_j → 0 with g_j (map (5)(g)).
- *Merged groups.* 2.1's tests are applied to the final merged group. A group containing an inherited attached member (att(j, ·), lw or wrap(j), j ≤ k) takes the first inherited species in D2.0(3)'s order (step ascending, then att(j, Z) < att(j, coll) < lw < wrap), whatever its (i)/(iii)/(γ) status. If that species is wrap(j), the group's set contains the member's, which does not lift, so (e)'s test gives wrap as well; its bound is (e)'s, with the growth L per later step with which D2.0(3) carries wrap(j). Otherwise, its bound is §5.2's (T2) (the descendant of its first attached member), with §2.2's per-slot factors pointwise in ω′ (R-src) where ω′ ≠ 0 and §4.2's (R-dec, R-dec⁺) where it is of kind (γ), and its sum is §5's lineage form (T0). Among the groups with no inherited attached member, the order is (e)'s: the wrap test first, then a group with a part failing (i) or (iii) is att(k+1, coll) (its untouched dev old members enter its bound as the factors of (11)(c)(β)'s product), then (ii), then (iv), with (a), (b) as stated (one order: inherited → wrap → (i)/(iii) → (ii) → (iv)). (The inherited rule must come first: under an (i)/(iii) override, touched descendants of att members, which cross Ω_{n+1}∩Λ_{n+1}ᶜ and so have ω′ ≠ 0, would enter D(n+1)(ii)'s per-traversal collar term while carrying ‖D‖, whose per-position size is not controlled there.) A product of a dev part with an att part is att. Multilinear expansion in the species is exact (D2.0(3) *Species*).
- *Rule changes (for review).* (1) SW (a3) attaches the failures of (i) and (iii) to the nearest component as att(k+1, Z); here they are att(k+1, coll), because their size is O(b_k) ≫ ρ̂_{k+1} and only the collar term of D(k+1)(ii) carries that size. (2) D2.0(3)'s "Q_W meets a component ⇒ att(k+1, Z)" is overridden by 2.1's order of tests for the same reason: a group with ω′ ≠ 0 or a boundary-type potential that also meets a component is att(k+1, coll). (3) The kind-(γ) groups without an inherited att/lw/wrap member (touched or not) are att(k+1, coll), not att(k+1, Z) (§4.3: they contain a collar cube, and the collar form needs no distance to a particular component). (4) (ii) is read exactly: "hull with allowance" is the arc I(𝒢) of D2.2(6)(d) *Arcs* (its length allowance included), and (ii) fails iff a component of 𝒢's polymers meets Ω_kᶜ (D2.1(4) case (B)) or a traversal of I(𝒢) is not contained in Ω_k (§4.3; the component clause is needed: a group with a component meeting Ω_kᶜ and its arc inside Ω_k would otherwise not fail (ii); it also fails (i) by 2.3's argument, since its component meets supp B′ ⊆ Ω_{k+1} and Ω_kᶜ ⊆ Ω_{k+1}ᶜ and so contains a π_k-cube of Ω_{k+1}∖Ω_{k+1}^{∼−1} ⊆ S_{k+1}, which carries σ_k; the clause makes the reading of (ii) exact). Such groups contain a cube of Ω_kᶜ ⊆ Λ_{k+1}ᶜ ⊆ Coll_{k+1}, so §4.3's collar form applies with no new geometry. SW §1(a1)(ii) is amended to match. All four are changes of the map's rules, not of its bounds.

*2.2 The ω′-dependent groups (R2).* By D2.2(5)(b), (6)(b6) the dependence on ω′ = (ω, σ_k) enters the marked activity Ĥ(M) through the source term −⟨A, C*Δ^{(k)}Cσ_k⟩ of (3.27), 𝐏^{(k)}(g_k,(A_k,A)), the 𝐄_k- and 𝐑_k-terms at U_k(…(A_k,A)…), the 𝐁_k-terms and A(g_k^{−2}(·) − g_k^{−2}, ·) of (3.25)'s curly bracket, and V^{(k)}(S_{k+1},(A_k,A)) (CRE (3.25), (3.27), (3.29), pp.270–272, the channel list of the map's D2.2(6)(e) l.956–958; A_k "the fluctuation field on Ω_{k+1}∩Λ_{k+1}ᶜ", p.270), all localised in domains meeting the σ_k-bonds (Ω_{k+1}∩Λ_{k+1}ᶜ)^{(k)*} ⊆ Λ_{k+1}ᶜ after Bałaban's localisation of C*Δ^{(k)}C (the random-walk expansion, (2) below; D2.2(6)(b): σ_k = the old A_k on Ω_{k+1}∩Λ_{k+1}ᶜ), and through the old insertions' ω-dependence (D2.2(4)(c), on their own domains). So a group with ω′ ≠ 0 has a polymer component or an old member's domain meeting the one-cube neighbourhood of Λ_{k+1}ᶜ, hence a cube Δ ∈ Coll_{k+1} in its domain.
- *Size (derived; SW §1(a4); the per-slot factors are derived, the group bound at real σ_k; over the ω-polydiscs at complex σ_k it needs (10) there, which is O6).* The per-slot bounds D2.4(11)(c)(α)–(β) are pointwise in ω′ on the polydiscs of D2.2(6)(e) (the Schwarz lemma in t_Y and the sup bound use no parity), so a group is bounded by (11)(c)'s per-slot factors, b_k/ε_H per pure-loop component ((α), radius T_Y = ε_H/|B′|_Y) and C_Tb_k/ᾱ_k per component of the (β)-slots, times e^{−(κ_s−3)#(G)}∏_W[e^{θ_k(W)}‖Y_W‖]; the gain of (11)(f) is not available, so a single-component pure-loop group is O(b_k) = O(C₁^{RG2}δ_k) and not O(ρ_{k+1}). No Cauchy gain in the field is claimed here. *The measure at ω′ ≠ 0.* (10) is stated in the map at ω′ = 0. CRE pp.271–272 take expectations under the measure (3.29), Z_t^{−1}dμ_{C^{(k)}(Λ_{k+1})}(A)χ^{(k)}exp[−t⟨A, C*Δ^{(k)}CA_k⟩ + 𝐏^{(k)}(g_k,(tA_k,A)) + {𝐄_k(U_k(…(tA_k,A)…)) − 𝐄_k(U_{k+1})}], for t ∈ [0,1] and A_k on the support, and file the result: "the expectation value of the sum of terms with at least one localization in the domain Ω_{k+1}∖Λ_{k+1}, hence this expression contributes to 𝐁^{(k+1)} only" (p.272). **R-src at real data (Bałaban's claim).** At real A_k the expectations under (3.27) and (3.29) are bounded as at A_k = 0, at real and, by p.261 (ii), (iv), at complex backgrounds (𝐔,𝐉) ∈ Ũ^c ((3.27) carries ω′ through the same source term, and p.271 files its boundary-term expectation "to 𝐁^{(k+1)} only"; p.276: "The action in (3.27) contains more expressions"). The printed places: (3.25) (p.270) carries the source −⟨A, C*Δ^{(k)}CA_k⟩ together with −½⟨A_k, C*Δ^{(k)}CC^{(k)}(Λ_{k+1})C*Δ^{(k)}CA_k⟩, the completed square of the Gaussian in A; p.276: the action of (3.29) "differs only by the additional dependence on the field tA_k, but this was already taken into acount in the above considerations"; p.272 files the expectations into 𝐁^{(k+1)}; and 𝐁^{(k+1)}'s terms satisfy (2.42) by Theorem 1 (p.262), with (2.41)–(2.42) (p.261) stating the bound for every value of A (read as the system of large-field fluctuation fields: this gloss is part of R-src, not printed on p.261), which is not restricted there (the analytic extension is in (𝐔,𝐉) only). (10)'s derivation then applies at real ω′ ≠ 0 as at ω′ = 0. *Complex σ_k.* §5.0 uses the att bounds jointly on 𝒟^{sub}_{k+1} and over D(k+1)(iii)'s ω-polydiscs, σ_k = σ_{k,0} + ζ with σ_{k,0} real in the support and |ζ| < r_{ω,k} per bond. Bałaban never complexifies the outer field A_k (p.261; RG2 (1.34) and CRE (3.43) complexify the integration field), so the ingredients below are derived here, except (1′); the joint bound they feed is not, while O6 is open (see the label). (1) The potentials 𝐄_k, 𝐑_k, 𝐁_k and A(·) depend on σ_k only through the configuration U_k(exp i[g_kC(σ_k, A) − hD̃(g_kC(σ_k, A))]V^{(k)}), with σ_k and the integration field A entering C jointly, and their raw bounds are sup bounds on complex spaces (Bałaban's expansion of (3.27)/(3.29) does not use these raw bounds but the localised re-expansions, which fall under O6, see (1′)): (2.42) for "the extended function" 𝐁^{(j)} on Ũ^c_j(X, α̃₀, α̃₁) (CRE p.261 (ii), (iv)); for 𝐄_k and 𝐑_k their own inductive assumptions (2.26)–(2.40) on the same spaces (CRE pp.258–261; (2.42) covers 𝐁^{(j)} only). A(·) = A(g_k^{−2}(·) − g_k^{−2}, ·) is the Wilson action (CRE p.243) with the plaquette term at p multiplied by g_k^{−2}(x(p)) − g_k^{−2} (p.253's notation A(φ, U)); its plaquette terms 1 − Re tr U(∂p) (RG1 p.260) are 1 − ½tr(U(∂p) + U(∂p)^{−1}) on G, analytic on the whole complexified configuration space, so no domain question arises. Its size is p.277's: its terms, localised in Z_k, "contribute to the boundary terms only, and they are treated as above", i.e. with 𝐁_k's terms on the spaces (3.43) and the bound (2.42) times O(ε_k), with the large values of g_k^{−2}(·) − g_k^{−2} "suppressed by the exponential decay of 𝐇_k"; this is the accepted p.277 estimate of §2.3, a bound on the spaces (3.43), so at complex σ_k it is under O6 like the other localised terms ((1′)), and the suppression by 𝐇_k's decay at complex B′ on the older layers is the map's R-dec (Bałaban's, accepted). D2.2(6)(e) (map l.959) places the configuration in these spaces for |ζ| < r_{ω,k} (|B′| ≤ 2C₁^{RG2}O(1)ε_k); that membership rests on (4)(f)'s chain condition with R-m, R-dec and R-adm (map (6)(e); SW (b3)), not on D2.1(4)'s smallness conditions alone. (1′) **The localised terms, 𝐏^{(k)} and V^{(k)}: NOT DERIVED, OPEN as O6.** *Scope.* The expansion of (3.27)/(3.29) uses localised terms, not the raw functions of (1): RG2 Lemma 2 (p.11) expands "𝐏^{(k)}(g_k,U_{k+1},B) + {⋯} = Σ_{Y∈𝐃_k}𝐕_k(Y,U_{k+1},B)", 𝐏^{(k)} together with the 𝐄_k-bracket, on the space (1.34); CRE p.276 (l.36–49): "The expression determined by 𝐑_k is treated in exactly the same way as 𝐄_k", and for 𝐁_k "To its terms we apply the formulas (3.6), (3.7) [I], and next we localize the obtained expressions … Terms of this expansion can be extended … to analytic functions defined on the corresponding spaces (3.43)"; p.277: A(·)'s terms "are treated as above". All these localised bounds are stated on (3.43) or (1.34), so the obstruction below applies to each of them, not to 𝐏^{(k)} alone. The raw sup bounds of (1) are inputs of the closure route, not a bound on the expansion. 𝐏^{(k)}(g_k,(A_k,A)) is a function of the fluctuation field (CRE (3.25), p.270; RG2 (1.41), p.11, "𝐏^{(k)}(g_k,U_{k+1},B)"). The obstruction is the form of the printed bounds of the localised terms: the raw potentials of (1) have sup bounds on configuration spaces Ũ^c, so membership of the shifted configuration suffices for the raw functions, while 𝐏^{(k)}'s bounds are stated on (background space) × (B-disc) (RG2 (1.34), p.9; CRE (3.43), p.276), e.g. (1/g_k²)V(H₁B′), defined in RG1 (2.8) as "terms of at least third order in H₁B′", the remainder after A(U_{k+1}), ⟨H₁B′,J⟩ and ½⟨H₁B′,Δ₁H₁B′⟩. Its terms that depend on A_k = σ_k are among the terms of (I.6.41) with localisation domain Y ⊄ Λ_{k+1} (Lemma 2, p.11, is stated on the space (1.34), whose terms depend on 𝐔, 𝐉, B "restricted to the interior of Y", RG2 p.9), and for these CRE p.276 replaces the space (I.6.34) by (3.43), Ũ^c_{k+1}(Y,(1+β)α̃₀,(1+β)α̃₁) × {A : supp A ⊂ Y∩Λ_{k+1}^{(k)*}, |A| < C₁p₁(g_k)}, which has no A_k variable. CRE pp.270–278 state the A_k-dependence nowhere else: p.276's "differs only by the additional dependence on the field tA_k, but this was already taken into acount" refers back to the same analysis, and p.275 starts that analysis from "the effective action given by the expression in the last exponential in (3.28)", the A_k = 0 action, so (3.43) is the space of the A_k-free remaining terms and A_k-analyticity is stated nowhere. RG2 p.10 gives analyticity of the localised pieces (1.38) in B′ on {B′ : e^{16κ₁}|B′| ≤ a₁ on Y} with (𝐔,𝐉) ∈ 𝐔′^c(T_η,α₀′,α₁′), and Lemma 2 (p.11) in B on all bonds of Y on the space (1.34), 𝐔^c_{k+1}(Y,(1+β)α₀,(1+β)α₁,α₀) × {B : |B| < ε₁g_k^{−1}}, both in [I]'s whole-lattice setting; CRE p.276 transfers Lemma 2 to the {Ω_j}-operators with the replaced space only. V^{(k)}(S_{k+1},(A_k,A)) is absorbed under R-x into the same question. So the joint bound of these terms at complex σ_k is neither printed nor derived. What closes it: a derivation that the localised terms with Y ⊄ Λ_{k+1}, i.e. those of (I.6.41) (𝐏^{(k)} with the 𝐄_k-bracket), 𝐑_k's, and the (I.3.6)–(3.7)-localised 𝐁_k- and A(·)-terms of p.276–277, extend analytically in A_k on the slot polydiscs, with the bound used here, by re-running RG2 §1 (pp.9–11: Lemma 1's s-localisation for the brackets, Lemma 2's for 𝐏^{(k)}) together with p.276's 𝐁_k-localisation, with CRE's {Ω_j}-operators for B = (A_k, A) and the raw bounds of (1) as inputs (p.276: "The same remarks apply to the expression 𝐏^{(k)}"), after identifying why (3.43) restricts A to C₁p₁(g_k), since the same reason may restrict A_k. The radius the consumers need is small (the complex σ values are the slot-value moves of map l.976–979, |Δσ_j| ≤ 4C_H(α_{0,j+1} + α_{1,j+1})e^{−R_j}/g_j, and the complex rotations R(w)σ of map (4)(c) and *Propagation* below, all inside the polydisc of the amendment below), but even a small radius needs the analytic extension. (2) The source term is not a function of the configuration. With K := C*Δ^{(k)}C and A real on the support (|A(b)| < r_k, map (11)(b)), compare at the same complex background. R-src at real data, read with p.261 (ii), (iv) and Theorem 1, gives Bałaban's bound at complex (𝐔,𝐉) ∈ Ũ^c and real A_k = σ_{k,0} (the extension is in (𝐔,𝐉)). The integrand at (σ_{k,0} + ζ, 𝐔) is Bałaban's with the potentials evaluated at the shifted configuration, its localised terms bounded on the support only under O6 ((1′)), times the factor e^{−⟨A, K(𝐔)ζ⟩} (with t in front for (3.29); (3.27)'s source term carries no t). *Localisation of the source term (per localisation domain, so that each factor's domain meets the σ_k-bonds, as the species rule (i) of 2.1 and §2.4's support statement need).* K = C*Δ_kC is expanded into Bałaban's generalized random walks (accepted from Bałaban, not derived here; (1.7) is printed for propagators): RG2 p.13, "This construction was discussed in [13] for all operators determining Δ_k", [13] = PBG, where Δ_k is (3.156) (p.428), p.427 constructs the expansions of G₁, H₁ and Δ^{(2)}_π with tree-like ω, "The estimates are the same as in (3.108)" (p.416: O(1)(L^jη)²O(M^{−1/2})^{|ω|}M^{−|ω|/2}e^{−(1/2)δ₀d(ω,y,y′)}), and p.428 states "a uniform exponential decay of C*Δ_kC"; the form is (1.6) of RG2 p.3, Σ_ωR_0(X_0)R_{α_1}(X_1)⋯R_{α_n}(X_n), each factor depending on the background restricted to X̃_j^5, a term bounded by (1.7), O(1)O(M₁^{−1/2})^{|ω|}M₁^{−|ω|/2}exp(−δ₀d(ω,y,y′)), "the remaining two factors" being used by Bałaban "to produce exponential bounds for localized terms" (p.3). Grouping the walks by their localisation domain X(ω) := ∪_jX̃_j^5 enlarged by C's range (C is almost local: "(C B̃)(b) depends on B̃ restricted to several blocks surrounding the bond b", PBG p.428) gives K(𝐔) = Σ_XK_X(𝐔), K_X := Σ_{ω:X(ω)=X}K_ω localised in X. Since ζ is supported on the σ_k-bonds, (K_Xζ)(b) ≠ 0 only if X ∋ b meets the σ_k-bonds. So ⟨A, Kζ⟩ = Σ_Xx_X with x_X := ⟨A, K_X(𝐔)ζ⟩ (times t for (3.29)), a function of A on X and of 𝐔 on X, and |x_X| ≤ |X|r_kr_{ω,k}Σ_{ω:X(ω)=X}‖K_ω‖ on the support, ‖K_ω‖ bounded by (1.7). The weight is paid as in RG2 p.5 (1.11): with d(ω) "a length of a shortest tree graph intersecting all localization domains of ω" and m the number of M-cubes the walk meets, "If m is big enough, for example m > 2⁴, then δ₀d(ω) ≥ δ₁mM, for a positive constant δ₁ depending on δ₀ only", so e^{2a·m}e^{−δ₀d(ω)} ≤ e^{32a} once δ₁M ≥ 2a, Bałaban's δ₁M ≥ κ₁ with 2a for κ₁, a condition on M in his order (M after δ₀ and the weights; §8). Bałaban's expansion of (3.29) uses the localised terms through their bounds on the support (R-fr's presupposition, §2.3), so it is re-run with the e^{−x_X} − 1 as extra local terms; their bound at the shifted configuration is derived, the other localised terms' is O6. Writing e^{−x_X} = 1 + (e^{−x_X} − 1), |e^{−x_X} − 1| ≤ 2|x_X| for |x_X| ≤ log 2, the factors e^{−x_X} − 1 enter Bałaban's expansion of (3.29) as additional local terms of the Mayer expansion of the integrand (as RG2 (2.1) expands exp Σ_Y𝐕_k), each with domain X meeting the σ_k-bonds and bounded on the support (the normaliser Z_t included: Z_t(σ_{k,0}+ζ)/Z_t(σ_{k,0}) is the expectation, under the measure with the potentials at the shifted configuration, of ∏_Xe^{−x_X}, times the ratio of the normalisers with the shifted and unshifted potentials, both cluster-expanded with these terms, so it does not vanish; the shifted-potential expansion is under O6). The Kotecký–Preiss condition of the combined gas, per cube: sup_Δ Σ_{X∋Δ}2|x_X|e^{a·m(X)} ≤ c_KP, with a the KP weight per M-cube and m(X) the number of M-cubes of X. Since |X| ≤ dM^dm(X) ≤ dM^d(ea)^{−1}e^{a·m(X)} (m ≤ e^{am}/(ea)), the sum is ≤ 2dM^d(ea)^{−1}r_kr_{ω,k}Σ_{ω:X(ω)∋Δ}e^{2a·m}‖K_ω‖ ≤ C_KB₀e^{32a}r_kr_{ω,k} by (1.7) and the RG2 p.5 step above (the first two factors of (1.7) control the sum over ω, "they determine the constant B₀", p.3), where C_K = C_K(d, M, M₁, a) also absorbs the rooting of the walk sum at the M-cube Δ (of order (M/M₁)^d, the factor |ω|+1 being absorbed by M₁^{−|ω|/2}), so it holds once C_KB₀e^{32a}r_kr_{ω,k} ≤ c_KP and δ₁M ≥ 2a. This is derived for the source term; for the other localised terms it rests on (1′), O6. The condition fails at the radius r_{ω,k} = c_ωε_k/g_k: r_kr_{ω,k} = c_ωA₁A₀(log g_k^{−2})^{2p₀} ≫ 1, the phases e^{−itx_X} oscillate at order one, and χ^{(k)} prevents moving Im ζ into a complex mean shift. **Amendment (for review; map D2.2(6)(e) *The radii*):** r_{ω,j} := g_j^{1/2} for j ≥ 1 (r_{ω,0} = 1 at step 1, V3's measure). Then C_KB₀e^{32a}r_kr_{ω,k} ≤ C_KB₀e^{32a}A₁g_k^{1/2}(log g_k^{−2})^{p₀} → 0. The radius is used only as a domain: no Cauchy estimate in ω is taken anywhere (the Schwarz steps act in t_Y and in 𝐔; §4.2(iii), §4.5). Its other users still hold. (a) Membership: g_k|σ_k| ≤ O(1)ε_k + g_k^{3/2}. (b) *Propagation*: |σ_{j,0}|/r_{ω,j} ≤ O(1)r_jg_j^{−1/2}, and the condition 16c_GC_w(2θ)^{k−j−1}h_k(|σ_{j,0}|/r_{ω,j} + 1) ≤ 1 needs h_kr_jg_j^{−1/2} small with h_k ≤ α_{1,k}. CRE (2.6) gives g_j ≥ g_k(1 + β′(k−j))^{−1/2} for g_k ≤ 1 and log g_j^{−2} ≤ log g_k^{−2} + log(1 + β′(k−j)), so h_kr_jg_j^{−1/2} ≤ Cg_k^{1/2}(log g_k^{−2})^{q₁+p₀}(1 + β′(k−j))^{1/4}(1 + log(1+β′(k−j)))^{p₀}, which (2θ)^{k−j−1} makes small uniformly in k, K for g_K small. (c) The slot-value move (map l.976–979): ≤ 4C_H(α_{0,j+1} + α_{1,j+1})e^{−R_j}/g_j ≤ r_{ω,j}/4 once 16C_H(α_{0,j+1} + α_{1,j+1})g_j^{−3/2}e^{−R_j} ≤ 1, true for g_K small since α_{i,j+1} = O(g_j(log g_j^{−2})^{q_i}) and R_j ≥ (log g_j^{−2})^r, r ≥ 2. **Derived** as ingredients: the source-term step (2), localised through the random-walk expansion of K (RG2 pp.3, 5, 13), with its Kotecký–Preiss condition, given the walk expansion of the operators determining Δ_k as Bałaban's (PBG pp.416, 427–428); the raw sup bounds of 𝐄_k, 𝐑_k and 𝐁^{(j)} at the shifted configuration, by membership; and A(·)'s entire analyticity; given R-src at real data (with Bałaban's bound at complex (𝐔,𝐉)), the inductive assumptions for 𝐄_k and 𝐑_k, D2.2(6)(e)'s membership and the amendment. Not derived: the bounds of the localised terms (𝐏^{(k)} with the 𝐄_k-bracket, 𝐑_k's, the localised 𝐁_k- and A(·)-terms, V^{(k)}) at complex σ_k, which are O6 ((1′)). **The conclusion, the joint bound at complex σ_k over the ω-polydiscs that §5.0 consumes, is NOT DERIVED: it rests on O6 (OPEN)**, since every ω′-dependent expectation is under (3.27) or (3.29), whose actions contain 𝐏^{(k)}(g_k,(σ_k,A)) or 𝐏^{(k)}(g_k,(tσ_k,A)) and V^{(k)} ((1′)).
- *The rooted sum at amplitude b_k (derived at real σ_k; over the ω-polydiscs at complex σ_k it rests on O6).* (12)(e)–(f) use, of the groups, (11)(c)'s per-slot bounds, the decay factors of (10)–(11)(d) and (f) (e^{−(κ_s−3)#}, the vacuum-link decay; (f)'s parity gain is the part not available here), and the Kotecký–Preiss conditions ε^{new} small and the reference gas's, the latter with the coll groups' weights included. Re-run with the per-slot factors of *Size* (ε^{new,α} ≤ C_Mb_k·pl in place of C_Mρ_{k+1}·pl), they give (S-w) for these groups with ρ̂_{k+1} replaced by b_k and C_S′ by C_S″, (12)(f)'s constant at that amplitude; the Kotecký–Preiss condition is C_Mb_k·pl below (12)(e)'s threshold, a smallness of g_K (b_k·pl → 0 with g_k). The same re-run covers 2.3's and 4.3's groups. The O6 qualifier of the heading concerns the ω′-dependent groups; for 4.3's ω′-free groups (those failing only (ii)) it does not apply, since their activities and their rooted sum do not depend on σ_k (4.3's label).
- *The bound in (ii)'s form.* Every group 𝒢 ∋ n of this species contains a collar cube Δ₀ ∈ X(𝒢), so dist(n, Coll_{k+1}) ≤ M(ext_{k+1}(𝒢)+1), and ext^{coll}(𝒢) ≤ 2ext_{k+1}(𝒢) + 1 (every □ ∈ Y_𝒢 lies within ext_{k+1} of n, and n within ext_{k+1}+1 of Δ₀). Split e^{ν_e·ext_{k+1}} as in 2.1(a): e^{−(ν_e/4)ext_{k+1}} ≤ e^{ν_e/4}max_{Δ∈Coll_{k+1}}e^{−(ν_e/4)dist(Δ,n)/M}, and e^{(3/4)ν_e·ext_{k+1}} ≥ e^{−(3/8)ν_e}e^{(3/8)ν_e·ext^{coll}}. With the re-run, Σ_{𝒢∋n, att(k+1,coll)}e^{ν_rσ(𝒢)}e^{(3/8)ν_e·ext^{coll}(𝒢)}e^{θ}‖Y_𝒢‖ ≤ C_S″b_k·pl·e^{(5/8)ν_e}Σ_{Δ∈Coll_{k+1}}e^{−(ν_e/4)dist(Δ,n)/M} ≤ C_S″·pl·e^{(5/8)ν_e}C₁^{RG2}(1+β₀)²u_*(2C′_χ)^{−1}ρ_{t,k+1}Σ_{Δ∈Coll_{k+1}}e^{−(ν_e/4)dist(Δ,n)/M} ≤ Cρ_{t,k+1}Σ_Δ𝔪̂^{(k+1)}_Δe^{−c_*′dist(Δ,n)/M}, using b_k = C₁^{RG2}A₁g_k(log g_k^{−2})^{p₀} ≤ C₁^{RG2}(1+β₀)²u_*ρ_{t,k+1}/(2C′_χ) (CRE (2.6)–(2.7) at n − m = 1; ρ_{t,k+1} = C′_χg_{k+1}(1+2r_{k+1})/u_*) and c_*′ := min(η_V/C_F, ν_e/8, κ/4) (ν_e/8, not ν_e/4: the att(j, Z) production of 2.1(a), §3 and §4.3 is at c_* = ν_e/8, and a bound at a smaller rate does not imply one at a larger rate; the display above, proved at ν_e/4, holds a fortiori). The rate carried in the totals is map (5)(f)'s c := min(c^{(1)}, c_*′) ≤ c_*′; the display at c_*′ implies the one at c, and N_{k+1}, C_N below are stated at c. **Amendment (for review):** D(k)(ii)'s collar term is stated with the rate c_*′ in place of η_V/C_F, and so are N_j(x) in (ii′-b) and the totals A_j(Z) of (5)(f); V3's step-1 bound at the rate η_V/C_F implies the weaker one, so D(1)(ii) holds as before. This is D(k+1)(ii)'s collar term with the marks of 2.0 and C = C_S″·pl·e^{(5/8)ν_e}C₁^{RG2}(1+β₀)²u_*/(2C′_χ). **Derived** given the re-run at amplitude b_k above (for review; it gives (S-w) to the coll groups, as SW §1's last paragraph states), D2.4(10)–(11) with R-src and the ω-radius amendment, at real σ_k; over the ω-polydiscs at complex σ_k it rests on O6.
- *Per component.* (ii′-b) at j = k+1: B_{k+1}(Z) = Cρ_{t,k+1}Σ_{Δ→Z}N_{k+1}(Δ), N_{k+1}(Δ) = Σ_{n}e^{−c·dist(Δ,n)/M} ≤ 2c_CMC_N (the contour has at most c_C(M+2r) step-(k+1) traversals within distance r of Δ, map (5)(f) *Per-step cost*; summing over shells of width M, C_N := Σ_{s≥0}(2s+1)e^{−cs} depends on c only; the factor 2 covers the shell count c_CMΣ_s(2s+3)e^{−cs} for c ≤ c_*′ ≤ ½). With 2.0's count, B_{k+1}(Z) ≤ 2Cc_CMC_N13^dρ_{t,k+1}R_{k+1}^d·#cubes_{k+1}(Z) ≤ C_Bρ_{t,k+1}R_{k+1}^d(d′_{k+1}(Z)+1), C_B := 2Cc_CMC_N13^d·3·2^{d−1} (2.0's conversion). This is the per-component form §7 pays.

*2.3 The boundary-type potentials (R3, R15).* CRE pp.276–277: the action of (3.27) contains, besides 𝐄_k and 𝐑_k, the terms of 𝐁_k (p.276, last paragraph: "The localized expansion is over domains Y₀ ∈ 𝐃_k having nonempty intersections with Λ_{k+1}. Terms of this expansion can be extended [as in (I.3.13)] to analytic functions defined on the corresponding spaces (3.43)"; the qualifier "Y ⊄ Λ_{k+1}" of l.26–35 belongs to the terms of (I.6.41), not to 𝐁_k), A(g_k^{−2}(·) − g_k^{−2}, ·) ("localized in Z_k, because supp(g_k^{−2}(·) − g_k^{−2}) ⊂ Z_k"), and V^{(k)}(S_{k+1}) ("at least one localization in S_{k+1}"). p.277 prints "estimated as in (2.42), but with the additional factor O(ε_k) arising from the bound of the fluctuation field" for 𝐁_k; that the other two are estimated in the same way is this note's extension of p.277's "treated as above" and "treated again as the boundary terms".
- *Where their groups live.* Z_k = Λ_kᶜ is printed (p.264); Λ_kᶜ ⊆ Λ_{k+1}ᶜ follows from Λ_{k+1} ⊆ Ω_{k+1} ⊆ Λ_k, and S_{k+1} ⊆ Λ_{k+1}ᶜ from (3.20) (S_{k+1} = (Ω_{k+1}∖Ω_{k+1}^{∼−1}) ∪ R_{k+1}, both outside Λ_{k+1} = Ω_{k+1}^{∼−2} ∩ (R′_{k+1}^∼)ᶜ) (derived). For 𝐁_k: CRE (2.47)–(2.48) (p.264) bound 𝐁^{(j,n)} by B₁2^{−(j−n)}|Γ_n| for j > n, "and this inequality with |Γ_n∖Λ_k⁰| for j = n", i.e. the terms are localised on the layers Γ_n, n ≤ k, with Γ_n∖Λ_k⁰ at j = n. **Reading R-𝐁 (labelled, for review):** every term of 𝐁_k's localised expansion has a localisation domain meeting Λ_{k+1}ᶜ, so that a group carrying it has a constituent meeting Λ_{k+1}ᶜ ((2.41) with (G1) gives Λ_{k+1}ᶜ, not Λ_kᶜ, since Z_j^∼ need not lie in Λ_kᶜ). Basis: p.276 l.26–35 applied inductively over the steps j ≤ k: at step j the terms with Y ⊄ Λ_{j+1} ("the remaining terms", with Y ∩ Λ_{j+1} ≠ ∅ and the analyticity space (3.43)) are the ones that "will contribute to the boundary terms 𝐁^{(j+1)}", and Λ_{j+1}ᶜ ⊆ Λ_kᶜ for j < k. What the reading adds beyond the print: (1) p.276 l.26–35 says this of the localised expansions (I.6.41) (of 𝐏^{(k)} and the 𝐄-terms); the reading applies it to 𝐁_k's own re-expansion, i.e. that the re-expansion of the older boundary terms at the later steps keeps a localisation domain containing the old one (p.276: "we resum them in localization domains Y₀ ∈ 𝐃_k"). That the listed sources exhaust 𝐁^{(j+1)}, and that the j = n = k terms on Γ_k∖Λ_k⁰ are covered, is derived in the form the species test uses. Every term of 𝐁^{(j)}, whatever its source, is a term of (2.41) (p.261: "the sum is over domains X ∈ 𝐃_j such, that X∩Ω_j ≠ ∅, and X∩Z_j^∼ ≠ ∅"; Theorem 1, p.262, gives this form at every step). A ∼-layer at scale j is at most 2 layers of LMR_{j+1}-cubes (R_j ≤ (L+1)R_{j+1}, (2.9)), and by (G1) the 6 such layers around Λ_jᶜ = Z_j lie in Ω_{j+1}ᶜ, so Z_j^∼ ⊆ Ω_{j+1}ᶜ ⊆ Λ_{k+1}ᶜ (Λ_{k+1} ⊆ Ω_{k+1} ⊆ Ω_{j+1} for j ≤ k). So every such X meets Λ_{k+1}ᶜ, the j = k terms on Γ_k∖Λ_k⁰ included; the test needs Λ_{k+1}ᶜ, not Λ_kᶜ. The cube size of ∼ in Z_j^∼ is not fixed on the pages read; the step holds for any cube of side at most MR_j (MR_j-, M- or M₁-cubes). Only (1), that the re-expansion at the later steps keeps a localisation domain containing the old one, remains a reading. No counterexample is known.
- *Size and species.* The boundary-type terms are potentials of the measure (3.27), not slots: each is bounded by its sup over the support of the integration (R-fr below), at every configuration of the polydisc, p.277's "estimated as in (2.42), but with the additional factor O(ε_k) arising from the bound of the fluctuation field", with no Cauchy gain in the field (the t_Y-deformation of (11)(c) acts on B′ inside the slots, not on the measure's field). *The field range.* No condition relating the support radius r_k = A₁(log g_k^{−2})^{p₀} of (11)(b) to (3.43)'s field radius C₁p₁(g_k) is used. CRE's pages read do not define p₁; where Bałaban defines it, p₁(g) = (log g^{−2})^{p₁} with p₁ < p₀ (LFR I p.183: "δ_j′ = g_jA₁p₁(g_j), p₁(g_j) = (log g_j^{−2})^{p₁}, and p₁ < p₀"; ER2 p.59: "δ₀ = g₀p₁(g₀), with p₁(g₀) smaller than p(g₀)"), and with that p₁ a condition r_k < c₄₃p₁(g_k) fails for small g. The size used is p.277's statement as printed, "The terms of the expansion can be estimated as in (2.42), but with the additional factor O(ε_k)", for the terms as they occur in the integral over the support of χ^{(k)}(Λ_{k+1}) (p.269 l.17–19). **R-fr (Bałaban's claim).** p.277's estimate, and the bounds of p.276's remaining 𝐄/𝐑-terms on (3.43), hold on the support of the integration. (a) The support is |A| < r_k = δ_k/g_k in the Gaussian variable. This is the phase reading of (3.16) (map (11)(b)), and it is Bałaban's own convention. [I]'s cutoff is |B′(b)| < ε₁ in the field variable (RG1 (2.9), p.266), written |B(b)| ≥ ε₁/g_k in the Gaussian one (RG2 (2.3), p.12). LFR II p.380 takes the large-field factor of χ^{(j−1)c} as exp(−γ₀g_j^{−2}δ_j²(…)), which needs |A| ≥ δ_j/g_j. (b) In [I] the analyticity radius of the fluctuation variable is the support radius: (I.6.34) = RG2 (1.34) (p.9) is "{B : |B| < ε₁g_k^{−1} on Y}", the same ε₁/g_k as RG2 (2.3)'s cutoff. Bałaban's integrals of these terms over the support are bounded through that domain (RG2 (2.1)–(2.3), p.12: the Mayer expansion of exp Σ_Y𝐕_k(Y,B) under dμ_{C^{(k)}}χ_k). (c) CRE expands the terms of (3.43) over the same support (equality of the radii is shown for [I] only): pp.277–278, on the last expectation value in (3.37), "the integral in the nominator … We take the expansion of the integrated function … In each integral we apply the Mayer expansion (I.7.1) … H′(Z₀) … is given by an integral with the integrand including, as one of the factors, a localized term from the expansion of the integrated function … it satisfies the same bound as the other factors". That integral is over the support of χ^{(k)}(Λ_{k+1}), so Bałaban's bound uses the terms' bounds at every point of the support. The same holds for the measures (3.27) and (3.29) (pp.271–272): their actions are treated in p.276's last paragraph ("Let us discuss briefly the above operations for the actions defining the measures (3.27), (3.29)"), and their cluster expansion is p.277's "exponentiated cluster expansion of Sect. 7 [I], or rather its appropriate generalization". So R-fr is presupposed by Bałaban's cluster expansion of (3.27) and (3.29) over the support (pp.271–272, 277–278); it is not printed as a step, and it is accepted as presented (Bałaban's presented-as-true construction, no counterexample), not an extension of this note. The symbol p₁ in (3.43) is not defined in CRE; it occurs only there (TRANSCRIPTION_FINDINGS, CRE p.276). LFR I's later p₁ (p.183, the cutoff δ_j′ = g_jA₁p₁(g_j) of a different construction) is not CRE's. (d) The O(ε_k) factor matches the support: "arising from the bound of the fluctuation field" is g_k|A| < δ_k = (A₁/A₀)ε_k there. *Complex σ_k:* as in 2.2 (*Complex σ_k*). For 𝐁_k the raw bound is (2.42) and for A(·) analyticity is entire, with size per 2.2 (1) (A(·) has no printed sup bound), and D2.2(6)(e) places the points with |σ_k − σ_{k,0}| < r_{ω,k} inside Ũ^c; the localised 𝐁_k- and A(·)-terms on (3.43) are under O6 (2.2 (1′)). For V^{(k)}(S_{k+1}) the analyticity in σ_k is part of O6 (2.2 (1′)), and every group's expectation is under (3.27) or (3.29), whose actions contain 𝐏^{(k)}; so the bound at complex σ_k over the ω-polydiscs rests on O6. For A(g_k^{−2}(·) − g_k^{−2}, ·) p.277 adds that "the large values of the function g_k^{−2}(·) − g_k^{−2} are suppressed by the exponential decay of 𝐇_k", so its terms, "localized in Z_k", are O(ε_k) as well. The potential enters the marked activity through the same expansion of the measure's potentials as the other potentials of (3.27) (D2.2(5)(b); RG2's interpolation (2.15)–(2.26) is the source there, not re-read here), in which each enters by its sup on its localisation domain. The group's bound is then (11)(c)'s per-slot bound with the factor O(ε_k) from the boundary-type potential: O(ε_k)·e^{−(κ_s−3)#(G)} times the ordinary slots' O(b_k) each. Since ε_k = A₀g_k(log g_k^{−2})^{p₀} ≤ (A₀/A₁)b_k/C₁^{RG2}, the group is O(b_k) at worst, and under R-𝐁 it has a collar cube Δ in its domain. So it is att(k+1, coll) with the bound of 2.2, the constant C enlarged by max(1, A₀/(A₁C₁^{RG2})). The parity of (11)(f) is not used (the boundary potentials are not shown to be even). The same holds for the 𝐄/𝐑-terms with Y ⊄ Λ_{k+1} (p.276 l.26–35: analytic on (3.43), the boundary-type potentials of §4.3), with R-fr: a connected Y meeting Λ_{k+1} and Λ_{k+1}ᶜ meets Ω_{k+1}∩Λ_{k+1}ᶜ, because Ω_{k+1}ᶜ lies at least two layers from Λ_{k+1} ((3.20)), so the group's domain meets the σ_k-bonds, which carry ω′, and (i) fails ((i) is read structurally: ω′ = 0 on 𝒢 means that no polymer domain and no member's domain of 𝒢 meets a bond of ω′), while (iii), as SW (a1) defines it, holds for 𝐄/𝐑-terms; such a group is att(k+1, coll) by (c) through (i). **Derived at real σ_k and complex 𝐔, given R-𝐁, R-fr (Bałaban's p.277) and 2.2's inputs (the re-run at amplitude b_k, D2.4(10)–(11) with R-src, the ω-radius amendment of 2.2). At complex σ_k over the ω-polydiscs NOT DERIVED: it rests on O6 (OPEN).** At complex 𝐔 (R15) the same holds on 𝒟^{sub}_{k+1} (SW §3), where the layer radii are the top-layer radii and the evaluation points of D2.2(4)(f) stay inside (SW §3(b3)): the potentials are analytic on Ũ^c_{k+1}(Y,(1+β)α̃₀,(1+β)α̃₁) (p.276), which contains 𝒟^{sub} on every layer.

*2.4 The reference extraction near Λ_{k+1}ᶜ, and the vacuum polymers (R5).*
- *Vacuum gas.* CRE p.277: the normaliser "is expanded in exactly the same way as in Sect. 7 [I]. The differences, like the restriction of the integration to Λ_{k+1}, or the larger class of localization domains, do not matter". The quoted sentence concerns the normaliser of (3.28)'s last integral (A_k = 0, the regular terms); for the boundary-potential polymers of (3.27) the basis is p.277's "the exponentiated cluster expansion of Sect. 7 [I], or rather its appropriate generalization" together with R-vac(ii) below. So the Kotecký–Preiss condition for the full vacuum gas on a labelled term, boundary polymers included, is Bałaban's statement given R-vac(ii) (CRE pp.277–279, R-vac(i′) extended by these sentences). **Reading R-vac(ii) (logged, for review):** the polymers meeting Λ_{k+1}ᶜ satisfy Lemma 3's bound with the O(ε_k) of their boundary potentials and R-L3's constants; p.277 names the class of domains and says the differences "do not matter", but the Lemma-3 form for these polymers is not printed. This closes "KP for the full gas on ℓ ≠ ℓ₀" (D2.4(2)(f), (9)) given R-vac(ii). The weights d(Z) = 9log2·|Z| of D2.4(2) are derived from Lemma 3's form, unchanged.
- *The interaction parts.* D2.2(6)(c) extracts the reference values at (1, 0, 0) and leaves the factors e^{−δΨ(M)} − 1 and e^{ε_TδΨ_≥(T)} − 1 as insertions. A cluster 𝒞 ≁ M meeting Coll_{k+1} (the single species test: it covers the clusters whose polymers meet Λ_{k+1}ᶜ and those whose activities depend on ω′, the source term (through the random-walk localisation of K, 2.2 (2), whose domains meet the σ_k-bonds) and V^{(k)}(S_{k+1}) of (3.27) being localised at the σ_k-bonds ⊆ Λ_{k+1}ᶜ) contributes to δΨ(M) a term bounded by |φ^T(𝒞)|e^{d(𝒞)} with the KP weight; summed, |δΨ^{coll}(M)| ≤ 2Σ_{𝒞≁M, 𝒞 meets Coll_{k+1}}|φ^T(𝒞)|e^{d(𝒞)} ≤ 2c|M|e^{−(κ/2)dist(M,Coll_{k+1})/M} (the factor 2 for the two terms compared in D2.2(6)(c); a cluster from M to the collar has at least dist/M π_{k+1}-cubes, each paying e^{−κ}; half the rate is spent, D2.4(2)(e)–(g)). So the part of e^{−δΨ(M)} − 1 due to such clusters is an insertion of size ≤ |δΨ^{coll}|e^{|δΨ^{coll}|} ≤ 2c|M|e^{2c|M|} (|e^{−x} − 1| ≤ |x|e^{|x|}; the cofactor e^{−δΨ^{rest}(M)} is absorbed as in (12)(c)) multiplying Ĥ(M), of species att(k+1, coll): the group's own bound (O(ρ_{k+1}) or O(b_k)) times 2c|M|e^{2c|M|} ≤ e^{5c#(M)}, a flat factor (not in θ) that raises (12)(f)'s flat budget from e^{6c#(M)} to e^{11c#(M)}, absorbable since c = (9/64)e^{−4κ}. *The clusters as vertices.* Expand e^{−δΨ^{coll}(M)} − 1 = Σ_{n≥1}(−δΨ^{coll}(M))^n/n! and each δΨ^{coll} into its clusters; each term is a product of n clusters 𝒞₁, …, 𝒞_n ≁ M meeting Coll_{k+1}, and these clusters are added to 𝒱(M) as vacuum links of the M-vertex, with shares sh(𝒞) ≤ #(𝒞)/4 paid by the spare half of their KP weight e^{d(𝒞)} (D2.4(2)(e)–(g)). The modulus sum over n and the clusters is still ≤ 2c|M|e^{2c|M|} times the vacuum-link factor e^{18c#(M)} of (12)(f), absorbable as above (c = (9/64)e^{−4κ}). The δΨ_≥(T) parts and the clusters of δΨ(M) not meeting Coll_{k+1} are (12)(c)'s vacuum links already (D2.2(6)(c) *Localisation*). So the group contains the clusters' supports, hence a collar cube, and the clusters are vertices of the M-vertex's link set, whose shares carry the extent weight; it enters 2.2's sum as a group with a collar cube, and 2.2's split gives its distance decay and its carried weight (the decay e^{−(κ/2)dist(M,Coll)/M} of the cluster sum is not needed for that). **Derived** given R-vac(ii) and 2.2's inputs, at real σ_k; over the ω-polydiscs at complex σ_k the ω′-dependent clusters, which sit in the (3.27) gas with 𝐏^{(k)}, rest on O6. The parts whose clusters do not meet Coll_{k+1} stay dev (D2.2(6)(c)).

*2.5 The species of Y^d_𝒢 near ℓ's data and the reference clause's sizes (R12).* D2.3(9): the deviation words Y^d_𝒢 of the bracket are the insertions of D(k+1) on ℓ; near ℓ's data they are the species of 2.1 by the same rule (their groups are the step-(k+1) groups). The reference clause (D2.0(3) l.322) asks for the factors at 𝐔 = e^{iη_kA′}·1 on every labelled term, with their bounds: for att species the bounds of 2.1–2.4 are sup bounds on 𝒟^{sub}_{k+1}; the reference point 𝐔 = e^{iη_kA′}·1 is not a point of that domain, and the bounds transfer to it by the uniformity of Bałaban's bounds on his space (RG2 p.6), exactly as D2.2(6)(c) argues for the vacuum (the same input, used there; D2.3(2) derives the analyticity at the reference point from D2.2(6)(e) at U_{k+1} = 1). No new size enters. **Derived given RG2 p.6 (accepted) and D(k)'s reference clause** (which makes (S-w) and (11)(c)(β) available at 𝐔 = 1 by induction), at real σ_k; where the clause asks for joint analyticity with ω within its radii, the att bounds at complex σ_k rest on O6.

## §3. The reclassified old dev insertions (R6)

- *Rule (D2.0(3) l.281, with 2.0's convention).* An old dev insertion W (made at step j(W) ≤ k) whose comparison region (the R_{t+1}-ball of its traversal, SW §5(e)) meets Ω_k∖Ω_{k+1} becomes att(k+1, Z), Z the component of Λ_{k+1}ᶜ containing the component of Ω_{k+1}ᶜ met by the region; one exists since Ω_k∖Ω_{k+1} ⊆ Ω_{k+1}ᶜ ⊆ Λ_{k+1}ᶜ. This also covers the map's "charge where Ω_{k+1} loses MR_k-cubes next to older regions": whatever the shape of Ω_{k+1}, every lost cube lies in Ω_{k+1}ᶜ ⊆ Λ_{k+1}ᶜ, in some component Z of Z_{k+1}, and the charge goes to Z's fundamental factor; no "step-(k+1) large-field set" smaller than Λ_{k+1}ᶜ is used.
- *Size at U_{k+1} (D2.4(6) l.1365–1370).* e^{θ(W)}‖Y_W(U_{k+1})‖ ≤ b_WDev_{Q^#_W}(U_{k+1}) by (T-dev) for W's comparison region 𝒬^{∼5} (SW §5(e)) inside Ω_k, and on Ω_k∖Ω_{k+1} the deviation of U_{k+1} at the step-k weight is < (1 − β/2)α_{0,k} by the membership (2.34) at j = k+1, n = k (derived in D2.2(6)(e) *Derived instead of assumed* for Ω_{k+1}; on Ω_k∖Ω_{k+1} it is CRE p.277's "no regularity improvement", i.e. the step-k conditions, R-m (Bałaban's, accepted), for k ≥ 2; at k = 1 the base point on Ω₁∖Ω₂ is D2.2(6)(e)'s OPEN item, used here as an input, not D2.6's). A step-(k+1) traversal n contains up to L step-k traversals, each with its own reclassified insertions (a one-time factor L). *The weights (§5.0's carried mass needs e^{ν_rσ}e^{(3/8)ν_e·ext^{coll}}, not e^{θ}‖Y_W‖ alone).* D(k)'s (ii-w) (map D2.4(12)(b), l.1529) carries W's reserve and extent weights, in W's production units (π_{j(W)}-sides): Σ_{W∋n_k}e^{θ(W)+ν_rσ(W)+ν_e·ext(W)}‖Y_W(U_{k+1})‖ ≤ C_χρ̃_k·c_σ(1 − β/2)α_{0,k}C_w, C_w := Σ_{t≥0}w_t, the right side of (ii) at U_{k+1} with the deviation bound above. The weights do not depend on the field; the bound at the argument U_{k+1} is D2.4(12)(b)'s carry clause, the weighted (T-dev) at U_{k+1} (map l.1529, accepted by the user there). *The distance from the extent, as in 2.1(a).* W's comparison region is the R_{t+1}-ball about a traversal n′ of W, t = t(W) the least t with X(W) ⊂ B_∞(n′, R_t), all in production units; minimality gives R_{t+1} ≤ M(ext(W)+1) + 2C_boxM for t ≥ 2 and R_2 = 3C_boxM, so the region lies within M(2ext(W) + 3C_box + 2) production bonds of every traversal of W and of every □ ∈ Y_W. The region meets a component of Ω_{k+1}ᶜ ⊆ Z, and a production side is L^{−(k+1−j(W))} π_{k+1}-sides, so d^Z_{k+1}(n)/M ≤ 2ext(W) + 3C_box + 3 (the +1 for n against its step-k traversal) and ext^{coll}(W) ≤ 2ext(W) + 3C_box + 2 (in π_{k+1}-sides; Z ⊆ Coll_{k+1}). Split e^{ν_e·ext} = e^{(ν_e/4)ext}e^{(3/4)ν_e·ext}: the first factor pays the distance, 1 ≤ e^{(ν_e/8)(3C_box+3)}e^{−(ν_e/8)d^Z_{k+1}(n)/M}e^{(ν_e/4)ext}, and the second the lineage weight, e^{(3/8)ν_e·ext^{coll}} ≤ e^{(3/8)ν_e(3C_box+2)}e^{(3/4)ν_e·ext}. So Σ_{W∋n, reclassified}e^{ν_rσ(W)}e^{(3/8)ν_e·ext^{coll}(W)}e^{θ}‖Y_W‖ ≤ L·e^{(ν_e/2)(3C_box+3)}C_χρ̃_kc_σC_w(1 − β/2)α_{0,k}e^{−c_*d^Z_{k+1}(n)/M} = O(Lρ_k(α_{0,k}/ᾱ_k))e^{−c_*d^Z_{k+1}(n)/M}, c_* = ν_e/8 the rate of 2.1(a), with the full reserve ν_r (no Cauchy–Schwarz is needed: the extent weight of (ii-w) gives both the distance and the lineage weight). Under q₀ = q₁ (SW §2, accepted by the user provisionally; q₀ ≤ q₁ is entailed by D2.4(6)(ζ), so no other case arises) α_{0,k}/ᾱ_k ≤ C₀/C₁ + 1, and ρ_k ≤ (1+β₀)⁸ρ_{k+1} ≤ Cρ̂_{k+1}, so this is Cρ̂_{k+1}e^{−c_*d^Z_{k+1}(n)/M} with W attached to Z, carrying §5.0's weights: D(k+1)(ii)'s per-component form. At complex data on 𝒟^{sub}_{k+1} the background's deviation gains the complex part a*_W + j*_W ≤ 3S₃₀α_{1,k}, where a*_W, j*_W are the maxima of SW (b2)'s per-traversal a*_n, j*_n over the traversals of W's comparison region (each ≤ the bound of (b2)), inside the same weighted sum, so the bound holds there with the constant multiplied by 1 + 3S₃₀α_{1,k}/((1 − β/2)α_{0,k}) ≤ 1 + 6S₃₀C₁/C₀ under q₀ = q₁. **Derived** given R-m (k ≥ 2), the OPEN k = 1 base point (D2.2(6)(e)), (ii-w) at U_{k+1} (D2.4(12)(b), accepted by the user), and (T-dev) at its inputs (SW §5–§6), among which R14's (β) on the cubes of (O1) is derived by §9.3's route (a) given R-ζ(b), R-h and R-ζ(a) beyond Ω_{i+1}^∼, for review. So §3 is not closed: it rests on O3 (OPEN) and on R-m (register R6).
- *Carrying.* From step k+2 on W is att(k+1, Z) and is carried by §5, with its bound fixed at step k+1: its weighted mass w(W) of §5.0 (reserve e^{ν_rσ(W)}, lineage weight e^{(3/8)ν_e·ext^{coll}(W)}) enters A_{k+1}(Z) through the bound above.

## §4. Kind (γ): groups with a component meeting Ω_kᶜ, case (B) of D2.1(4) (R4)

*4.1 Where the size question sits.* D2.1(4) case (B): a group whose polymer has a component Y meeting Ω_kᶜ. Kind (γ) also takes the groups whose arc leaves Ω_k ((11)(c), 2.1(b), rule change (4)). Y_G depends on B′ through 𝐇(s, tB′) on Y (D2.1(4)(b)); at points of a layer Ω_m∖Ω_{m+1}, m < k, the kernel of 𝐇 carries the prefactor (L^mη)^{−1} = L^{k−m} (VAR (190), p.308: the first entry of the bracket, (L^jη)^{−1}, for y ∈ Λ_j in VAR's notation, VAR's B^j(Λ_j) being CRE's Ω_j∖Ω_{j+1}, map D2.1(5) V-2), against the walk decay e^{−δ₀d(y,y′)/8} with d the weighted distance of PRT2 (2.46) (map D2.2(6)(e) l.944–945). Every surviving component Y meeting Ω_kᶜ also meets supp B′ ⊆ Ω_{k+1} (D2.1(5) l.536: a component with B′|_Y = 0 contributes zero), so it crosses the layer Ω_k∖Ω_{k+1}, of width ≥ 6LR_{k+1} π_k-sides ((G1) as stated: dist(Ω_{k+1}, Λ_kᶜ) ≥ 6 layers of LMR_{k+1}-cubes, and Ω_kᶜ ⊆ Λ_kᶜ); more precisely, a component reaching the layer Ω_m∖Ω_{m+1} crosses the layers m+1, …, k−1, each of weighted length > RM₁ with R = R_k (derived: by (G1) at step j the layer j has width ≥ 6LMR_{j+1} in step-j units; CRE (2.5) (p.255: R_j the smallest power of L with R_j ≥ (log g_j^{−2})^r) gives R_k < L(log g_k^{−2})^r, and (2.7)'s first line (p.255, n = k > m = j+1) gives (log g_k^{−2})^r ≤ (1+β₀)(log g_{j+1}^{−2})^r ≤ (1+β₀)R_{j+1}; so 6LMR_{j+1} > 6MR_k/(1+β₀) ≥ M₁R_k for β₀ ≤ 5 and M ≥ M₁), and the layer k by (G1) (its width 6LMR_{k+1} in step-k units is ≥ RM₁: VAR (1)'s R for CRE's sequence is taken as R_k, the identification of (G2) and map D2.2(6)(e) ("R of the size of R_j"; CRE p.256 prints the domains as unions of MR_j-cubes with boundary distance ≥ 2MR_j, not VAR's R), and (2.7)'s second line (p.255, m = k, n = k+1) gives (log g_k^{−2})^r ≤ (1+β₀)(log g_{k+1}^{−2})^r, so R_k < L(1+β₀)R_{k+1} by (2.5) and 6LMR_{k+1} > 6MR_k/(1+β₀) ≥ M₁R_k for β₀ ≤ 5, M ≥ M₁); so its walk weight is at most e^{−δ₀(k−m)RM₁/8}. The s-parameters, and R-dec's scope, sit on D2.1(4)'s uniform π_k-partition, not on CRE p.276's proper-scale partition (side ML^jη on Ω_j∖Ω_{j+1}). **Derived** from (G1), VAR (1), CRE (2.5) and (2.7) (p.255; at j = k−1 the case n = m is trivial; layer k from (2.7)'s second line with m = k, n = k+1), and D2.1(5) (PRT2 (2.46), the weighted distance d, as the map cites it).
*4.2 Sizes (derived from D2.2(4)(f) *The older regions* and D2.4(11)(c), on the sub-domain).* With R-dec (D2.2(6)(e); Bałaban's, accepted), on the polydisc sup_{layer m}|𝐇(s,tB′)| ≤ O(1)(Lθ)^{k−m}h_k, θ = e^{−δ₀RM₁/32}. The analyticity margin for the old factors on the layer m, on 𝒟^{sub}_k (SW §3 (b1): the top-layer radius α_{1,k} with the index factor F_n = 1 − β(1 − 2^{−(k−n)}) on the layer n), is at least β2^{−(k−m)−1}α_{1,k} in the step-k field units of the top layer, so the deformation/margin ratio on layer m is ≤ O(1)(2Lθ)^{k−m}·r′_k = O(1)ϑ^{k−m}r′_k ≤ (O(1)ϑ)^{k−m}r′_k with ϑ := 2Lθ, r′_k := h_k/((β/2)α_{1,k}) (the top-layer ratio without the O(1)) and R-dec's O(1) ≥ 1. Under RM₁ ≥ 32log(4L·O(1))/δ₀ (g_K small: R ≥ (log g^{−2})^r by CRE (2.5), as in D2.2(6)(e) *Propagation*), O(1)ϑ ≤ ½, so the ratio on layer m is ≤ 2^{−(k−m)}r′_k and the top layer binds. So the polydisc radius T_Y of D2.2(4)(f), the largest T with the deformation inside the margin on every layer that Y meets, is at least the top-layer value, and (11)(c)'s 1/T_Y ≤ C_Tb_k/ᾱ_k holds for every component Y, whether or not Y meets Ω_kᶜ. The per-slot bounds (α), (β) of (11)(c) then hold for kind (γ) on (4)(f)'s polydisc, with D2.2(6)(e) *Analyticity of the new insertions* on CRE's domain (RG2 p.15 for the potentials and the Gaussian parts, (4)(f) for the old factors). For (α) and (11)(f), which run at T_Y = ε_H/|B′|_Y, R-dec is needed on D2.1(4)'s polydisc |s(Δ)| ≤ e^{κ_s}, |t_YB′|_Y ≤ ε_H < ε₃^{RG2} (RG2's ε₃, not VAR's in (77)): **reading R-dec⁺ (an extension of R-dec, logged in §8, for the user's review):** on that polydisc, sup_{layer m}|𝐇(s,tB′)| ≤ O(1)(Lθ)^{k−m}·4B₀e^{16κ_s}max_Y|t_YB′|_Y. R-dec is posed on (4)(e)–(f)'s polydisc, where |t_YB′|_Y is of order α_{1,k}; D2.1(4)'s allows |t_YB′|_Y up to ε_H, larger by a factor ~ε_H/α_{1,k}, so R-dec⁺ is a new reading, not a restatement. Its basis is R-dec's (RG2 p.5's single-scale argument holds for |B′| < ε₃^{RG2}, and ε_H < ε₃^{RG2}; (190) bounds δ𝓗/δB uniformly in B on its range, so |𝓗| is linear in sup|B|; CRE p.277 is qualitative). VAR Prop. 9's B-range: (190) rests on (189), stated for A′ satisfying (77) (p.308), and the B-range of Prop. 9 (p.309: "small configurations V′", with ε₂ ≤ B₅ε₁ by (173)) is (172), p.305, |B′| < 2C₁^{VAR}ε₁ "for ε₁ sufficiently small"; (77), p.290, is VAR's internal smallness of ε₃. Let ε̄₁ be a value of ε₁ for which VAR Sect. G holds: Bałaban's "sufficiently small" of (172) and (187), which includes (169)'s M′ε̄₁ ≤ a₁ (p.305; a₁ bounds M′ε₁, with M′ ≤ M/(R₁M₁), p.304). (172)'s hypothesis is |V′ − 1| < C₁^{VAR}ε₁, and |B′| < 2C₁^{VAR}ε₁ is its consequence. On (α)'s closed polydisc |B′| ≤ ε_H, with B′ 𝔤^ℂ-valued at complex t_Y, so |V′ − 1| = |e^{iB′} − 1| ≤ e^{ε_H} − 1 < 2ε_H in a submultiplicative norm (|Σ_{n≥1}(iX)^n/n!| ≤ e^{|X|} − 1, and (e^x − 1)/x ≤ e − 1 < 2 for 0 < x ≤ 1, under the constant condition ε_H ≤ 1, in D2.1(4) *Constants*; ε_H < ε₃^{RG2} does not imply it). With c_V ≥ 1 the constant of |V − 1|_{VAR} ≤ c_V|V − 1| on {e^{iX} : |X| ≤ ε_H} (only this local form is used: globally it fails for a non-faithful π, e.g. V = −1 in SU(2)'s adjoint; locally e^{iX} = 1 with |X| < 2π forces X = 0; it depends on G and π only; c_V = 1 if VAR's norm is the one used here), |V′ − 1|_{VAR} < 2c_Vε_H, and the range covers it under the constant condition ε_H ≤ C₁^{VAR}ε̄₁/(2c_V), with ε₁ := max(the data regularity, 2c_Vε_H/C₁^{VAR}) ≤ ε̄₁ (then C₁^{VAR}ε₁ ≥ 2c_Vε_H > |V′ − 1|_{VAR}, strict as (172) needs), in D2.1(4) *Constants*. It is not coupling-dependent: ε̄₁ does not depend on VAR's R ≥ R₁. Theorem 1 prints this for its threshold: "The constants a₀, a₁, B₃, depend on d and L only" (p.279), for every R ≥ R₁ (p.277), with a₁ "a largest constant such, that the restriction ε₁ ≤ a₁ implies all the other restrictions we have imposed on ε₁" (p.304). Sect. G's further smallness ((172)'s "ε₁ sufficiently small", p.305; (77) (p.290), re-imposed at p.307 for A′ "satisfying (77) with ε₃ sufficiently small"; the Neumann series, p.308) re-runs Sects. A–E and is stated through VAR's C₁ (= C₁^{VAR}), "some absolute constant" (p.280, where C₁ = L³ is taken), VAR's C₄ (d and L only, p.293), B₃ (d and L only, p.303) and B₅ = 6B₁B₃C₁ ((173), p.305), through (187)'s O(1) (p.308: "≤ O(1)B₀C₄B₅ε₁ … for ε₁ sufficiently small"), an absolute constant in VAR's notation (accepted from Bałaban: VAR writes O(1) for an absolute constant, p.291 after (86), "We have gathered together all the constants into an absolute constant O(1)", and p.291 after (90), "with a different absolute constant"; "absolute" in VAR's sense, as C₁ = L³ at p.280, so R-independent), and through B₀ and B₁: B₀ is the constant of (46), p.285, from "Theorem 3.12 from [5]" ([5] = PBG), and PBG p.423–424 defines B₀ as the common best constant of those operators, "These constants also depend on d and L only"; B₁ enters at p.281 through "(1.36)–(1.39) of [6]" ([6] = REG), and REG p.83 states Theorem 2 for "the constants B₁, B₂(β₀) … absolute constants depending on d and L only". VAR's M of (169) is the regularity-cube parameter of Theorem 1, "M ≤ M(ε₁)", "M(ε₁) = R₁M₁(a₁/ε₁)" (p.279), and M′ ≤ M/(R₁M₁) (p.304), so "M′ε₁ ≤ a₁" (p.305) is the condition ε₁ ≤ R₁M₁a₁/M in p.304's second case (M′ = (R₁M₁)^{−1}M) and ε₁ ≤ a₁ in the first (M′ = 1); in both cases it is R-independent. R₁, M₁ are "fixed in such a way that all the results of [3, 5, 6] hold" (p.277). So for Theorem 1's a₁ the uniformity in R is printed (p.279, p.304), and for Sect. G it is derived from the printed dependences of VAR's C₁, C₄, B₀, B₁, B₃ and B₅ on d and L only and from (187)'s O(1) being absolute. So the uniformity is Bałaban's: printed for a₁, derived from printed constant dependences for Sect. G. The data part, data regularity ≤ ε̄₁, is a smallness on g_K (the map's VAR (7) item), not part of the constant condition. That (172) admits complex V′ on the polydisc is part of the reading R-dec⁺. C₁^{VAR} is VAR's C₁, not CRE's C₁ in α_{1,k} or C₁^{RG2}. ε_H is shrunk by R-dec⁺'s O(1), c_πc_CM·O(1)·4B₀e^{16κ_s}ε_H ≤ 1, and by ε_H ≤ C₁^{VAR}ε̄₁/(2c_V), constants fixed before the coupling. The shrunk ε_H replaces ε_H everywhere it occurs (T_Y = ε_H/|B′|_Y, the hypotheses O(1)ε_k ≤ ε_H and C₁^{RG2}δ_k ≤ ε_H/2, c_ev, c_M; map D2.1(4) *Constants* says so), and (11)(f)'s T_Y ≥ 2, i.e. C₁^{RG2}δ_k ≤ ε_H/2, still holds for g_K small. Three further inputs of (11)(c) for kind (γ): (i) the 𝐇-part of the covariant transports on the older-layer bonds of the group's words: the factor per fine bond is exp(c_πη|𝐇(b)|) with |𝐇(b)| ≤ O(1)(Lθ)^{k−m}h_k ≤ O(1)h_k by R-dec, and a π_k-cube carries C-length ≤ c_CM, i.e. ≤ c_CM/η fine bonds, so the factor per cube is ≤ e^{c_πc_CM·O(1)h_k} ≤ e under c_πc_CM·O(1)h_k ≤ 1, which is (11)(c)(β)'s e^{1}; at (α)'s radius ε_H the same factor per cube is ≤ e by the ε_H shrink above, and that is the e^{1} for 𝐇-transports inside e^{−(κ_s−3)#(Y)} of (11)(c)(α), which it fits unchanged; (ii) a′ ∈ 𝒟^{sub}_{k+1}, and the evaluation points e^{iη(𝐇(s,tB′)+a″)}U_{k+1} of the group's old factors lie in 𝒟^{sub}_k (SW §3(b3), under O(1)Lθ ≤ (1−β)/min(β,1), listed in §8); on 𝒟^{sub}_{k+1}, |a′| < α_{1,k+1}/L per fine bond, in step-k field units, on every layer, so (11)(c)'s 20c_πM‖a′‖_∞ ≤ 1 holds off Ω_k as well (the att clause on 𝒟^{sub}, §5.0, for review); (iii) the inherited members inside the group (att, lw, wrap(j) and dev) enter (11)(c)(β)'s product ∏_We^{−θ(W)}[e^{θ(W)}‖Y_W‖] by per-member sup bounds on 𝒟^{sub}_k over D(k)(iii)'s ω-polydiscs, which follow term by term from the (ii)-form totals (§5.0–5.1). At every point of D(k)(iii)'s ω-polydiscs, R(w(s,t))ω included, the members fall into three classes. (1) A member made at a step j+1 ≥ 2 that depends on ω: by 2.1(i) its producing group had ω′_j ≠ 0, so it is att(j+1, coll) (2.1(c)–(d)), wrap(j+1) (2.1(e)), or of an inherited species, att, lw or wrap (*Merged groups*); its total over the ω-polydiscs rests on O6 (§2.2 (1′), 2.1(e)) (conservatively for a member whose producing group depended on ω only through ω₀ of a touched step-1 member, with no σ_j-bond and no boundary-type potential: its inputs are then O3 and §5.0), and a touched one is evaluated at R(w(s,t))ω, complex for complex s, t even at real outer data (D2.2(4)(f)), so this holds at every point of the (s, t_Y)-polydisc. (2) A member made at step 1 (att(1, ·), wrap(1), lw(Z), or a step-1 dev insertion): it depends on ω only through ω₀, V3's exterior fields A_e, a_old (map D(k)(iii) *Outer fluctuation fields*; the lw slots through a_old, A_e and m̂, §6.1 *The ω-polydisc*), and its bound over the r_{ω,0}-polydisc is D(1)(iii)'s bound clause, "with the same bounds" (the map derives the joint analyticity at k = 1, *At k = 1*, on 𝒰_c by V3 Lemma charts, L475; the bound clause at complex ω₀ is 5.0's amendment, for review) and, for lw, §6.1 *The ω-polydisc* with R-st (derived); not O6. Its evaluation point R(w)ω₀ lies over Ω₁∩Λ₁ᶜ ⊆ Ω₁∖Ω₂, where *Propagation* rests on O3 (4.5). (3) A member made at a step j+1 ≥ 2 that does not depend on ω: in particular every dev member of step ≥ 2, since by 2.1(i), read structurally, a dev group has no polymer domain and no member's domain meeting an ω′_j-bond; its bound is ω-free, and nothing changes. So the dev members that can depend on ω are step-1 dev insertions only, in class (2); whether a step-1 good group's Υ⁰_W consults ω₀ is not settled here (its free inputs lie in D(Λ), IT-1 §3d(4), but the rows it uses may be fed by A_e, map D(1)(iii) *At k = 1*); if it does, its bound over the polydisc is D(1)(iii)'s, not O6. The Schwarz step of (11)(c)(β) uses no derivative of the members; their sum over the kind-(γ) groups containing them is §5's lineage form (T0). The gradient, curvature and 𝐉 parts of the deformation 𝐇(s,tB′) on the older layers are bounded by R-dec in those norms (D2.2(6)(e) *Older layers*, with SW (b3)'s radii; the curvature margins are full-domain). The prefactor L^{k−m} of VAR (190) is inside (Lθ)^{k−m} and is beaten by θ^{k−m}: that is the comparison the map asked for (D2.1(5) l.535–536), and it is R-dec's content. **Derived** given R-dec with its gradient part R-adm, R-dec⁺, R-m, R-cpl₀ (inside SW (b3)), SW §3(b2)'s restated dev clause on 𝒟^{sub} with its basis (LPG) (SW §5, for review), SW (b3)'s condition, the smallness conditions RM₁ ≥ 32log(4L·O(1))/δ₀ and c_πc_CM·O(1)h_k ≤ 1, the constant conditions on ε_H (c_πc_CM·O(1)·4B₀e^{16κ_s}ε_H ≤ 1, ε_H ≤ C₁^{VAR}ε̄₁/(2c_V) with ε̄₁ VAR Sect. G's threshold, accepted from Bałaban; its R-uniformity printed for a₁ (p.279, p.304) and derived for Sect. G from the printed constant dependences), and §5.0–5.1 (for review), with the constant condition ε_H ≤ 1; input (iii) for every touched inherited member made at a step ≥ 2 that depends on ω (of species att, lw or wrap(j); class (1)), in the (T2) and the wrap(j) cases, at every point of D(k)(iii)'s ω-polydiscs, R(w(s,t))ω included: NOT DERIVED (O6); for the step-1 members that depend on ω₀ (class (2): att(1, ·), wrap(1), lw, step-1 dev) not O6: their polydisc bounds are D(1)(iii)'s and §6.1's, and that R(w(s,t))ω₀ lies inside the polydisc rests on O3 (4.5).
*4.3 Parity and the species.* (11)(f) bounds the odd part of the potentials in the integrated field; it uses the evenness of the Gaussian measure and of χ^{(k)}, not of the potentials. Boundary-type potential (definition, from CRE p.276 l.2–16 and l.26–35): every potential of the localised action (3.27) whose localisation domain Y is not contained in Λ_{k+1} (p.276 sends exactly these to the boundary terms 𝐁^{(k+1)}, with the analyticity space (3.43); on the older layers the terms are those of 𝐄^{(j)} "with η replaced by L^{−n}", p.276 l.2–16, and of 𝐑_k by ¶3's "treated in exactly the same way as 𝐄_k"; all of them have Y ⊄ Λ_{k+1}, by set inclusion alone: Λ_{k+1} ⊆ Ω_{k+1} and Ω_n∖Ω_{n+1} ⊆ Ω_{k+1}ᶜ for n ≤ k), together with 𝐁_k, A(g_k^{−2}(·) − g_k^{−2}, ·) and V^{(k)}(S_{k+1}) of §2.3. A kind-(γ) group with ω′ = 0 and no boundary-type potential has all its potentials localised in Λ_{k+1}, where they are the terms of 𝐄_k and 𝐑_k with Lemma 2's structure (the identification of Lemmas 5–6 [I] with Lemma 2's structure that (11)(f) *Scope* accepts, R-vac(i′)), so (11)(f) applies on the potential side, and on the slot side at the (α) radius by R-dec⁺; the group is O(ρ_{k+1}), as kinds (α)–(β). A kind-(γ) group with ω′ ≠ 0 or a boundary-type potential is O(b_k) by §2.2–§2.3 at real σ_k and complex 𝐔; at complex σ_k over the ω-polydiscs this rests on O6 (2.2's and 2.3's labels). *Species (rule change (3) of 2.1, with 2.1's precedence).* A kind-(γ) group containing an inherited att(j, ·)/lw/wrap(j) member, touched or untouched, takes the first inherited species (2.1 *Merged groups*), whatever its (i)/(iii) status. If that species is wrap(j), the group is wrap, with 2.1(e)'s bound and the growth L per later step. Otherwise its bound is §5.2's (T2) with §2.2's per-slot factors pointwise in ω′ (R-src) and 4.2's (R-dec, R-dec⁺), and its sum is §5's lineage form (T0). Untouched old members join groups through D2.2(6)(d)'s arc and second rules, so "containing" is the test, not "touched". Of the remaining kind-(γ) groups, those with no inherited member, the ones whose set X(𝒢) does not lift to the cover (a group with an inherited att/lw member keeps that species even when X(𝒢) does not lift: (T2) is a Cauchy estimate on §4.2's polydisc radius, and (T3) uses torus distances, (G1) on the torus and a path inside the group from Q_D to Ω_{n+1}, so neither uses a lift) are wrap(k+1) by 2.1(e)'s precedence, with amplitude C_S″b_k from the re-run (D(k)(ii)'s wrap display max(C_S′ρ̂_j, C_S″b_j); neither parity nor R-vac(i′) is used) and §4.2's per-slot bounds; rule change (3) applies to those whose set lifts: they are att(k+1, coll) ((γ1) and (γ2) below use distances defined on the cover). *The collar cube.* (ii)'s "hull with allowance" is the arc I(𝒢) of D2.2(6)(d) *Arcs*, whose length allowance length(I(𝒢)) ≤ 20Σ_γ(diam supp_γ + 1) is already part of I(𝒢); so (ii) fails iff a component of 𝒢's polymers meets Ω_kᶜ or some traversal of I(𝒢) lies outside Ω_k (this makes 2.1(ii) exact; rule change (4), with its component clause). Two cases, both with a collar cube: (γ1) a component Y of the group meets Ω_kᶜ ⊆ Λ_{k+1}ᶜ at a point p; the π_{k+1}-cube of p is in Coll_{k+1} (2.0); p ∈ X(𝒢), so for every traversal n, dist(n, Coll_{k+1}) ≤ M(ext_{k+1}(𝒢)+1), and every □ ∈ Y_𝒢 ⊆ X(𝒢) is within 2ext_{k+1}(𝒢) + 1 sides of p's cube, so ext^{coll}(𝒢) ≤ 2ext_{k+1}(𝒢) + 1; (γ2) no component meets Ω_kᶜ, but a traversal n′ of I(𝒢) is not contained in Ω_k; take a point p′ ∈ n′ ∩ Ω_kᶜ ⊆ Λ_{k+1}ᶜ; the π_{k+1}-cube of p′ is in Coll_{k+1}; a traversal is one step-(k+1) bond, so dist(x, p′) ≤ dist(x, n′) + 1 (in bonds, M per π_{k+1}-side); by ext_{k+1}'s definition (the ℓ∞-ball of radius ext_{k+1} sides about every traversal of 𝒢's interval contains X(𝒢)), for every traversal n and any x ∈ X(𝒢), dist(n, p′) ≤ dist(n, x) + dist(x, n′) + 1 ≤ 2M·ext_{k+1}(𝒢) + 1, so dist(n, Coll_{k+1}) ≤ 2M·ext_{k+1}(𝒢) + 1, and every □ ∈ Y_𝒢 lies within ext_{k+1}(𝒢) + 1/M sides of p′, so ext^{coll}(𝒢) ≤ ext_{k+1}(𝒢) + 1. *The split, per case.* (γ1) is 2.2 verbatim, with the split (¼, ¾): e^{−(ν_e/4)ext_{k+1}} ≤ e^{ν_e/4}max_{Δ∈Coll}e^{−(ν_e/4)dist(Δ,n)/M}, and e^{(3/4)ν_e·ext_{k+1}} ≥ e^{−(3/8)ν_e}e^{(3/8)ν_e·ext^{coll}} from ext^{coll} ≤ 2ext_{k+1} + 1. (γ2) uses the split (½, ½): from dist(n, Coll) ≤ 2M·ext_{k+1} + 1, e^{−(ν_e/2)ext_{k+1}} ≤ e^{ν_e/(4M)}max_{Δ∈Coll}e^{−(ν_e/4)dist(Δ,n)/M}, and from ext^{coll} ≤ ext_{k+1} + 1, e^{(ν_e/2)ext_{k+1}} ≥ e^{−ν_e/2}e^{(ν_e/2)ext^{coll}} ≥ e^{−ν_e/2}e^{(3/8)ν_e·ext^{coll}}. The split is chosen per group, so the termwise bound and the sum hold at the rate c_*′ ≤ ν_e/4 with the carried weight e^{(3/8)ν_e·ext^{coll}}; (γ1) costs e^{(5/8)ν_e} and (γ2) e^{ν_e/2 + ν_e/(4M)} ≤ e^{(5/8)ν_e} (M ≥ 2), both inside 2.2's C. *Bound.* The att(k+1, coll) kind-(γ) groups are bounded in 2.2's collar form: Σ_{𝒢∋n, (γ)}e^{ν_rσ(𝒢)}e^{(3/8)ν_e·ext^{coll}(𝒢)}e^{θ}‖Y_𝒢‖ ≤ Cρ_{t,k+1}Σ_Δ𝔪̂^{(k+1)}_Δe^{−c_*′dist(Δ,n)/M}, using for every such group the re-run at amplitude b_k ≤ Cρ_{t,k+1} (2.2's chain). The parity gain of (11)(f), and with it R-vac(i′), is not needed for any att(k+1, coll) or wrap(k+1) kind-(γ) group; the O(ρ_{k+1}) remark above is not used. The per-component totals then come from 2.0's nearest-component marks, as in 2.2 *Per component*. The interval extent contains the arc's traversals, so no arc-hull distance enters. **Derived** given R-dec, R-dec⁺ (for the (α) factor b_k/ε_H), (S-w) and 2.2's re-run at amplitude b_k, R-src for ω′ ≠ 0 with the ω-radius amendment, §2.3's bound for the boundary-type potentials (derived for the group's own production at real σ_k and complex 𝐔; for the ω′ ≠ 0 and boundary-type groups at complex σ_k over the ω-polydiscs NOT DERIVED, resting on O6; for the inherited-species cases, (T2) and wrap(j), NOT DERIVED (O6) whenever a touched inherited member made at a step ≥ 2 depends on ω (4.2(iii) class (1), of species att, lw or wrap(j): 2.1(e) produces wrap groups failing (i)), at every point of the (s, t_Y)-polydisc, since it is evaluated at R(w(s,t))ω, complex even at real outer data, D2.2(4)(f), (6)(e) (untouched members are evaluated at (U_{k+1}, ω), D2.2(4)(e)); a touched step-1 member that depends on ω₀ (class (2)) rests on O3 there, not on O6; the att(k+1, coll) groups failing only (ii) are ω′-free in 2.1(i)'s structural sense (no polymer domain and no member's domain meets an ω′-bond), and the reference-extraction clusters meeting Coll_{k+1}, which carry δΨ(M)'s ω′-dependence, are routed into att parts by 2.1(d); since ω′ enters only through terms localised in domains meeting the σ_k-bonds and through the old members' own domains (2.2), the activity of such a group, and the set of such groups, do not depend on ω′, so each term of their rooted sum, and the sum, equal their values at real σ_k, where the re-run at amplitude b_k (2.2) is derived: their bound holds over the ω-polydiscs and is derived; with R-𝐁 and R-fr, Bałaban's p.277), the inputs (i)–(iii) of 4.2, SW (a3), the sub-domain form (SW §3), §5.0–5.2, 2.1(e) (the wrap precedence and bound, with the torus condition D2.0(5)(g)), rule changes (3) and (4) and the c_*′ amendment (all for review).
*4.4 The bonds crossing ∂Λ_j^{VAR} (D2.2(4)(d), (6)(e) l.980–981).* The gauge transformation w of (106) is a site function on each block B^n(y) (D2.2(4)(d): w block-local, |log w| ≤ O(1)sup|𝐇_k|; the chain of (106) runs on the block of the site). A bond b = (x, x′) of C whose endpoints lie in blocks of different layers transports in the words with U_{k+1}(b), and under the step's gauge transformation U(b) ↦ w(x)U(b)w(x′)^{−1}, with w(x), w(x′) given by (106) on their own blocks. The words are traces of products of covariant slots and transports (D2.0(3)), so Cov(k+1) holds across such bonds by the transport law alone; nothing block-wise is needed (the covariance of the slots at the two ends is D2.2(6)(b), per block). The size of the transport at complex data is e^{c_π|phase(b)|}, with the phase on 𝒟^{sub}_{k+1} bounded by α_{1,k+1} per step-(k+1) bond on every layer (§5.4). So the crossing bonds add nothing to the sizes of the insertions beyond §5.4's transport factor and the 𝐇-part of the transport on the crossing bond itself, bounded by 4.2(i) (R-dec). **Derived** given R-dec, R-REG81 (the block-locality of (106), inherited from D2.2(4)(d)) and §5.4. What remains OPEN at these bonds is D2.2(4)(d)'s representative item (the gauge frame of σ_j, OPEN there), which is not a size question.
*4.5 What (4)(c)'s ω-factors cost.* For the old ω-dependent factors inside a kind-(γ) group, |R(w)ω − ω| ≤ (e^{2c_G|log w|} − 1)|ω| (D2.2(4)(c)) with |log w| ≤ C_wθ^{k−j−1}h_k on ω_j's layer (D2.2(6)(e) *The size of w*); the factors are evaluated within D(k)(iii)'s ω-polydiscs by *Propagation*; their bounds there are their production bounds, in 4.2(iii)'s three classes: sup bounds on 𝒟^{sub}_k for the members made at a step ≥ 2 that depend on ω, of species att, lw or wrap(j) (class (1); §5.0; at every point of D(k)(iii)'s ω-polydiscs, R(w(s,t))ω included, they rest on O6); D(1)(iii)'s bounds over the r_{ω,0}-polydisc for the step-1 members att(1, ·), wrap(1), lw (with §6.1 *The ω-polydisc*) and the step-1 dev insertions, which depend on ω through ω₀ only; and, for the dev members of step ≥ 2, none needed, since by 2.1(i) they are ω-free (4.2(iii) class (3)), with (T-dev)'s bounds in 𝐔 (with §9.3's route (a) for the cubes of O1, derived given R-ζ(b), R-h and R-ζ(a) beyond Ω_{i+1}^∼, for review). No new size. **Derived for the dev members of step ≥ 2 (ω-free). For every touched member made at a step ≥ 2 that depends on ω (of species att, lw or wrap(j)) NOT DERIVED, resting on O6, at every point of the (s, t_Y)-polydisc whatever the outer data: they are evaluated at R(w(s,t))ω, which is complex for complex s, t even at real outer σ (D2.2(4)(f), (4)(g), (6)(e) *The size of w*). For the step-1 members that depend on ω₀ the bounds are D(1)(iii)'s, not O6, and the evaluation rests on O3 (below);** given R-dec, R-adm, R-m, R-cpl₀, R-𝐇 and R-𝐇₀ (through *Propagation*), the sub-domain form of the att bounds over D(k)(iii)'s ω-polydiscs (§5.0, for review); for the dev members with §9.3's route (a) for the cubes of (O1), given R-ζ(b), R-h and R-ζ(a) beyond Ω_{i+1}^∼; the ω-radius amendment r_{ω,j} = g_j^{1/2} (for review, used by *Propagation*); not closed, at every k, for the factors depending on ω₀ or ω₁ and for every factor whose evaluation domain meets Ω₁∖Ω₂ (O3: the j = 0 and j = 1 base points on Ω₁∖Ω₂, map D2.2(6)(e) *The inclusion*, OPEN there, reaching every k through (4)(f)), or where the gauge frame of σ_j enters (D2.2(4)(d), OPEN there).

## §5. The carry lemma: attached insertions through later steps (R7, R8)

*5.0 Statement.* Fix j and a component Z of Z_j (species att(j, Z); the same for att(j, coll) per assigned mark and for lw(Z), §6.4; wrap(j) stays in (ii), 2.1(e)). Normalisation: (ii′-a) counts each insertion of att(j, Z) once, at the first of its traversals ((ii)'s per-traversal sums are added over the traversals n of the component; an insertion lying on several traversals is counted at its first; the map's (ii′-a) display is amended to this normalisation). Weighted mass: w(W) := e^{ν_rσ_j(W)}e^{(3/8)ν_e·ext^{coll}_j(W)}e^{θ(W)}‖Y_W‖, with σ_j(W) the reserve count of D2.4(12)(b) and the lineage extent ext^{coll}_j(W) := max_{□∈Y_W}dist(□, Coll_j) in π_j-sides (Coll_j ⊇ Z's cubes). Here and in 2.1, 5.2–5.6, Y_W as a set means the set of π-cubes meeting W's domain X_W (map l.622, which contains W's interval) or its dependence set Q_W ((T1)); it is not the value Y_W of the insertion in ‖Y_W‖. Let A_j(Z) be the production total of this species (the att(j, Z) term of map (5)(f)'s A_j(Z); it leaves (ii) "at the end of step j + 1's map", D2.0(3) l.300): Σ_{W∈att(j,Z)}e^{c_W}w(W) ≤ A_j(Z) at step j, so the base of 5.3 is M_j ≤ A_j(Z). The constant factor e^{c_W} of map (12)(d) pays the one merge in which an insertion can be a non-first member (5.5); 7.1's constants carry it. **Amendment (for review):** the reserve weight of (12)(b) and the lineage extent weight e^{(3/8)ν_e·ext^{coll}} are carried in (ii′)'s per-component totals.
