At production they come from (S-w)'s e^{ν_rσ + ν_e·ext_{k+1}} by the split of 2.1(a), 2.2 and 4.3 (e^{(ν_e/4)ext_{k+1}} pays the distance, e^{(3/4)ν_e·ext_{k+1}} ≥ const·e^{(3/8)ν_e·ext^{coll}}; for 4.3's (γ2) the split is (½, ½), with e^{(ν_e/2)ext_{k+1}} ≥ const·e^{(ν_e/2)ext^{coll}} ≥ const·e^{(3/8)ν_e·ext^{coll}}); (S-w) holds for the coll groups by 2.2's re-run, and for the groups failing only (iv) by §2.1(a)'s "its count does not use (iv)", which is within (S-w)'s scope as SW §1(a1) states it (groups satisfying (i)–(iii)). The extent is measured at the current scale: ext^{(n)}(W) := max_{□∈Y_W}dist(□, Coll_j) in π_n-sides, so ext^{(n)} = ext^{(j)}L^{−(n−j)} and the production weight dominates every later one. **Amendment (for review):** all att bounds are complex-data bounds on 𝒟^{sub}_n (SW §3), over D(n)(iii)'s ω-polydiscs, at production and at every later step; D(n)(iii)'s full domain carries analyticity only (5.4). **Lemma (product form).** For every later step n+1 ≤ N of the lifetime of the component containing Z, on 𝒟^{sub}_{n+1}: summed over the terms of the multilinear expansion at step n+1, the products of the insertions of species att(j, Z) present at step n+1 (W itself at the new evaluation point, and every descendant made at steps j+1, …, n+1 that inherits the species) are bounded by Π_{D∈𝔅_j}(1 + e^{c_W}w(D)·Π_{m=j}^{n}(1+δ_m)) ≤ exp(A_j(Z)Π_{m=j}^{n}(1+δ_m)) ≤ exp(A_j(Z)e^{C_δc̄S_R}) ≤ exp(2A_j(Z)), 𝔅_j the production set, with δ_m := C_δc̄e^{−(25/8)(ν_e/L)R_{m+1}}, c̄ := e^{(3/8)ν_e}c̄_*sup_{m≤N}b_m/ᾱ_m (c̄_* := max(2K_β, C″), (T2) and 5.5), C_δ := 2 and S_R := Σ_{m≤N}e^{−(25/8)(ν_e/L)R_{m+1}}, under C_δc̄S_R ≤ log 2 and L ≥ 16 (the rate (25/8)ν_e/L is the merged insertions' of 5.5, the slower of the two; (25/8)/L ≤ 15/16 for L ≥ 4; L ≥ 16 for 5.5's merge-extent charge). M_n below is Σe^{c_W}w. g_* := sup_{m≤N+1}g_m over the steps used (R_{N+1} enters δ_N). In words: the total mass M_n := Σe^{c_W}w at step n, which is the exponent of this bound, grows at most by the factor 1+δ_n per step; this is the form (ii′) and §7.2 use (Π(1+‖Y‖) ≤ e^{Σ‖Y‖}). The constants do not depend on j, n, N, K, Z, the lifetime or the labelled term. *S_R is small.* CRE (2.5) gives R_m ≥ (log g_m^{−2})^r with r ≥ 2 (p.245, l.34). The running of the coupling: for a compact simple G in d = 4, u_m := g_m^{−2} satisfies the pairwise bound u_m ≥ u_{m′} + b(m′ − m) − C for all m ≤ m′ ≤ K, with b, C > 0 depending on L and G. Source: hypothesis (W) of Balaban\ONE_LOOP_PROOFS.md §2 (l.85–86) on the window [m, m′), supplied for all 0 ≤ m ≤ n by Thm 8.1 (l.722–724), and satisfied by the true flow by the proof of Lemma 2.1, step 4 (l.107–109: "(i) is (W) on [k, K), and (ii) is (W) on [k, l)"); Cor. 8.2 (l.734) is RG I Thm 2 for simple G, proved in the Bałaban project (RG1_Thm2_REP). So any window [X + sb, X + (s+1)b), X := min_mu_m ≥ g_*^{−2}, contains u_m for at most 2 + C/b indices m (two indices m < m′ in it have b(m′−m) − C ≤ u_m − u_{m′} < b), and with f(u) := e^{−(25/8)(ν_e/L)(log u)^r}, decreasing, S_R ≤ (2 + C/b)Σ_{s≥0}f(X + sb) ≤ (2 + C/b)(f(X) + b^{−1}∫_X^∞f(u)du); for u ≥ X with (25/8)(ν_e/L)(log X)^{r−1} ≥ 2, f(u) ≤ u^{−2}, so S_R ≤ (2 + C/b)(e^{−(25/8)(ν_e/L)(log g_*^{−2})^r} + g_*²/b). This uses the lower bound on u_m, i.e. the upper bound on the coupling in D2.0(5)'s terms; it makes this closure a user of Cor. 8.2, the coupling upper bound (D2.0(5)'s list of the upper bound's users includes it). The condition C_δc̄S_R ≤ log 2 is then a smallness condition on g_* alone, after L (c̄ bounded under (R-m′): b_m/ᾱ_m ~ (log g_m^{−2})^{−(q−p₀)}, q the exponent of ᾱ, q > p₀). The range of S_R: δ_N uses R_{N+1}, hence g_{N+1}, so g_* is the supremum over m ≤ N+1 (the couplings whose R_m enter S_R).
*5.1 Evaluation at the new background (no growth).* At the step n → n+1 every carried insertion D is evaluated at the points e^{iη(𝐇_n(s,tB′) + a″)}U_{n+1} of D2.2(4)(e)–(f), a″ ∈ 𝒟^{sub}_{n+1}, and these lie in 𝒟^{sub}_n around the real point U_n(V_n) on the support by (4)(f)'s chain condition (R-m, R-dec on the older layers, SW §3(b3) for the sub-domain; at n = 1 the base point is D2.2(6)(e)'s OPEN item). The att bound of D is a sup bound on 𝒟^{sub}_n (D(n)(iii): "(ii) holds there", and the att species carry no deviation, D2.0(3) l.300), so ‖D‖ at every point reached, in particular at U_{n+1} (t = 0, a″ = 0), is at most its production bound. No use of (β) of D2.0(5)(c) is made: the real-support evaluation that (β) secures is needed for the dev bound (a deviation of the argument), not for a sup bound. This closes the map's "stability under the evaluation at U_{k+1}" for the att species, and D2.0(5)(c)(ε) (the attached parts of old insertions at U_{k+1} keep their species and their bound). **Derived** given (4)(f)'s chain condition with its inputs R-m, R-dec, R-adm and R-cpl₀, the sub-domain amendment of 5.0, and, for ω-dependent members (att, lw, wrap(j)), the ω-polydisc propagation (D2.2(6)(e) *Propagation* and *The slot values at complex backgrounds*, with R-𝐇 and R-𝐇₀) at the ω-radius r_{ω,j} = g_j^{1/2} (§2.2 *Complex σ_k*, an amendment for review); not closed at n = 1, nor at any later n for members whose evaluation domain meets Ω₁∖Ω₂. Their evaluation uses that layer's k = 1 base point: for the j = 0, 1 slot values through the map's *The inclusion*, and for every member through map (6)(e) *Derived instead of assumed* and SW §3(b3), which use the base point for the real-base-point and curvature conditions of every evaluation point on the layer (OPEN, O3; map l.975, l.984); the representative of D2.2(4)(d) (the gauge frame of σ_j) is OPEN there and enters through the evaluation point.
*5.2 The touched part (the fluctuation parts of (5)(d)).* The part of D that the step integrates, ∫dt ∂_tD(e^{iη𝐇_n(s,tB′)}U_{n+1}), with the s-localisation of RG2 (1.9)–(1.10) (RG2 pp.3–4), is a constituent of new groups 𝒢 of kind (β) at step n+1 (D2.4(11)), which take D's species (D2.0(3) l.286: a fluctuation part keeps its parent's species; a merged group takes the first inherited attached species, whatever its (i)/(iii)/(γ) status, §2.1 *Merged groups*). Two facts about such a 𝒢:
- (T0) *Lineage form (a re-run of (12)(e)–(f), accepted by the user).* On a labelled term the words are expanded multilinearly in the species (D2.0(3) *Species*). A term of the expansion at step n+1 fixes the set 𝔅 of att(j, Z)-insertions present at step n, as factors at fixed positions; they are fixed data, not vertices. (12)(e)–(f) are re-run with the vertex set made of the new constituents (the fluctuation parts, the new polymers, the merged new members) and the dev old members; a (β)-vertex that contains the touched part of a fixed att member D carries D's Cauchy factor of (T2), so ε^{new} contains no att ε^{old}, and (12)(e)'s Kotecký–Preiss condition is over these vertices only. *Positions.* (A step-(n+1) traversal can contain L^{n+1−j} disjoint att(j) intervals, the interval I(M) of a vertex can contain a touched member's interval far from the component, and a fixed D's activity and reserve are not in w̃(v).) The positions, int(v) and win(v) of a re-run vertex are defined from its new constituents and dev members only. Every link of a new vertex to a fixed member D (touch, second rule, hull) is a link to a fixed root: the groups containing D are summed rooted at D, as the map's (12)(f) note and 5.5 do. D has at most 163Mσ(D) positions: 81Mσ(D) by map (12)(e), plus the traversals of win(D), at most |win(D)| = |int(D)| + 40(diam_D + 1) ≤ Mσ(D) + 40((M/L)σ(D) + 1) ≤ 82Mσ(D) (map (12)(c): the window extends the interval by 20(diam_D + 1) bonds at each end; |int(D)| ≤ n(D) ≤ Mσ(D), diam_D ≤ (M/L)σ(D) by (12)(d) and σ(D) ≥ 1), charged to b(D) = (ν_r/4)σ(D) (c_D^{chg} := 0 in the charge of a fixed member, distinct from map (11)(a)'s c_D ≤ 81; its c_W in the share is 5.5's). The rooted sum needs (12)(e)–(f)'s Σ_{v′~D}w̃(v′)e^{b(v′)} ≤ b(D)/2 = (ν_r/8)σ(D), which gives T(D) ≤ e^{b(D)/2}. The dev old W- and Γ-vertices of the re-run also link to D at its window and cube positions, and so do the vertices whose window reaches int(D) (map (12)(e): "old v′ whose window reaches int(v): ≤ L·n_{k+1}(M)·C_win(ε^{old}_t + ε^{ref,k}_t)", the same for M′-vertices with ε^{new}_t), at most n(D)C_win ≤ C_winMσ(D) further positions. So the condition is (163 + C_win)M(ε^{new}_{re-run} + ε^{old}_n + ε^{ref,n}) ≤ ν_r/8. It is a smallness of g under (R-m′) (q > p₀). The root factor b(D)e^{b(D)/2} is paid from D's reserve in w(D) (C_b of 5.5). The per-position sum is then the animal sum rooted at the position: a (β)-vertex has activity (C_Tb_n/ᾱ_n)^{n(G)}e^{−(κ_s−ν_e−3)#}, so ε^{new}_{re-run} ≤ C_Mb_n·pl + (C_Tb_n/ᾱ_n)Σ_{animals ∋ p}e^{−(κ_s−ν_e−3)#} ≤ C_Mb_n·pl + K′b_n/ᾱ_n, independent of 𝔅. (12)(e)'s condition over the new vertex set then needs K′b_n/ᾱ_n below its threshold: a smallness of g under (R-m′), q > p₀ (b_n/ᾱ_n ~ (log g_n^{−2})^{−(q−p₀)}). Fixed att members carry no interval-extent shares; a descendant's extent is tracked by the lineage weight. *Product form.* Each new group containing a touched or merged part of some fixed member is assigned to its first such member in D2.0(3)'s order (the first-member injection), and each fixed D has at most one group assigned to it in a term (the hard core). Step 1: for fixed 𝔅, Σ_𝔊[Π_{D∈𝔅 untouched}w(D)·Π_{𝒢∈𝔊}w(𝒢)] ≤ Π_{D∈𝔅}(w(D) + m_D)·exp(new-only), m_D the weighted mass of the group assigned to D with the non-first members' e^{c_W}w(D′) factored into their own slots, and exp(new-only) the bound of the step's own species. Step 2: m_D ≤ δ_n·w(D) by (T2)–(T3) and 5.5, so the sum is ≤ Π_{D∈𝔅}w(D)(1+δ_n)·exp(new-only). Step 3: summing over 𝔅 within the present lineages gives Π_D(1 + e^{c_W}w(D)(1+δ_n)) (5.0's normalisation; the recursion is homogeneous, so the constant e^{c_W} passes through), with w(D) at the new point by 5.1. Step 4: the lineage at step n+1 (D untouched, or its descendant) is again one factor, so the induction iterates; this is the induction step of 5.0's lemma. The map's D(k)(ii′-a) display and its (ii′) stability bullet are restated to this form (map l.290–292). An insertion with several old members (5.5) is counted at its first-species member; the others are data of the term, paid by their own factors in the same product.
- (T1) *Anchoring.* With s(σᶜ) = 0 the propagators vanish across the components of Y(σ) (RG2 p.4), so D depends on B′ only through the components of Y(σ) meeting its dependence set Q_D, and only on B′ restricted to them; supp B′ ⊆ Ω_{n+1} (map D2.1(4)(b); the integrated field of (3.43) is supported in Λ_{n+1} ⊆ Ω_{n+1}, and the weaker Ω_{n+1} is used). If none of these components meets Ω_{n+1} the s-derivatives vanish. For an ω-dependent D the touch can also come through R(w(s))ω on the blocks meeting X_D (D2.2(4)(c)); a block lies within one π_n-cube, so Q_D is taken to include those cubes, and w(s) depends on B′ only through the same components. So every nonzero touched term has a polymer containing a connected union of cubes from Q_D to Ω_{n+1}, paying e^{−(κ_s−3)} per π_n-cube in (11)(c).
- (T2) *The touch factor.* By §4.2 the polydisc radius of every carried D is ≥ T_Y whatever layers D meets, so (11)(c)'s Cauchy estimate gives ‖∂_tD‖ ≤ 2sup‖D‖/T_Y (the factor 2 of the estimate on the half-radius disc), and with (T0)'s sum of the new groups containing D's touched part rooted at D, (2ε^{new}_n/ε_*)C_b·w(D) ≤ C″(b_n/ᾱ_n)w(D) (5.5's computation; the other new members paid by their own weights, (12)(d)), each group 𝒢 carries the bridge factor e^{−κ′N_𝒢} and the touched mass is ≤ c_ne^{−κ′N_𝒢}·(D's weighted mass) per group inside that rooted sum, c_n := c̄_*b_n/ᾱ_n, c̄_* := max(2K_β, C″), where N_𝒢 is the number of π_n-cubes of the bridge of (T1) and κ′ := ν_e. **Amendment (accepted by the user):** e^{−(κ_s−3)#(G_M)} = e^{−ν_e#(G_M)}e^{−(κ_s−ν_e−3)#(G_M)} is split before (11)(d), and (11)(d)–(13) are run with κ_s − ν_e, under κ_s − ν_e ≥ κ_s^{(12)} (which contains (11)(d)'s condition); so e^{−ν_e} per π_n-cube of the new polymer is reserved and not spent by (12)(c)–(e), and κ′ is this reserve. The factors e^{16κ_s} (R-m's inequality, C_T, D2.2(6)(e)) grow by e^{16ν_e}, harmless for q > p₀ and recorded in the equality-case conditions of §8. The descendant's own factors e^{θ_{n+1}(𝒢)} and e^{ν_rσ_{n+1}(𝒢)} are inside (12)(f)'s bound (extended to ω′ ≠ 0 and kind (γ) by §2.2's re-run and §4.2); being of species att(j, Z), it needs no (ii-w) extent weight from the step's traversals (map l.292: those weights are for the dev species), so the ν_e-parts of the shares are free for (T3). What (T3) tracks is the lineage weight, the extent from Coll_j.
- (T3) *The bridge is long.* Z ⊆ Λ_jᶜ ⊆ Λ_nᶜ (Λ_{m+1} ⊆ Ω_{m+1} ⊆ Λ_m: CRE p.265 (3.5), p.269 (3.20)), and Coll_j lies within 6 π_j-sides of Λ_jᶜ, so dist(Ω_{n+1}, Coll_j) ≥ dist(Ω_{n+1}, Λ_nᶜ) − 6L^{−(n−j)} π_n-sides ≥ 6LR_{n+1} − 6 ≥ 5R_{n+1} π_n-sides (R_{n+1} ≥ 6) by (G1) at step n+1 (6 layers of LMR_{n+1}-cubes, each LR_{n+1} π_n-sides since a π_n-side is M in step-n units; the weaker 6R_{n+1} is used). The bridge starts at a cube of Q_D ⊆ Y_D, whose distance from Coll_j is ≤ ext^{(n)}(D), and ends in Ω_{n+1}: ext^{(n)}(D) + N_𝒢 ≥ 5R_{n+1}, N_𝒢 the number of π_n-cubes on a tree path in 𝒢 from Q_D to Ω_{n+1}. *The payment of the path.* (The shares pay ν_e per π_{n+1}-side, not per π_n-cube, so the off-component cubes are not paid from them at e^{−ν_e} per π_n-cube.) For a touched D the bridge is taken inside the (T1) component: by (T1) it lies in one connected union of cubes of the new polymer's components, each π_n-cube of which pays e^{−ν_e} from (T2)'s split; so the N_𝒢 path cubes are π_n-cubes paid at ν_e each. The rest of 𝒢 (cubes of M∖D′, of vacuum links, of merged old domains, of hull gaps) is counted in π_{n+1}-sides by the full ν_e-share E′_𝒢 := Σ_{v∈𝒢}(|win(v)|/M + 2), diam_v in step-(n+1) bonds ((12)(c)): (iv′)-linked vertices lie at most 20(diam_v+1) bonds apart along C ((12)(c)–(b)), so E′_𝒢 bounds the spread of the vertex supports in π_{n+1}-sides, and it is paid at e^{−ν_e} per unit by the ν_e-parts of the shares sh(v) of map (12)(d), which are free here (T2). The supports of the fixed members of 𝒢 are not vertices; a path through the support of a fixed member D″ ∈ 𝔅(𝒢) (D itself or a non-first member) crosses at most diam X_{D″} ≤ (M/L)σ(D″) bonds of step j(D″)+1 (map (12)(d), at D″'s production scale; Mσ(D″) step-n bonds at n = j), at most σ(D″)/L^{n+1−j(D″)} ≤ σ(D″)/L π_{n+1}-sides for every n ≥ j(D″), and a cube of Y_𝒢 inside such a support is reached within the same distance. A cube of Y_𝒢 meeting an arc gap of I(𝒢) next to D″ lies in no support. Every point of I(𝒢) lies in an interval or in a tree-edge gap between members u, u′ linked by (ii)/(iii) ((12)(c) *Links* (3)). On the square C, every point of the shorter arc between b ∈ int(u) and c ∈ int(u′) lies within ℓ^∞-distance |b − c|_∞ ≤ diam_u + diam_{u′} + 1 of b: on one side it lies between them; on adjacent sides the arc turns one corner and every point is within max of the two legs; on opposite sides |b − c|_∞ is at least the side length. So such a gap cube is within 2diam X_{D″} of the point where the path enters X_{D″}, plus terms already in E′_𝒢. Hence the members add at most 2Σ_{D″∈𝔅(𝒢)}σ(D″)/L π_{n+1}-sides. The descendant's own lineage extent, in π_{n+1}-sides, is ext^{(n+1)}(𝒢) ≤ (ext^{(n)}(D) + N_𝒢)/L + E′_𝒢 + 2Σ_{D″∈𝔅(𝒢)}σ(D″)/L + 1. The factor e^{2aσ(D″)/L} ≤ e^{(ν_r/16)σ(D″)} (a = (3/8)ν_e, ν_e ≤ ν_r/2, L ≥ 6) is paid from D″'s reserve inside w(D″), once, at the merge: it is the merge-extent charge of 5.5's reserve budget. So, with a := (3/8)ν_e the carried rate of 5.0, its weighted mass is at most
  e^{(a/L)(ext^{(n)}(D)+N_𝒢) + aE′_𝒢 + a}·c_ne^{−ν_eN_𝒢 − ν_eE′_𝒢}·e^{ν_rσ(D)}e^{θ(D)}‖D‖ ≤ e^{a}c_ne^{−(a/2)(ext^{(n)}(D)+N_𝒢)}·w(D) ≤ e^{a}c_ne^{−(15/16)ν_eR_{n+1}}·w(D),
using L ≥ 2: a/L − a ≤ −a/2, a/L − ν_e ≤ −(13/16)ν_e ≤ −a/2 and a − ν_e < 0, and (a/2)·5R_{n+1} = (15/16)ν_eR_{n+1}. The parent's weight at scale n exceeds the descendant's at scale n+1 by the factor that, with the bridge's reserve, pays 5R_{n+1} π_n-sides. (The polydisc radius T_Y is not reduced by D's layers, §4.2, so no further factor enters.)
*5.3 The lifetime sum.* Let y_{n+1} be the weighted mass (at scale n+1) of the descendants made at step n+1, and M_n the weighted mass present at step n (at scale n). By (T0)–(T3) and 5.5, y_{n+1} ≤ c̄e^{−(15/16)ν_eR_{n+1}}M_n + c̄e^{−(25/8)(ν_e/L)R_{n+1}}M_n ≤ δ_nM_n (the touched descendants by (T3), the merged ones by 5.5; (25/8)/L ≤ 15/16 for L ≥ 4; C_δ = 2). The insertions present at step n+1 are those present at step n, re-evaluated (5.1: no growth of ‖D‖; e^{θ(D)}, e^{ν_rσ(D)} unchanged; the extent weight decreases), plus the new descendants: M_{n+1} ≤ M_n + y_{n+1} ≤ (1+δ_n)M_n, M_j ≤ A_j(Z). Hence M_{n+1} ≤ A_j(Z)Π_{m=j}^{n}(1+δ_m) ≤ A_j(Z)exp(Σ_mδ_m) ≤ A_j(Z)e^{C_δc̄S_R}, the lemma. The lifetime does not enter: the species att(j, Z) persists when Z's region merges into a larger component (the merging is LFR II pp.385–387, (1.84)–(1.88); that the insertions keep their species and their bound is this closure's rule, and the merged component's factor contains Z's, §7.2) and ends when the R-operation removes the component (k+1 > N, D2.7); the sum over the steps of the lifetime is bounded by the full sum S_R, which converges by the running of the coupling (5.0). **Derived** given (T0)'s re-run of (12)(e)–(f) (accepted by the user), (12)(d)–(f) with the split amendment of (T2), R-dec, R-m, R-adm, the sub-domain amendment and (S-w) with the carried weights (for review, 5.0), §2.2's R-src with the ω-radius amendment and §2.3's R-𝐁 and R-fr (Bałaban's) (touched descendants of att members may be ω′-dependent or kind (γ)), and, for the complex-data bounds over the ω-polydiscs that 5.0 consumes, O6 (OPEN), R-dec⁺ (through §2.1 *Merged groups*), R-cpl₀, R-𝐇 and R-𝐇₀ (through 5.1), (T0)'s Kotecký–Preiss smallness K′b_n/ᾱ_n below (12)(e)'s threshold (q > p₀, (R-m′)), §2.1's precedence for merged groups, Cor. 8.2's pairwise running and the smallness conditions C_δc̄S_R ≤ log 2, (25/8)(ν_e/L)(log g_*^{−2})^{r−1} ≥ 2 and L ≥ 16; the base points of O3 enter through 5.1, at n = 1 and at every later n for every member whose evaluation domain meets Ω₁∖Ω₂ (map (6)(e) *Derived instead of assumed* and SW §3(b3) use that layer's base point for every evaluation point on it). No per-step factor remains: a touch at step n+1 of an insertion of species att(j, Z) can only come through supp B′ ⊆ Ω_{n+1}, which is 5R_{n+1} π_n-sides from Coll_j, and the insertion's own extent weight together with the bridge's reserve pays that distance. For the step-1 species the extent is measured from 𝒩₁, the 102ℓ-neighbourhood of Λ₁ᶜ, and the steps before n_B have no bridge gain; the bound is C_lin times the production total (5.6).
*5.4 Older layers' transports (R8).* On 𝒟^{sub}_{k+1} the complex part of the background is bounded by the top-layer radius α_{1,k+1}L^{−(k+1)} per fine bond on every layer, so the phase along a step-(k+1) bond is ≤ α_{1,k+1} on every layer and a transport along an arc of ℓ step-(k+1) traversals has norm ≤ e^{c_πℓα_{1,k+1}}, as D2.4(8) has on Ω_{k+1}; its condition 4c_πMα_{1,k+1} ≤ 1 covers every layer. For a carried group made at step j, the arc I(W) has ℓ_j(W) step-j traversals and ℓ_j(W)L^{−(k+1−j)} step-(k+1) traversals, so the transport factor inside Y_W at step k+1 is at most e^{c_πℓ_j(W)α_{1,k+1}L^{−(k+1−j)}} ≤ its production value e^{c_πℓ_j(W)α_{1,j}} (α_{1,k+1} ≤ (1+β₀)²(k+1−j)^{1/2}α_{1,j} by CRE (2.6)–(2.7), and (k+1−j)^{1/2}L^{−(k+1−j)} ≤ (2(1+β₀)²)^{−1} for L ≥ 2(1+β₀)²): the transports of a carried insertion do not grow. **Derived** on the sub-domain given R-cpl₀ and CRE (2.6)–(2.7) in the form (2.28) of the radii that D2.4(8) uses; on D(k)(iii)'s full domain the phase on the layer m would be L^{k+1−m}α_{1,m} per bond, which is why the att bounds are stated on 𝒟^{sub} (SW §3) and the full domain carries analyticity only.
*5.5 Multi-parent insertions.* A group with several old members (touched or merged by the hard-core rule) is counted once, in the species of its first attached member (first in D2.0(3)'s order of species, then of position); its mass is bounded by the product of its members' bounds times the new factors, which (T0)'s first-member injection charges to its first-species member, the other old members being data of the term (paid by their own factors in the same product) and the new members' weights e^{θ} paid by (12)(d). So the per-species totals of 5.3 are not overcounted. An untouched old insertion W merged into a new group adds mass ≤ w̃(W)F(W) with (12)(f)'s bound for a sum rooted at an old vertex (map l.1571), w̃(W)F(W) ≤ (2ε^{new}_n/ε_*)w̃(W)b(W)e^{b(W)/2}, b(W) = (ν_r/4)σ(W) the charge of a fixed member (no extent part; c_W^{chg} := 0 for fixed members), the factor b(W)e^{b(W)/2} paid from W's reserve weight e^{ν_rσ(W)}; ε^{new}_n is the full new-vertex sum of the re-run (T0), ε^{new}_n ≤ C_Mb_n·pl + K′b_n/ᾱ_n ≤ (K′+1)b_n/ᾱ_n for small g (ᾱ_n = g_n·min(C₀, C₁)(log g_n^{−2})^{q}, so C_Mb_n·pl ≤ b_n/ᾱ_n). So the merged mass is ≤ C″(b_n/ᾱ_n)w(W) before the bridge, C″ := 2(K′+1)C_be^{(5/8)ν_e}/ε_*, where w̃(W)b(W)e^{b(W)/2} ≤ C_be^{−(5/32)ν_rσ(W)}w(W) with C_b := (8/7)e^{c_W−1}. For a fixed member, w̃(W) = |activity|e^{sh(W)} with sh(W) ≤ (ν_r/4)σ_k(W) + c_W ≤ (ν_r/2)σ_k(W) + c_W (no extent share, (T0); map (12)(d)); c_W := c_sh, map (12)(d)'s share constant 2ν_f(16 + 10240/L + 41/M) + 2ν_e(2 + 41/M) (the ℓ_X factor included), the same for every W. Also b(W) = (ν_r/4)σ_k(W) and |activity| ≤ e^{θ}‖Y_W‖. So with x := ν_rσ the product is ≤ e^{c_W}(x/4)e^{(5/8)x}e^{θ}‖Y_W‖ = e^{c_W}(x/4)e^{−(7/32)x}·e^{(27/32)x}e^{θ}‖Y_W‖, and sup_{x≥0}(x/4)e^{−(7/32)x} = (8/7)e^{−1}. *The constant c_W of the other members.* c_W is of order ν, not small against (ν_r/32)σ at small σ. So a non-first member D′ of a group enters with w̃(D′) ≤ e^{c_W}e^{−(ν_r/2)σ(D′)}w(D′) ≤ e^{c_W}w(D′), and its transports, merge-extent charge, occurrence charge and root factor T(D′) ≤ e^{(ν_r/8)σ(D′)} lie inside the freed (ν_r/2)σ (1/16 + 1/16 + 1/32 + 1/8 = 9/32 ≤ 1/2). An insertion is a non-first member of at most one merge: afterwards it is part of the composite, a new insertion of the first member's species with its own weight. So the factor e^{c_W} is taken once per insertion. 5.0 normalises the species mass with it: Σe^{c_W}w ≤ A_j(Z), and the lemma's factors are 1 + e^{c_W}w(D)Π(1+δ_m). *Reserve budget of a fixed member.* Of e^{ν_rσ(W)} in w(W): (5/8)ν_rσ for w̃ and b e^{b/2}, (7/32)ν_rσ for the polynomial, (ν_r/16)σ for the transports, (ν_r/16)σ for the merge extent ((T3)) and (ν_r/32)σ for (12)(e)'s occurrence charge a(W); 5/8 + 7/32 + 1/16 + 1/16 + 1/32 = 1. In a term of the expansion a fixed member is untouched or in exactly one new group (the hard core of (T0)), so each charge is spent at most once per term and step, and the non-first members' factors are paid inside their own w(D′) in the same product, as Step 1 of (T0) writes it. *The bridge of a merged W.* The merging new group depends on B′, supported in Ω_{n+1} (T1), so it meets Ω_{n+1}, and the merged insertion has a path from Y_W to Ω_{n+1} of P ≥ 5R_{n+1} − ext^{(n)}(W) π_n-sides (T3). Off the new polymer's components this path is paid only by the shares, at e^{−ν_e} per π_{n+1}-side, and by the crossings of fixed members. The path runs through vertex supports, spanned by the full share E′_𝒢 of (T3), and possibly through the support of another fixed member D″ ∈ 𝔅(𝒢): a group can join W to the vertex meeting Ω_{n+1} only through D″ (W ~ v₁ ~ D″ ~ v₂). Each fixed member adds at most 2σ(D″)/L π_{n+1}-sides to the group's reach, the allowance of (T3) (a crossing of its support, or a tree-edge gap ending at D″ reached around it), so L(E′_𝒢 + 2Σ_{D″∈𝔅(𝒢)}σ(D″)/L + 1) ≥ P. The fixed-member part is paid at e^{−(5/4)(ν_e/L)σ(D″)} inside D″'s merge-extent charge: 2a/L + (5/4)ν_e/L = 2ν_e/L ≤ ν_r/L ≤ ν_r/16 for L ≥ 16 (ν_e ≤ ν_r/2). The +1 costs the constant factor e^{(5/8)ν_e}, which is in C″ below. The lineage extent at step n+1 is ≤ ext^{(n)}(W)/L + E′_𝒢 + 2Σ_{D″∈𝔅(𝒢)}σ(D″)/L + 1 π_{n+1}-sides ((T3), every fixed member, W included, paid there by its merge-extent charge), so the weight changes by at most e^{a}e^{(a/L − a)ext^{(n)} − (ν_e − a)E′_𝒢 − (5/8)(ν_e/L)Σσ(D″)} ≤ e^{a}e^{(5/8)ν_e}e^{(a/L − a)ext^{(n)} − ((5/8)ν_e/L)(5R_{n+1} − ext^{(n)})} ≤ e^{a}e^{(5/8)ν_e}e^{−(25/8)(ν_e/L)R_{n+1}} for L ≥ 16 (the coefficient of ext^{(n)} is −a(1−1/L) + (5/8)ν_e/L ≤ −(9/32)ν_e + (5/32)ν_e < 0). So the merged part is ≤ e^{a}C″(b_n/ᾱ_n)e^{−(25/8)(ν_e/L)R_{n+1}}w(W) ≤ c̄e^{−(25/8)(ν_e/L)R_{n+1}}w(W), the second term of 5.3's y_{n+1}. *Merges with no new constituent* (map (12)(f), the third class) carry no B′ and need no bridge: they regroup old interaction factors already bounded inside e^{θ} ⊂ w, and add no mass beyond factors paid from the members' reserves: (12)(e)'s occurrence charge a(W) ≤ (ν_r/32)σ(W) per member and the hull-gap extent paid by (T3)'s merge-extent charge. **Derived** given (T0) (accepted by the user), (T1)–(T3), §2.1's precedence for merged groups and L ≥ 16 (for the merge-extent charge, implied by the map's L ≥ L₀ = 5000).
*5.6 Step-1 lineages: the extent from 𝒩₁ (O5(a); every step-1 species). Superseded by WILSON_IT1_O2PRIME_CLOSURE_2026_10_26.md §3 (Z₁ = Λᶜ; the extent is measured from 𝒩₁′, the 2ℓ-neighbourhood of Λᶜ; dist(Ω₂, 𝒩₁′) ≥ 5R₂ π₁-sides by CRE p.264 l.52–56, (3.5) and (2.9), so (T3) and 5.5 run from n = 1 on with 5.0's factor 2; n_B and C_lin unused). The record as it stood:* At j = 1 the band of 6.2(b) matters. V3's de-younged bonds D∖D^{(0)} lie up to 100ℓ from Λ₁ᶜ, so a step-1 member anchored there can be of order R₁M_V/M π₁-sides from Coll₁, and 5.0's carried weight e^{(3/8)ν_e·ext^{coll}} is then e^{cR₁} (M is Bałaban's constant, §1). This concerns lw(Z) (6.4), the att(1, Z) parts decaying from bonds of J(Λ) in the band (IT-1 §3d(4)), and the inputs term of 7.1. Put 𝒩₁ := the 102ℓ-neighbourhood (fine bonds) of Λ₁ᶜ. Every member of a species attached at step 1 to a component Z of Z₁ is anchored at a bond of 𝒩₁: the lw slots by 6.2 and 6.4; the att(1, Z) parts and the inputs term at the bonds assigned to Z, which lie within R_t + c_J ≤ 101ℓ of Λ₁ᶜ (6.2, 7.1; the masses anchored at Ret(Λ) only are attached to no Z, 7.1); the step-1 att(1, coll) marks, which are V3's marks and not collar marks on Coll₁, at their anchors (7.1 *Step-1 marks*). These anchors are points of J_col(Λ), inputs in J(Λ), or target bonds b_W ∈ E(B_ξ) ∪ (I∖D(Λ)) (7.1 rule (b)), each in J(Λ) (V3 L2336, L2978) or in E(B_ξ) = E(Λ₁ᶜ); outside D^{(0)}∖D(Λ) they lie within c_J ≤ 101ℓ of Λ₁ᶜ (6.2). A mark located at b has ext^{𝒩,(1)} ≤ 2ext₁ + 1 + d(b, e(b))/(LM) (triangle inequality as in *Production* below), and the extra weight e^{(3/8)ν_e d(b,e(b))/(LM)} is paid from a quarter of its decay under (3/2)ν_e ≤ μ_xLM (7.1, §8). For the lineages of these species the extent in 5.0's weight is measured from 𝒩₁: ext^{𝒩,(n)}(W) := max_{□∈Y_W}dist(□, 𝒩₁) in π_n-sides (0 if □ meets 𝒩₁), in place of ext^{coll}_1. *Production.* A member W anchored at a bond b ∈ Q_W ∩ 𝒩₁ has ext^{𝒩,(1)}(W) ≤ max_{□∈Y_W}dist(□, b) ≤ 2ext_1(W) + 1 (□ and b both lie within ext_1(W) of a traversal of W), which is the bound 5.0's split uses for ext^{coll}; so e^{(3/4)ν_e·ext_1} ≥ e^{−3ν_e/8}e^{(3/8)ν_e·ext^{𝒩,(1)}}. For lw the weights are 6.4(b)'s. *Steps n ≥ n_B.* 𝒩₁ ⊆ Λ_nᶜ. By (G1) at step n, every point within 6 layers of LM_VR_n-cubes in step-(n−1) units, i.e. within 6L^nM_VR_n fine bonds, of Λ_{n−1}ᶜ lies in Ω_nᶜ ⊆ Λ_nᶜ, and Λ_{n−1}ᶜ ⊇ Λ₁ᶜ (Λ_{m+1} ⊆ Ω_{m+1} ⊆ Λ_m, CRE (3.5) p.265, (3.20) p.269). CRE (2.9) (p.256: R_m ≤ (L+1)(n−m)^{β₀}R_n) gives R₁ ≤ (L+1)(n−1)^{β₀}R_n, so 102LM_VR₁ ≤ 6L^nM_VR_n once L^{n−1} ≥ 17(L+1)(n−1)^{β₀}. Let n_B be the least n ≥ 2 from which this holds for all larger n; it depends on L and β₀ only. For n ≥ n_B, dist(Ω_{n+1}, 𝒩₁) ≥ dist(Ω_{n+1}, Λ_nᶜ) ≥ 6LR_{n+1} ≥ 5R_{n+1} π_n-sides by (G1) at step n+1, so (T3) and 5.5 run verbatim with ext^𝒩, with the factor δ_n. *Steps n < n_B (touches at steps 2, …, n_B).* (T3)'s display without the bridge length gives a touched descendant's weighted mass ≤ e^{a}c_ne^{−(a/2)(ext^{𝒩,(n)}(D)+N_𝒢)}w(D) ≤ c̄w(D) (its first inequality uses only the exponents). 5.5's weight change is ≤ e^{a}e^{(a/L−a)ext^{𝒩,(n)} − (ν_e−a)E′_𝒢} ≤ e^{a}, so the merged part is ≤ c̄w(W). So y_{n+1} ≤ C_δc̄M_n at these steps, and 5.3's product is ≤ (1+C_δc̄)^{n_B}e^{C_δc̄S_R} ≤ 2(1+C_δc̄)^{n_B} =: C_lin, a constant (c̄ is bounded uniformly, 5.0). The descendants' extents obey (T3)'s recursion with ext^𝒩 (the triangle inequality from a cube of Q_D). So the carried total of every step-1 species is ≤ C_lin times its production total, in place of the factor 2 of 5.0's lemma; 7.2 uses C_lin at j = 1. **Derived** given (G1), CRE (2.9), (3.5), (3.20), the inputs of (T3) and 5.5, and the anchoring of the step-1 species named above (6.2, 7.1; att(1, coll) at the anchors of 7.1 *Step-1 marks*), with ℓ = LM_VR₁ (6.2 *Units*; 102ℓ = 102LM_VR₁ above).

## §6. lw(Z) and the label sum (R9)

*6.1 The object.* lw is defined on every labelled term (not on V3's covered event A^fin_cov alone: its complement is not a term of the class, the mark inequality L2449 bounding an indicator in V3's history average, and on covered terms 1_{A^fin_cov} depends on D(Λ) along the whole contour, V3 L2446, L2594), with T_i := U₁(r_i), which is Λ-independent on every term, and T̂_i := U_ξ(m̂)(r_i), V3's contour link at the term's data (IT-1 §3d(1): exposed links are constants of the young integral, inside the words). Three classes of fine contour bonds i: (I) r_i ∈ Ω₁ with its row within 2R_c + 2L of I (V3 Lemma charts, L475, as IT-1 §3d(2)(d) applies it on every term): the row input is m̂ on D(Λ), a_old on I∖D(Λ) (|a_old|_σ < r, its box factor, L2982) and A_e on J (|A_e|_σ < r, Ψ_ξ(A_e) = A_e, collar-coverage (i), (iii) within 3R_c of I; V3 L2491(b): r^Λ_e < r), so the input lies in the row polydisc 𝒰_c with every input bond ≤ 2r, statement (a)'s form holds with the exterior input, T̂_i = e^{iΦ_i}T_i with Φ_i := q_U(Ψ_ξ(m̂ + a_old + A_e))(r_i), and Lemma c5-5-7z's Cauchy estimates hold with V3's constants (§3d(2)(d) *Conclusion*); (II) r_i ∉ Ω₁: the link is V₀(r_i) (U₁ = V₀ on Ω₁ᶜ by CRE (1.12), p.248), "links off Ω₁ equal to their background value" (V3 L2757 as quoted in IT-1 §3d(1)), and the T₁-side link is the pending V₀ there (§3d(1)), so Y_i = 0; (III) r_i ∈ Ω₁ with its row c beyond 2R_c + 2L of I (a far row, V3 L452): its free inputs lie in J (the free variables are a ∈ 𝔤^I and A_e ∈ 𝔤^J, (c-bondwise-shift); by the remark after (c-bondwise-shift), V3 L462, a row with a bond of 𝓔_c within 2R_c+1 of I lies within 2R_c+2L of I, so a far row has no bond of 𝓔_c in I, and E_{Ω,*} = I ⊔ J with D(Λ₁) = I, V3 L2223, L2336), so T̂_i = e^{iΦ_i}U₁(r_i) with Φ_i := q_{U₁}(Ψ_ξ(A_e))(r_i) does not depend on a, m̂, a_old or Λ (I and J are Λ₁'s; V3 L2978: J(Λ) = J ∪ (I∖D(Λ)) differs from J only inside I); the row solution there is φ_c of (B4)(i), analytic on the ball ‖A|_{𝓔_c}‖ < ϑ_*, and the arguments A = g₀𝖢_{U₁}B of the domain of integration lie in the ball of radius ϑ_*/2 (V3 L452, Lemma c2-fibre-smallness(i)–(ii)); the small solution has h(d_c) < ρ_d = 2K_*ϑ_*² (Lemma c2-uniform-chart(ii)). So h(Φ_i) ≤ C_q, a constant depending on L and the group (Φ_i = A − h_Ud_c at r_i, (native-source-local-phase)). This general bound is not small in g₀; at the data of a term it is: by L3742 the class-(III) phase is O(c_Aε₀) (*Phase* below). Such i have an input bond in J ⊆ E(Λ₁ᶜ) within 2L − 1 of r_i, hence lie within 2L of Λ₁ᶜ ⊆ Z₁. The covered event A^fin_cov holds iff classes (II) and (III) are empty and no class-(I) input bond lies in J(Λ) (the inputs are absent, not zero). Map l.279: f_ξ(m̂) − f_1 = Σ_{i=1}^{P}dim(π)^{−1}tr(π(T_1)⋯π(T_{i−1})·Y_iπ(T_i)·π(T̂_{i+1})⋯π(T̂_P)) (ordered products along C), with T_i, T̂_i as above. Class (I): Y_i := π(T̂_i)π(T_i)^{−1} − 1 = e^{iπ(Φ_i)} − 1 carries no background factor (V3 Lemma c5-5-7z(a), L2737, L_n(a) = e^{iq_U(Ψ_ξ(a+A_e))(r_n)}U₁(r_n), q_Z(0) = 0 by (c4-10-2)). Real data: ‖Y_i‖ ≤ C_linkg₀Σ_{b∈𝓕_{r_i}}|input_b|_σ ((a)'s "Consequently" clause along the segment from 0; the clause is stated for derivatives in D(Λ)-directions, and the segment also moves the inputs on I∖D(Λ) and J, which the proof of (a) covers, since it uses only ‖A‖ ≤ ϑ_*/4, and this holds along the whole segment with every input bond ≤ 2r by §3d(2)(d) *Conclusion*, IT-1 l.795) ≤ 2C_link|𝓕|g₀r. Here h ≤ |·|_σ (V3 Remark c5-1-3), so h(a_old,b), h(A_e,b) < r; m̂ is §3j(1)'s shift as amended in 6.4 (*Shift*), |m̂_b|_σ ≤ r/4 at real data. Complex data (U₁ ∈ 𝒟^{sub}_1; the slot at a complex background 𝐔 is the analytic continuation of the real function U₁ ↦ Y_i, i.e. the stencil background 𝐔 and the shift m̂(𝐔), 6.4 *Shift*; a_old and A_e are real data of the term at the evaluation point; their moves over D(1)(iii)'s ω-polydisc are *The ω-polydisc* below): the phase q_Z(a) is jointly analytic in the stencil background Z and a, with ‖D_aq_Z(a)‖ ≤ C_{0,1}g₀ on the joint domain on which V3 states (c4-10-4) (§c4-10-1, L4880–4899: Cauchy estimates from the joint balls ‖Z‖ < 2τ_b, g₀‖𝖢_Za‖ < 2θ₀; V3 prints no radius for the smaller balls; Cauchy's estimates from the joint balls give (c4-10-3)–(c4-10-4) on ‖Z‖ ≤ τ_b, ‖A‖ ≤ θ₀, with constants of L and the group, which is how they are used below). The complex part of the background on 𝒟^{sub}_1 is ≤ α_{1,1}L^{−1} per fine bond (§5.4); in the stencil frame 𝐔 = e^{iηa′}U_Z = e^{iZ′} with ‖Z′ − Z‖ ≤ C|ηa′| (BCH for small arguments), so Z′ moves by ≤ Cα_{1,1}/L, real and imaginary parts. **R-st (derived from V3 Lemma row-locality, §c4-10-1 and Lemma c2-fibre-smallness, L464, L3715–3747, L4880–4899, given the reading of §c4-10-1's radii below (Cauchy estimates from the joint balls give (c4-10-3)–(c4-10-4) on ‖Z‖ ≤ τ_b, ‖A‖ ≤ θ₀)):** every contour row in Ω₁ (classes (I) and (III)), at real and complex backgrounds, lies in the joint domain ‖Z′‖ ≤ τ_b, ‖A‖ ≤ θ₀ in a stencil frame. *Gauge step (V3 L412–430 and Lemma c2-uniform-chart, L3689–3712).* The stencil frame is the site gauge g on 𝔓_c of Lemma c2-fibre-smallness(i)'s proof (V3 L3740; *Real part* below), U₁^g = e^{iZ} on 𝓔_c. Under a site gauge transformation u, h_{U^u} = R_uh_U(R^{(1)}_u)^{−1}, 𝖢_{U^u} = R_u𝖢_UR_{u,*}^{−1} and 𝒩_{U^u}∘R_u = R^{(1)}_u𝒩_U (V3 L430, with (R_uq)(b) = Ad_{u(b₋)}q(b), CRE (1.17), L412), and V3 L3689 states that the bounds of (i)–(iii) transfer under this orthogonal fibre action. So (A, d) ↦ (R_uA, R^{(1)}_ud) carries the row equation d = 𝒩_{U,c}(A − h_Ud) to the row equation at U^u and preserves the ball ‖d‖ ≤ ρ_d; by the uniqueness in (ii), D̃_{U^u,c}(R_uA) = R^{(1)}_uD̃_{U,c}(A) and q_{U^u}(R_uA) = R_uq_U(A). V3 states the intertwinings for torus gauges; g is extended arbitrarily off 𝔓_c, since the row objects depend only on U|_{𝓔_c} (Lemma row-locality), and ‖R_uA‖ = ‖A‖ < ϑ_*. At a complex 𝐔 = e^{iηa′}U₁ (a′ the complex direction, written a′ as in IT-1 §3e to keep A′ for the (1.22) variable; 𝐔^g = e^{iηR_ga′}U₁^g, g the real site gauge of U₁ on 𝔓_c) the original-frame slot is *defined* as R_g^{−1} applied to the stencil-frame expression at Z′(a′); that expression is analytic in a′ on the connected polydisc, since R-st keeps Z′ in the joint domain on all of it, and at real a′ it equals the real function by the real covariance. So it is the unique continuation of the real function a′ ↦ Y_i(e^{iηa′}U₁), and two choices of the real gauge give continuations that agree on the real slice, hence everywhere (identity theorem; §c4-10-1 has no original-frame joint domain). Hence Φ_i = Ad_{g(r_{i,−})}^{−1}Φ_i^{st}, Φ_i^{st} the phase computed in the stencil frame. The Hilbert norms, h and h_ℂ are Ad-invariant, and π(Ad_wX) = π(w)π(X)π(w)^{−1} with π(w) unitary also for complex X, so ‖e^{iπ(Φ_i)} − 1‖ = ‖e^{iπ(Φ_i^{st})} − 1‖. Every bound below is proved in the stencil frame and holds in the original gauge. *Real part.* By Lemma row-locality (V3 L464) the row equation of c, its solution and q_U(B) on 𝓔_c depend on U only through U|_{𝓔_c}, so the stencil frame is taken on 𝓔_c ⊆ 𝔓_c ((regular-stencil-bonds), V3 L340). 𝔓_c is a lifted rectangle of side ≤ 2L+1 ≤ s_𝔓 with both blocks of c in Ω₁^∼ ((B1)(iv)), and the proof of Lemma c2-fibre-smallness(i) (V3 L3740) gives, from (B1)(i), (iii) and Lemma c2-local-gauge, a site gauge on 𝔓_c with phases h(Z_b) ≤ 3c_Gc_hs_𝔓ε₀ on every bond. §c4-10-1 measures backgrounds in "the maximum h-norm on this finite label set" (V3 L4880), so ‖Z‖ ≤ 3c_Gc_hs_𝔓ε₀ ≤ τ_b/2 under the g₀-threshold 3c_Gc_hs_𝔓ε₀ ≤ τ_b/2 (§8; ε₀ = g₀p₀(g₀) → 0). *Complex part.* ‖Z′ − Z‖ ≤ Cα_{1,1}/L ≤ τ_b/2 once Cα_{1,1}/L ≤ τ_b/2 (§8; α_{1,1} ∝ g₁(log g₁^{−2})^{q₁} → 0, map l.113), so ‖Z′‖ ≤ τ_b. *Phase.* Class (I): the argument g₀𝖢_{Z′}(input) has norm ≤ C_𝖢′g₀(C(1+r)+1) < θ₀ under the protected-input g₀-threshold (V3 L4897 prints g₀‖𝖢_Za‖ < θ₀/2 "after reducing the coupling"; the complex inputs are ≤ C(1+r)+1 by (b) below). Class (III): by the proof of Lemma c2-fibre-smallness(ii) (V3 L3742: h(q^{fl}(b)) ≤ c_Gc_h·2O(L²)ε₀ on every bond b of Ω₁, and 𝓔_c has at most n_𝓔 bonds, q^{fl} vanishing on those off Ω₁; the statement L3726 records only the consequence ≤ ϑ_*/4) the real fluctuation phase q^{fl} (V3's A′ of CRE (1.13) at this place, renamed here to keep A′ for the (1.22) variable and the complex direction) has ‖q^{fl}|_{𝓔_c}‖ ≤ c_Aε₀ with c_A := n_𝓔^{1/2}c_Gc_h2O(L²). Then A|_{𝓔_c} = q^{fl}|_{𝓔_c} + h_{U₁}(c)d_c with d_c = 𝒩(q^{fl}) and h(d_c) ≤ ½K_*‖q^{fl}|_{𝓔_c}‖² (Taylor with 𝒩(0) = D𝒩(0) = 0 and Lemma c2-uniform-chart(i), L3742), so ‖A|_{𝓔_c}‖ ≤ c_Aε₀(1 + ½H_*K_*c_Aε₀) ≤ 2c_Aε₀ once H_*K_*c_Aε₀ ≤ 2, which follows from 4K_*H_*ϑ_* < 1 once c_Aε₀ ≤ 8ϑ_*, which is implied by c_Aε₀ ≤ ϑ_*/4, V3 threshold item 6's second condition of (c2-fibre-thresholds) as used at L3742 (no new condition). The complex background moves A = g₀𝖢_{Z′}B by at most a relative C(α_{1,1}/L) (𝖢_Z analytic in Z with bounded first derivative, L4889), so ‖A‖ ≤ 4c_Aε₀ ≤ θ₀ under the g₀-threshold 4c_Aε₀ ≤ θ₀ (§8). Class (I): the mean-value inequality along s ↦ s·input gives h_ℂ(Φ_i) ≤ C_{0,1}g₀Σ_bh_ℂ(input_b), and ‖e^{iπ(X)} − 1‖ ≤ √2C_πh_ℂ(X)e^{√2C_πh_ℂ(X)} ≤ 2√2C_πh_ℂ(X) once √2C_πh_ℂ(X) ≤ log 2 (a g₀-threshold, with g₀(r+1) small for the protected-input condition along the segment, §8). So ‖Y_i‖ ≤ C_link′g₀Σ_{b∈𝓕_{r_i}}h_ℂ(input_b) with C_link′ := max(C_link, 2√2C_πC_{0,1}), and ‖π(T_i)^{−1}‖ never enters. Class (III): by R-st, Φ_i = q_{Z′}(A)(r_i) = (A − h_{Z′}D̃_{Z′}(A))(r_i) is analytic on the joint domain (at a real background D̃ is the row solution φ_c of Lemma c2-fibre-smallness(i), V3 L3723, "the function φ_c = D̃_{U₁,c} of Lemma c2-uniform-chart(ii)", the unique solution with h(d_c) ≤ ρ_d, so the small solution is the one used; at a complex background D̃_{Z′} is its analytic continuation in the stencil background, by §c4-10-1's joint analyticity), with h_ℂ(Φ_i) ≤ ‖A‖ + H′C‖A‖² ≤ 2θ₀ (H′ := sup‖h_Z(c)‖ over the joint domain, finite by §c4-10-1's analyticity; C from ‖D̃_Z(A)‖ ≤ C‖A‖², (c4-10-3)) ≤ ϑ_*/2 ≤ C_q + 1 ((c4-10-3) with k = 0; 2θ₀ ≤ ϑ_*/2, L4881). So ‖Y_i‖ ≤ e^{√2C_π(C_q+1)} + 1 =: C_III, a constant depending on L and the group, at real and complex data. Class (III) slots are Λ-independent and bounded, not small; their cost is in 6.3 and 7.1. Species lw(Z) for the near slots of 6.2, Z the component of Z₁ nearest to i (ties by a fixed order); the retreat slots of 6.2 ride with the label activities (6.5); θ := 0; the words U_j at U_ξ(m̂) expand in the same way (6.5). (The class-(III) bound can be sharpened, since h_ℂ(Φ_i) ≤ ‖A‖ + H′C‖A‖² ≤ 2‖A‖ gives ‖Y_i‖ ≤ 16√2C_πc_Aε₀ at real and complex backgrounds and ‖Y_i‖ ≤ 4√2C_π(4c_Aε₀ + C_𝖢′g₀) on the ω-polydisc (a) below, for g₀ small; 6.3 and 7.1 use the constant C_III, which suffices.) *The ω-polydisc.* §5.0 states the att and lw bounds over D(n)(iii)'s ω-polydiscs, and the lw slots depend on the step-0 outer data: class (I) on a_old and A_e, and on A^{Λ,Ψ} through m̂; class (III) on A_e. On the bonds of (R₁^{∼2} ∩ S₁)^* V3's variable is A′, with A = Ψ_ξA′ through the C^∞ cutoff ω_b (V3 L538), so the slots are not analytic in A′ there. The repair is the convention the map already uses for j ≥ 1 (D2.2(6)(b), map l.813: "Every insertion is a function of the slot variables; ψ_{k+1} only says which values they receive"), extended to j = 0. On S₁^* the outer variable ω₀ is CRE (1.15)'s A = Ψ_ξA′. A′ and the cutoff values ω_b(A′(b)), frozen as real factors at the real configuration, only give its values, and the later steps' rotations R(w) act on these slot values (map l.216). **Amendment (for review; map D(1)(iii) and D2.2(6)(e) l.936 amended):** at j = 0 the ω-clause is in slot variables. With it: (a) class (III): Φ_i = q_{Z′}(A)(r_i) is analytic in A on the joint domain (R-st); on the polydisc |ω₀ − ω₀,₀| < r_{ω,0} = 1 (B-units) the argument A = g₀𝖢_{Z′}B moves by ≤ C_𝖢′g₀, so ‖A‖ ≤ 4c_Aε₀ + C_𝖢′g₀ ≤ θ₀ under the g₀-threshold 4c_Aε₀ + C_𝖢′g₀ ≤ θ₀ of §8, and ‖Y_i‖ ≤ C_III there; (b) class (I): the inputs a_old, A_e move by ≤ 1, and m̂ moves linearly through ℓ_Λ (6.4 *Shift*) by ≤ C_mv, C_mv the sup over b of the ℓ¹ row sum of the linear map ω₀ ↦ m̂_b (finite by 6.3's decay bounds, of order N_nbσ_γ times the H_DJ row sum); with C := max(C_m^ℂϑ_V, 1, C_mv), h_ℂ(input_b) ≤ C(1 + r) + 1 on the polydisc, and the mean-value bound above gives ‖Y_i‖ ≤ C_I″g₀(r+1) with C_I″ := C_link′·(C+2)·max_i#𝓕_{r_i} (this is the class-(I) per-slot bound used throughout: 6.3's lw_1, 6.4(b)'s Ȳ, 6.5's retreat slots, 7.1's lw term and §8 are written with C_I″g₀(r+1), since §5.0 needs the bounds on the polydisc); the protected-input condition holds with C(1+r) + 1 for C(1+r). These are the bounds §5.0 needs for lw(Z), at real and complex backgrounds.
*6.2 Support.* *Units.* Distances are in fine bonds (ℓ^∞ distance of initial sites). ℓ := LM_VR₁ is the side of V3's cubes, the aligned ℓ-cubes of CRE (1.10), (1.20) (V3 L122, L2403, L2407: CRE's M is V3's M_V, fixed before the coupling); these are the MR₁-cubes of LFR II p.384 behind d₁′ and #cubes_1, and §6–§7.1 count in them. M is §1's π-side parameter (M ≥ 2LM₁ + 2R₁M₁, map D2.2(3), with VAR's constant R₁: M is a constant; and LM ≥ 4ℓ_s + 4ρ_L, 6.4 (b)); a π₁-cube has side LM; it enters only the extents and weights of 6.4. w_coll does not enter §6. r is V3's radius parameter. The components carrying d₁′ are the ∗-components X of Z₁ = Λᶜ: CRE (2.3), Z_j = Λ_jᶜ, with the domain that plays CRE's Λ₁ for the later steps, which at the defect step is Λ = Λ₁^fin (IT-1 §3c(4⁗)(d) *The extra label*, l.740; map D2.2(3)(b)); they are the label polymers of IT-1 §3j(6) and of 7.2's ledger. Λᶜ = Λ₁ᶜ ∪ (Λ₁∖Λ₁^{(0)}) ∪ Ret(Λ), Ret(Λ) := Λ₁^{(0)}∖Λ the retreat region of the label (corrected on 26 Oct 2026 from "Z₁ = Λ₁ᶜ"; WILSON_IT1_O2PRIME_CLOSURE_2026_10_26.md §1, for review).
  *Where Y_i can be nonzero.* Class (I): only if some input of the row is nonzero. m̂_b ≠ 0 only if d(b, J₁(Λ)) ≤ R_t (IT-1 §3j(2) and (6) *Range*: R_t ≤ (10/μ_H)log g₀^{−2} + 5ℓ_s(N₀+3) + 2ρ_L), with J₁(Λ) ⊆ J_col(Λ) ⊆ J(Λ) (V3 L2336; in 6.3(c)–(d) the A^{Λ,Ψ}-dependence is through J_col(Λ), and V3 Lemma c5-3-1(b) gives the same bound there); a_old and A_e enter only on J(Λ) = J ∪ (I∖D(Λ)) (V3 L2978). 𝓕_{r_i} lies within 2L+1 of r_i (V3 L2598). So Y_i ≠ 0 forces d(i, J(Λ)) ≤ R_t + 2L + 1. Class (III): within 2L of Λ₁ᶜ (6.1). Class (II): Y_i = 0.
  *Where J(Λ) lies.* (a) J = J(Λ₁) = E_{Ω,*}∖D(Λ₁) has no endpoint in Λ₁ (V3 L2336; a free bond outside D(Λ) has no endpoint in Λ, V3 L2451). (b) A bond of D∖D^{(0)} has an endpoint in Λ₁∖Λ₁^{(0)}, within 100ℓ of the unfilled bad region Q₁′^{∼4} ∪ (B(P₁¹)ᶜ)^{∼5} ∪ R₁′^{∼1} (V3 Lemma c5-3-0(b)). That region is the complement of Λ₁ before CRE's filling: Ω₁ᶜ = Q₁′^{∼2} ∪ (B(P₁¹)ᶜ)^{∼3} (CRE (1.10), p.248), so (Ω₁^{∼−2})ᶜ = Q₁′^{∼4} ∪ (B(P₁¹)ᶜ)^{∼5}, and Λ₁ = Ω₁^{∼−2} ∩ (R₁′^{∼})ᶜ (CRE (1.20), p.251); the filling only removes cubes, so the region lies in Λ₁ᶜ. (c) A bond of D^{(0)}∖D(Λ) has an endpoint in Ret(Λ) := Λ₁^{(0)}∖Λ, the retreat region of the label (V3 Lemma c5-3-1(c): every point of Ret(Λ) is within 102ℓ of x_e for a bond e found bad on the term). So J(Λ) = J ∪ (I∖D(Λ)) is the set of free bonds without an endpoint in Λ (D(Λ) is the set of bonds of I with an endpoint in Λ, V3 L2451), hence J(Λ) ⊆ E(Λᶜ) = E(Z₁), and J₁(Λ) ⊆ J_col(Λ) ⊆ J(Λ) ⊆ E(Z₁) (V3 L2336): the three kinds of free bonds lie in the three parts of Z₁ (J in Λ₁ᶜ, D∖D^{(0)} at Λ₁∖Λ₁^{(0)}, D^{(0)}∖D(Λ) at Ret(Λ)). The constant c_J := 100ℓ + 2, the distance of J(Λ) from Λ₁ᶜ ∪ Ret(Λ), is 0 when measured from Z₁ and is no longer used. Ret(Λ) is a union of chains of Q(e)-blocks (a bad collar bond e of round k lies in J_col(Λ₁^{(k)}), which near earlier retreats is inside Λ₁) and can reach far into Λ₁; it is part of Z₁, and the step-2 construction keeps Ω₂ at least eight layers of LMR₂-cubes from all of Z₁ (CRE p.264 l.52–56: Z₁′ the LMR₂-cover of Z₁, P₂ ⊂ (Z₁′^{∼4})ᶜ, B²(P₂¹) = (P₂′^∼)ᶜ ∩ (Z₁′^{∼5})ᶜ; (3.5) p.265: Ω₂ = (Q₂′^{∼2} ∪ (B²(P₂¹)ᶜ)^{∼3})ᶜ ⊆ (Z₁′^{∼8})ᶜ), so Ret(Λ) ∩ Ω₂ = ∅. The volume and entropy of the chains are paid by the fundamental factor of their component (IT-1 §3f(2) Minor 4: Z₁ = Λᶜ ⊂ ⋃_G G^{∼107}, with the bad cubes as the fourth generator). Consequently a contour bond i has Y_i ≠ 0 only if d(i, Z₁) ≤ R_lw′ := R_t + 2L + 1 ≤ ℓ (the threshold R_t + 2L + 3 ≤ ℓ of §8), every slot with Y_i ≠ 0 has species lw(X), X the component of Z₁ nearest to i (ties by a fixed order), and there is no retreat class: every step-1 anchor of 7.1 lies in Z₁ or within R_t of it, and the counts 205^d of 6.3, 7.1 (102 layers) remain valid upper bounds for the 5^d (two layers) that suffice (WILSON_IT1_O2PRIME_CLOSURE_2026_10_26.md §2–§3, Constants; derived on 26 Oct 2026, for review).
  *Support.* A contour bond i has Y_i ≠ 0 only if d(i, Z₁) ≤ R_lw′ := R_t + 2L + 1 (class (I): an input bond of its row in J(Λ) ⊆ E(Z₁) or within R_t of J₁(Λ) ⊆ E(Z₁), and 𝓕_{r_i} within 2L + 1 of r_i; class (III): within 2L of Λ₁ᶜ ⊆ Z₁; class (II): Y_i = 0). Every such slot has species lw(X), X the component of Z₁ nearest to i (ties by a fixed order), and lies in an ℓ-cube within one layer of X. For small g₀, R_t + 2L + 3 ≤ ℓ (R_t = O(log g₀^{−2}), ℓ ≥ L(log g₁^{−2})^{r_C} with CRE's exponent r_C ≥ 2, CRE (2.5) with r ≥ 2 printed on p.245 (recorded at V3 L3968); r_C is not V3's radius r), so R_lw′ ≤ ℓ. (Before 26 Oct 2026 this paragraph split the slots into near ones, within R_lw := R_t + c_J + 2L + 1 ≤ 101ℓ of Λ₁ᶜ, and retreat ones paid by the label activities; with Z₁ = Λᶜ the retreat class is empty, and the bounds below that use R_lw and 205^d remain valid upper bounds.)
*6.3 Size per component.* *The shift.* By the pointwise form IT-1 §3j(5) at m̂, h(m̂_b) ≤ 2C_mϑ_VrΣ_{e∈J_col(Λ)}e^{−μ_xd(b,e)} for every bond b (ϑ_V is V3's parameter, not §4's ϑ; J_col(Λ) ⊇ J₁(Λ) as in 6.2, Lemma c5-3-1(b) giving the same constants there), and Σ_{e∈J_col(Λ)}e^{−μ_xd(b,e)} ≤ Σ_{D≥0}4(2D+3)⁴e^{−μ_xD} =: C_μ (valid for any integer-valued bond distance). At a complex background 𝐔 the slot uses m̂(𝐔) = −λP_𝐔Σ_{n≤N}K_𝐔ⁿℓ_𝐔 (6.1, 6.4 *Shift*): §3j(1)'s G_Δ, K_Δ, P at H_𝐔, and ℓ_𝐔 := H_{𝐔,DJ}A^{Λ,Ψ}. *Complex bound.* Entry norms are h_ℂ-operator norms (the Hermitian extension of h, so a real entry keeps its norm). For a bond matrix A put ‖A‖_μ := sup_bΣ_{b′}‖A(b,b′)‖e^{μd(b,b′)}; it is submultiplicative (triangle inequality for d) and dominates ‖A‖_{∞→∞}. Put E := H_𝐔 − H_{U₁}. Its entries vanish beyond range ρ_L and have norm ≤ C_eρ₁ (IT-1 §3e(3.12) *Size of δℓ*, l.1107, with C_e the entrywise Cauchy constant of §3e(3.1), l.974), so ‖E‖_{μ_H/2} ≤ C_Eρ₁, C_E := 4(2ρ_L+1)⁴e^{μ_Hρ_L/2}C_e. The entry bound ‖G_Δ(b,b′)‖ ≤ γ⁻¹e^{−μ_Hd(b,b′)} that §3j(1) *Row sums* uses (proof of Lemma c5-1-1) gives ‖G_Δ‖_{μ_H/2} ≤ σ_γ := γ⁻¹N_lat(μ_H/2). (a) *Local inverses.* H_{𝐔,Δ̃} = H_{Δ̃}(1 + G_ΔE_{Δ̃Δ̃}) on Δ̃∩D(Λ), so G_Δ(𝐔) = Σ_{j≥0}(−G_ΔE_{Δ̃Δ̃})^jG_Δ and ‖G_Δ(𝐔)‖_{μ_H/2} ≤ 2σ_γ once σ_γC_Eρ₁ ≤ 1/2 (a coupling threshold, §8; ρ₁ → 0). Each term is a polynomial in entries analytic in the background (§3e(3.1)) and the series converges uniformly, so G_Δ(𝐔) is analytic and is the continuation of the real G_Δ; H_𝐔P_𝐔 = 1 − K_𝐔 holds by §3i(4)'s algebra (*Parametrix*, l.1613–1614). (b) *K.* For y ∈ D(Λ)∖Δ̃, K_Δ(𝐔)(y,z) = −Σ_wH_𝐔(y,w)G_Δ(𝐔)(w,z) with d(w,y) ≤ ρ_L and z ∈ Δ, so d(w,z) ≥ ℓ_s − ρ_L (l.1615). The row sums of H_𝐔 are ≤ m_L + C_Eρ₁ ≤ m_L + 1 ((I2) and ‖E‖_{μ_H/2}), so ‖K_𝐔‖_{∞→∞} ≤ q_s^ℂ := 2N_nb(m_L+1)σ_γe^{−μ_H(ℓ_s−ρ_L)/2}. **Amendment of §3j(1)'s choice of ℓ_s (for review; like that choice, a constant of L and the group fixed before the coupling):** ℓ_s is also so large that e^{μ_Hℓ_s/4}q_s^ℂ ≤ 1/2 (so q_s^ℂ ≤ 1/2; strengthened to e^{μ_Hℓ_s/4}R₀q_s^ℂ ≤ 1/2 in 6.4 (b)). This is possible since N_nb does not grow with ℓ_s (box sides and enlargements scale together, ρ_L ≤ ℓ_s). (c) *Bound.* ‖P_𝐔‖_{∞→∞} ≤ 2N_nbσ_γ, Σ_{n≤N}‖K_𝐔‖ⁿ_{∞→∞} ≤ 2, and h_ℂ(ℓ_𝐔(c)) ≤ (c_ℓ + C_Eρ₁)ϑ_Vr ≤ 2c_ℓϑ_Vr (§3j(3)'s c_ℓ; A^{Λ,Ψ} is real with h < ϑ_Vr on J_col(Λ), Lemma c5-3-1(b)). With λ ≤ 1, h_ℂ(m̂(𝐔)_b) ≤ C_m^ℂϑ_Vr, C_m^ℂ := 8N_nbσ_γc_ℓ, a constant of L and the group, at every background of 𝒟^{sub}_1 (at U₁ too). This is a sup bound; the slot bound below uses no more. (d) *The difference, with decay (for 6.4 *The step's shift convention*).* K_Δ(𝐔)(y,z) = 0 for d(y,z) > 5ℓ_s (§3j(2)), so ‖K_𝐔‖_{μ_x} ≤ e^{5μ_xℓ_s}q_s^ℂ = e^{μ_Hℓ_s/4}q_s^ℂ ≤ 1/2 (μ_x = μ_H/20), and likewise at U₁. From (a), ‖G_Δ(𝐔) − G_Δ‖_{μ_H/2} ≤ 2σ_γ²C_Eρ₁; then ‖P_𝐔 − P‖_{μ_x} ≤ 2N_nbσ_γ²C_Eρ₁ and ‖K_𝐔 − K‖_{μ_x} ≤ C_Kρ₁, C_K := 2N_nbe^{μ_Hℓ_s/4}σ_γC_E(1 + m_Lσ_γ), since (K_𝐔 − K)_Δ = −1_{D∖Δ̃}(EG_Δ(𝐔) + H(G_Δ(𝐔) − G_Δ))χ_Δ. Telescoping, ‖Σ_{n≤N}(K_𝐔ⁿ − Kⁿ)‖_{μ_x} ≤ Σ_nn2^{1−n}C_Kρ₁ ≤ 4C_Kρ₁. ℓ_𝐔 − ℓ = E_{DJ}A^{Λ,Ψ} and ℓ, ℓ_𝐔 vanish beyond ρ_L of J_col(Λ), so h_ℂ(ℓ_𝐔(c)) ≤ 2c_ℓϑ_Vre^{μ_xρ_L}Σ_{e∈J_col(Λ)}e^{−μ_xd(c,e)} and h_ℂ((ℓ_𝐔 − ℓ)(c)) ≤ C_Eρ₁ϑ_Vre^{μ_xρ_L}Σ_ee^{−μ_xd(c,e)}. An operator A maps the decay form Σ_ee^{−μ_xd(·,e)} to ‖A‖_{μ_x} times itself (triangle inequality). So h_ℂ(m̂(𝐔)_b − m̂(U₁)_b) ≤ C_Δρ₁ϑ_VrΣ_{e∈J_col(Λ)}e^{−μ_xd(b,e)}, C_Δ := e^{μ_xρ_L}(8N_nbσ_γ²C_Ec_ℓ + 8N_nbσ_γC_Kc_ℓ + 4N_nbσ_γC_E), a constant of L and the group. No factor (r+1) or κ_ℂ enters from the background continuation; the ω-polydisc at j = 0 adds 6.1(b)'s +1.
  *Per slot.* Class (I): each input bond carries one of m̂, a_old, A_e (D(Λ), I∖D(Λ), J are disjoint), so with |𝓕| := max_i|𝓕_{r_i}| and h ≤ |·|_σ, ‖Y_i‖ ≤ C_link′g₀|𝓕|·max(C_m^ℂϑ_V, 1)r =: C′g₀r at real and complex backgrounds; on the ω-polydisc of D(1)(iii) the bound is C_I″g₀(r+1) (6.1 *The ω-polydisc* (b)), and every use below (lw_1, 6.4(b)'s Ȳ, 6.5's retreat slots, 7.1, §8) is with C_I″g₀(r+1). Class (III): ‖Y_i‖ ≤ C_III (6.1).
  *Count.* The contour has at most c_CM_VR₁ fine bonds per ℓ-cube (map D2.0(5)(f) *Per-step cost*, read in fine bonds; c_C = 4L suffices for the square contour: each of its four sides meets an ℓ-cube in at most ℓ = LM_VR₁ fine bonds). The near slots of Z lie within R_lw ≤ 101ℓ of Z (6.2), so in the ℓ-cubes within 102 layers of Z's cubes, at most 205^d#cubes_1(Z) cubes; class (III) slots lie within 2L of Z, in at most 3^d#cubes_1(Z) cubes. #cubes_1(Z) ≤ 3·2^{d−1}(d₁′(Z)+1) (p.384's inequality at n = j, map (5)(f) *Lifetime sum*; at d₁′ = 0, where p.384 prints no count, it is derived from RG1 p.257's definition, 6.5 *Absorption*). Hence, with 6.4 (b)'s weights W_I (summed over the pieces of a class-(I) slot) and W_III := e^{2^dν_r},
  lw_1(Z) := Σ_{near i→Z}Σ_Xe^{ν_rσ(Y_{i,X})}e^{(3/8)ν_e·ext^𝒩(Y_{i,X})}‖Y_{i,X}‖ ≤ (C_{lw}g₀(r+1) + C_{lw}^{III})M_VR₁(d₁′(Z)+1), C_{lw} := C_I″c_C205^d·3·2^{d−1}W_I, C_{lw}^{III} := C_IIIc_C3^d·3·2^{d−1}W_III,
the weighted production total of lw(Z). C_lw and C_lw^{III} are constants (L, the group, d, V3's constants; for a class-(III) slot X = ∅ and the sum is its one term); the class-(I) part is g₀ times a polylog (r, R₁); the class-(III) part is a polylog (R₁), not small in g₀, and is paid in 7.1–7.2 within p₀ large. **Derived** given §3j(1)–(6) (derived there; its minor repairs not re-reviewed, §3j(7)), IT-1 §3i(4) and §3e(3.1), (3.12) for the complex bound, the amendment of ℓ_s (for review), V3 Lemma c5-5-7z(a), V3 §c4-10-1, V3 L452, R-st (derived, §6.1), map D2.0(5)(f), LFR II p.384 with LFR's d₁′ read as RG1 p.257's linear size (LFR II p.380 l.28), and the g₀-thresholds (6.1's log 2, 6.2's R_t + 2L + 3 ≤ ℓ, C_Eρ₁ ≤ c_ℓ, H′C‖A‖ ≤ 1), listed in §8.
*6.4 Carrying.* *Shift (the clip m̂_b := m̃_b·min{1, r/(4|m̃_b|_σ)} of IT-1 §3j(1) is not analytic in the background, and the touching (§5.2) needs Cauchy estimates through m̂).* **Amendment of IT-1 §3j(1) (for review):** the bondwise clip is replaced by the global rescaling m̂ := λm̃, λ := (r/4)/(r/4 + ε_N/2), a constant. §3j(3) gives |m̃_b − m_b|_σ ≤ c_hh(m̃_b − m_b) ≤ ε_N/2 and (c5-4-1) gives |m_b|_σ ≤ r/4, so |m̃_b|_σ ≤ r/4 + ε_N/2 and |m̂_b|_σ ≤ r/4; |m̂_b − m_b|_σ ≤ (1−λ)|m̃_b|_σ + |m̃_b − m_b|_σ ≤ ε_N/2 + ε_N/2 = ε_N, §3j(3)'s bound; m̂_b = 0 where m̃_b = 0, so §3j(2)'s locality and range stand. The tilt: δ̂ := Hm̂ + ℓ_Λ = λδ_N + (1−λ)ℓ_Λ, with 1 − λ ≤ 2ε_N/r and h(ℓ_Λ(c)) ≤ c_ℓϑr (§3j(3)), so sup_bh(δ̂(b)) ≤ q_s^{N+1}c_ℓϑr + 2c_ℓϑε_N ≤ C_sh′ϑr·q_s^{N+1}, C_sh′ := c_ℓ(1 + 4c_hc_ℓϑγ^{−1}N_lat(μ_H)), the form of §3j(3)'s C_sh, with δ̂ supported within R_N + ρ_L of J₁(Λ) as before (ℓ_Λ within ρ_L). m̂ = −λPΣ_{n≤N}Kⁿℓ_Λ is a finite sum of products of entries of P, K and H_{DJ}, each analytic in the background where the parametrix exists (at complex 𝐔: the local inverses as Neumann series around the real G_Δ, 6.3 *Complex bound* (a), on IT-1 §3i(4) *Parametrix* and the entries of H_𝐔 − H_{U₁} from §3e(3.1), (3.12)). *The step's shift convention.* D(1)(iii) is factorwise: every carried factor is a function of the real background, and its bound at a complex 𝐔 is a bound on its analytic continuation; the identity "integral = sum of the factors' products" is used at real points only. The lw slots are functions of U₁ through m̂(U₁), so at 𝐔 they use m̂(𝐔) (6.1, 6.3). The same holds for every carried t > 0 species: at real U₁ it is built with the centre tm̂(U₁) (§3j(4)), so its continuation has the complex centre tm̂(𝐔). §3j(6) instead bounds, at a complex 𝐔, the representation with the frozen real centre tm̂₀, m̂₀ := m̂(U₁) of the base point (IT-1 l.1757). That representation is analytic on the polydisc and expands the same integral, but its species agree with the real species only at the base point, since the split between the reference Gaussian and the host tilts depends on the centre. The two centres differ by t(m̂(𝐔) − m̂₀), which 6.3 (d) bounds by C_Δρ₁ϑ_Vr in (c5-5-1)'s decay form. The bound of the continued t > 0 species is OPEN (O4, §8). These species lie outside §6; the lw slots do not depend on it. Y_i = e^{iπ(Φ_i)} − 1 depends on (U₁, ω) only: through the background of the row stencil of r_i (within R_loc = 2L+2 of r_i, V3 L660) and through m̂ on 𝓕_{r_i} (within 2L+1 of r_i, V3 L2598). m̂_b depends on Λ and A^{Λ,Ψ} within R_N of b (§3j(2)) and on U₁ within R_N + ρ_L of b (every entry of H, P, K read along a walk depends on U₁ on its entry stencil, within ρ_L = 4L of its bond, V3 L416). So Y_i's dependence set is the R_dep-neighbourhood of i, R_dep := R_t + 6L + 2 (the stencil range of the entries included). The step n → n+1 acts on Y_i by the evaluation at the new background (§5.1: its bound is a sup bound on 𝒟^{sub}_1, which contains the points reached; at n = 1, and at every later n where the evaluation domain of Y_i meets Ω₁∖Ω₂ (§5.1), this uses the base point of D2.2(6)(e), OPEN (O3)) and by the touching (§5.2: ∂_t acts through the stencil background and through m̂(𝐔), both analytic (6.1 with R-st; *Shift* above), with the Cauchy factor of (T2) on R-st's margin and §4.2's radius, and the bridge of (T1) from the dependence set to Ω_{n+1}). The transports around the lw slots of an S-term (6.5: an S-term carries |S| slots, with the transports π(T_i) between them along the contour; π(T̂_i) does not occur in the subset expansion) are those of the slot form, bounded by D2.4(8) on arcs as for every slot; no whole-word norm is used (D2.0(5)(h)). 5.5's merging applies with ‖Y_{i,X}‖ for ‖W‖. So lw(Z) is carried by §5's lemma as an att(1, Z)-type family of the pieces of (b) below, with production total lw_1(Z) (6.3, weighted), the extent measured from 𝒩₁ and the factor C_lin in place of 2 (5.6; (a) below). *(T0)'s count for lw slots.* lw slots have Q = 1 against every insertion and can sit on every fine bond of a traversal. The re-run (T0) (§5.2 *Positions*) uses no compatibility between fixed members: every link to a fixed member D is rooted at D, with at most 81Mσ(D) positions charged to b(D). So each piece Y_{i,X} of an lw slot (*Extent and reserve count* (b) below) is counted there as a fixed member with its own σ; no bound on the number of att intervals through a traversal is used. *Extent and reserve count (O5, for review).* The R₁ of (C-M) is VAR's, fixed with M₁ before the coupling (VAR p.277: "the numbers R₁, M₁ are fixed in such a way that all the results of [3, 5, 6] hold for these numbers"; RG2 p.3: "a power of L satisfying R₁M₁M^{−1} ≤ 1"; map l.1128), so M is Bałaban's constant, not a multiple of CRE's R₁, and neither the extent nor the dependence set of a slot is bounded by a constant in π₁-units: (a) a near slot lies up to R_lw ≈ 101ℓ fine bonds from Λ₁ᶜ (the band of V3's de-younged bonds, 6.2(b)), so its weight e^{(3/8)ν_e·ext^{coll}} is e^{cR₁}; (b) R_dep ≍ (10/μ_H)log g₀^{−2}, so one reserve count for the whole slot is of order (log g₀^{−2})^d. (a) and (b) below treat them.
  (b) *Pieces of a class-(I) slot.* Boxes Δ (sides in [ℓ_s, 2ℓ_s)), Δ̃, Δ̂ are §3j(1)'s, i.e. IT-1 §3i(4)'s with (ℓ_s, ρ_L) for (ℓ₀, R_H). Call two boxes adjacent if their distance is ≤ 5ℓ_s; at most N_adj ≤ 13^d boxes lie within 5ℓ_s of a box, itself included (per coordinate an interval of length < 12ℓ_s meets at most 13 intervals of length ≥ ℓ_s). Start(i) := the boxes Δ₀ with Δ̃₀ ∩ 𝓕_{r_i} ≠ ∅; they are pairwise adjacent (𝓕_{r_i} has diameter ≤ 4L+2 < ℓ_s, as ρ_L = 4L ≤ ℓ_s − 1), so |Start(i)| ≤ N_adj. For s = (s_Δ) ∈ ℂ^{boxes} put K_𝐔(s) := Σ_Δs_ΔK_Δ(𝐔), m̂(s) := −λP_𝐔Σ_{n≤N}K_𝐔(s)ⁿℓ_𝐔 (so m̂(1) = m̂(𝐔)), and F_i(s) := Y_i with m̂(s) in place of m̂ (a_old, A_e and the stencil background unchanged). **Amendment of ℓ_s (for review; it contains 6.3(b)'s and replaces it):** ℓ_s is so large that e^{μ_Hℓ_s/4}R₀q_s^ℂ ≤ 1/2, with R₀ := 1 + 2eN_adje^{2^d+1}, an absolute constant (d = 4). Like §3j(1)'s choice it is a constant of L and the group fixed before M₁, M and the coupling. On the polydisc |s_Δ| ≤ R₀, ‖K_𝐔(s)‖_{∞→∞} ≤ R₀q_s^ℂ ≤ 1/2 (6.3(b)'s q_s^ℂ bounds the box sum with the factor N_nb, and each K_Δ enters with |s_Δ| ≤ R₀), so 6.3(c) holds verbatim for m̂(s): h_ℂ(m̂(s)_b) ≤ C_m^ℂϑ_Vr, and 6.1(b)'s bound on the ω-polydisc. Hence F_i is analytic on the polydisc (a polynomial in s inside the slot's analytic domain with 6.1's protected input) and sup|F_i| ≤ Ȳ := C_I″g₀(r+1), the per-slot class-(I) bound on the ω-polydisc (6.1(b); it contains 6.3's C′g₀r at real and complex backgrounds). By the fundamental theorem of calculus in each s_Δ, Y_i = F_i(1) = Σ_XY_{i,X}, Y_{i,X} := ∫_{[0,1]^X}∂^XF_i(s_X, 0)ds_X, over the (finitely many) box sets X. Cauchy's estimate on circles of radius R₀ − 1 around points of [0, 1] gives ‖Y_{i,X}‖ ≤ Ȳ(R₀−1)^{−|X|}, at real and complex backgrounds (Y_{i,X} is analytic in the background, as F_i is). *Locality.* At s = (s_X, 0), m̂(s)_b for b ∈ 𝓕_{r_i} is a sum of walk terms G_{Δ₀}χ_{Δ₀}K_{Δ₁}⋯K_{Δ_n}ℓ_𝐔 with Δ₀ ∈ Start(i), Δ₁, …, Δ_n ∈ X and consecutive boxes within ℓ_s + ρ_L (§3i(4) *Walks*). If a box of X is not joined to Start(i) through adjacent boxes of X, F_i(s_X, 0) does not depend on its s_Δ and Y_{i,X} = 0. So Y_{i,X} ≠ 0 forces X ∪ Start(i) to be connected. Y_{i,X} depends on the background, on Λ and on A^{Λ,Ψ} only on D_i(X) := (the R_loc-neighbourhood of r_i, which contains the row stencil and 𝓕_{r_i}) ∪ ⋃_{Δ∈X∪Start(i)}(the (ℓ_s + 2ρ_L)-neighbourhood of Δ): G_Δ reads entries on Δ̃, K_Δ on Δ̂ × Δ̃, ℓ_𝐔(c) within ρ_L of c, and each entry reads the background within ρ_L of its bond. A walk of K(s)ⁿ, n ≤ N, has the range of §3j(2), and a box that no such walk from Start(i) reaches does not enter F_i (so Y_{i,X} = 0 if X contains it); hence the nonzero pieces depend on Λ and on the background only within R_dep of i, where Y_i does: 6.2's support statements and 6.5's Λ-dependence of the S-terms (within R_t + 2L + 2 of S) hold for every piece, and 6.5's slot-activity size Π_{i∈S}‖Y_i‖ becomes Π_{i∈S}‖Y_{i,X_i}‖ summed over the X_i. *Reserve and extent.* **Condition (for review):** LM ≥ 4ℓ_s + 4ρ_L, a lower bound on M, admissible because ℓ_s and ρ_L are fixed by L and the group before M₁ and M (V3 L3968: γ = γ_L/2, h_+ = m_L precede M₁, M), as M ≥ 2LM₁ + 2R₁M₁ is. Then each of the at most 1 + |Start(i)| + |X| parts of D_i(X) has diameter < LM and meets at most 2^d π₁-cubes, so σ(Y_{i,X}) := #π₁-cubes meeting D_i(X) ≤ 2^d(1 + N_adj + |X|). The parts form a chain from the stencil part, and the gap between the (ℓ_s + 2ρ_L)-neighbourhoods of two adjacent boxes is ≤ 3ℓ_s − 4ρ_L < LM, so every cube meeting D_i(X) is within 2(|X| + N_adj + 1) π₁-sides of the stencil part. *Count.* The X with |X| = k and X ∪ Start(i) connected number at most C_st(eN_adj)^k, C_st := 2^{N_adj+1}(eN_adj)^{N_adj}: X ∪ Start(i) is a connected set of at most k + N_adj boxes through a fixed box of Start(i), and there are at most 2(eN_adj)^{k+N_adj} of these (the bound (eD)^n for connected n-sets through a fixed vertex of a graph of degree ≤ D); X is then fixed by X ∩ Start(i). With ν_r ≤ 1 and ν_e ≤ 1 (ν_r = ν = V3's η, and 1000ηA_s < ϖ/8 ≤ 1/128 with A_s ≥ 1, V3 Lemma c4-8-2(c) and (c4-8-3); ν_e = ν/(2C_box)) and ext^𝒩 of 5.6 and (a) below (≤ 2(|X| + N_adj + 1), the stencil part lying in 𝒩₁),
  Σ_Xe^{ν_rσ(Y_{i,X})}e^{(3/8)ν_e·ext^𝒩(Y_{i,X})}‖Y_{i,X}‖ ≤ e^{(2^d+1)(N_adj+1)}C_stΣ_{k≥0}(eN_adje^{2^d+1}/(R₀−1))^kȲ = W_IȲ, W_I := 2C_ste^{(2^d+1)(N_adj+1)},
a constant of d. The pieces Y_{i,X} replace Y_i in the multilinear expansion (an S-term of 6.5 chooses one piece per slot). Each is a fixed member of lw(Z) with its own σ, and D_i(X) is its dependence set Q_D in (T1). Class (III) slots do not depend on m̂ (6.1). Their dependence set lies within R_loc + 1 of r_i, in one part of diameter < LM, so σ := 2^d, ext^𝒩 = 0 and the weight is W_III := e^{2^dν_r} ≤ e^{2^d}. The same interpolation localises the m̂-dependence of the carried t > 0 species (§3j(4)); it does not bound their continuation, which is O4.
  (a) *Lineage extent.* The lw lineages are step-1 lineages, so 5.6 applies: their extent is measured from 𝒩₁, the 102ℓ-neighbourhood of Λ₁ᶜ (the stencil part of a near slot lies within R_lw + R_loc ≤ 101ℓ + 2L + 2 ≤ 102ℓ of Λ₁ᶜ, 6.2, hence in 𝒩₁), and the weighted total of lw(Z) is ≤ C_lin·lw_1(Z) at every step, in place of 2lw_1(Z), lw_1 the weighted production total of 6.3 with W_I and W_III. **Derived** given §5, §4.2, D2.4(8), 6.1's complex bounds (with R-st), 6.3's complex bound (IT-1 §3i(4), §3e(3.1), (3.12)), the amendment of §3j(1) above, the amendment of ℓ_s and the condition LM ≥ 4ℓ_s + 4ρ_L, the j = 0 slot convention of 6.1 *The ω-polydisc* (all for review), (G1), CRE (2.9), (3.5), (3.20), and V3 Lemma c4-8-2(c) for ν_r ≤ 1; not closed only through O3 (OPEN) at n = 1.
*6.5 The label sum (IT-1 §3j(0)).* The contour-wide factors are f_ξ(m̂) and the words U_j at U_ξ(m̂). Each is a trace of an ordered product of transports π(T̂_i) (and, for the words, slots); expanding every transport as π(T̂_i) = (1 + Y_i)π(T_i), the ordered product Π_i(1 + Y_i)π(T_i) = Σ_{S⊆C}(the ordered product with Y_i at the positions i ∈ S and 1 elsewhere), so f_ξ(m̂) = Σ_Sf^{(S)} and likewise each word (elementary algebra). The S-term depends on Λ through its class-(I) and retreat slots only (class (III) slots are Λ-independent, 6.1): through m̂_b for b ∈ 𝓕_{r_i} (within 2L+1 of i), each depending on Λ within R_N ≤ R_t (§3j(2)), and through which input bonds of 𝓕_{r_i} lie in D(Λ); so its Λ-dependence lies within R_t + 2L + 2 of S, inside the neighbourhood N_S of S of radius R_dep (the +1 is D(Λ)'s endpoint rule). Its slot-activity size is ≤ Π_{i∈S}‖Y_i‖, with the transports in slot form (6.4; D2.4(8) on arcs, no whole-word norm). On every term T_i = U₁(r_i) (6.1), so the S = ∅ term is f_1 = W_χ(C; U₁), Λ-independent. **Amendment of map l.279 (for review):** its one-insertion form "f_ξ(m̂) − f_1 = Σ_i …" is replaced by this subset expansion; lw slots have Q = 1 against each other and against every other insertion of the word (map *In the family sum* clause amended). So every contour-wide factor is a Λ-independent factor plus a sum of terms each depending on Λ only within N_S. On the term labelled Λ each S-term is a term of D2.0(3)'s multilinear expansion with |S| lw insertions, each attached to its component X of Z₁ = Λᶜ (6.2 *Support*) and bounded per term; no merge of label polymers is made within D2.6 (CRE's later steps act term by term, (3.1) p.264; no R-operation for k ≤ N by R-h), and the Λ-dependence of the pieces within 2ℓ of a second component enters D2.7's joint linking expansion (7.4(b)(ii)) as links, with the factor 2 per class-(I) slot of *Near slots* as their budget (WILSON_IT1_O2PRIME_CLOSURE_2026_10_26.md §5, 26 Oct 2026, for review; before that date this paragraph merged the label polymers meeting N_S into one contour-anchored activity at the cost exp(a_lab·Σ_{i∈S}n_t(i)) ≤ 2^{|S|}, whose acceptance by the later steps was OPEN as O2′). *Near slots.* Σ_SΠ_{i∈S}(2‖Y_i‖ for class (I), ‖Y_i‖ for class (III)) = Π(1 + 2‖Y_i‖)Π(1 + ‖Y_i‖) ≤ e^{2Σ_{(I)}‖Y_i‖ + Σ_{(III)}‖Y_i‖}: the label cost doubles the class-(I) part of lw_1(Z) in §7. *No retreat slots.* With Z₁ = Λᶜ every slot with Y_i ≠ 0 is an lw(X) insertion within R_lw′ ≤ ℓ of its component X (6.2 *Support*), the four retreat-anchored masses of 7.1 are att(1, X), B₁(X), the inputs term, and the exposed-segment term of X, and the per-ℓ-cube factors 2^{#ℓ-cubes(Ret(Λ))}, 4^{#ℓ-cubes(X)} with the share 3·2^dlog 2·(d₁′(X)+1) carved out of κ₁(X) in 7.2 and the condition 4C_I″g₀(r+1)·205^dc_CM_VR₁ ≤ log 2 are no longer needed (7.2's printed condition K₁ ≥ 4(1 + C_X)B₁ + 12·2^dlog 2 is stronger than the K₁ ≥ 4(1 + C_X)B₁ that suffices and stays valid). The useful count for 7.2 remains #ℓ-cubes(X) ≤ 3·2^{d−1}(d₁′(X)+1), from RG1 p.257's linear size in its edge-graph form (an edge meets at most 3·2^{d−1} cubes, p.384's inequality for d′ ≥ 1; at d′ = 0 the tree is a vertex meeting at most 2^d ≤ 3·2^{d−1} cubes). The later steps see the lw(X) insertions as they see att(1, X) (§5 carries them, the bridge (T3), 5.5 holding at every step since Ret(Λ) ∩ Ω₂ = ∅, 6.2), and 7.1–7.2 pay them per X out of κ₁(X); IT-1 §3j(0)'s question how such a factor enters the label sum is answered: within D2.6 the factor enters no sum, the Λ-sum being performed at the R-operation and the final bound (LFR I p.177, LFR II (1.90)–(1.97)), which is D2.7's, where an lw piece whose dependence set meets a second component within 2ℓ is a link of the e_X kind. The retreat bonds are IT-1 §3d(4)'s case D2(d), l.866–869, now anchored in E(Z₁). For the t > 0 species of a group W, §3j(6)'s share e^{ησ(W)/2} pays exp(a_lab n_t(W)) under a_lab(2(R_t+ρ_L)/(LM_V)+3)⁴A_E ≤ η/2 (A_E counts V3's LM_V-cubes). **Derived** given §3g(2), §3j(1)–(6), 6.3–6.4 and WILSON_IT1_O2PRIME_CLOSURE_2026_10_26.md (26 Oct 2026; formerly not closed, resting on O2′; for review). O2 (whether the later steps accept the pair (Λ₁, Λ), IT-1 §3g(2), D2.2(3)(b)) is a separate item.
*Budget.* V3 Lemma c5-5-8 (L2791) gives e^{4νσ}M_Λ(W) ≤ C_pinρ_t, with b̄_W ≤ 2M_Λ(W) and e^{θ°} ≤ e^{νσ/16} (L2806); η = ν (map l.1529). Per unit νσ the t-family groups spend: 1/16 for θ°; 2 for §3j(6) (decay ν, σ-payment ν/2, label share ν/2); ν_r/ν + ν_eC_box/ν for D2.4(12)(b)'s e^{ν_rσ+ν_e·ext}, using ext ≤ C_box(σ+1) (map l.1529, base k = 1), the constant e^{ν_eC_box} going to C_pin; at complex backgrounds, 1/8 for IT-1 §3e(4)'s factor e^{C′𝔰ρ₁} ≤ e^{η𝔰/8} (l.1040: "absorbed by lowering the decay rate to 30η/8"); and the tail rate η_w/η_V of (ii-t). At the map's rates ν_r = ν, ν_e = 2ν/C_box, η_w = (7/8)η_V this is 1/16 + 2 + 3 + 1/8 + 7/8 > 4: overspent. **Amendment (accepted by the user with D2.4(12)(b)):** ν_e := ν/(2C_box) and η_w := (5/16)η_V, ν_r = ν unchanged. Then 1/16 + 2 + 3/2 + 1/8 + 5/16 = 4. *The 1/16 and §3a(6)'s 1/8.* §3a(6) books η/8 for e^{θ°} (IT-1 l.1040: "η/8 on e^{θ°} and 31η/8 on the decay"); V3 L2806 gives e^{θ°} ≤ e^{νσ/16}, so §3a(6)'s booking has slack 1/16, and this ledger uses V3's 1/16. *(iii)'s complex rate.* (iii)'s rate of map (5) at complex backgrounds is 4 − 1/16 − 1/8 = 61/16 with V3's θ°-booking (30/8 with §3a(6)'s); with the weights (3/2) and §3j(6)'s share (2) inside the sum it is 5/16 = η_w/η_V, so (12)(b)'s comparison holds (not below η_w). With §3a(6)'s 1/8 it would be 2/8 < 5/16, which is why V3's 1/16 is used. So IT-1 D1(iii)′ and (3.8) and map D(k)(iii) (δ*^{30}, a*) are to be read at the rate 61/16 (§3e(3.8) re-run with θ° ≤ νσ/16 in place of η/8; the amendment of those three places is recorded here). *Inputs of the ledger.* ext ≤ C_box(𝔰+1) at k = 1 is map l.1531's *Base* (from (E₁), V3 L4583, L2797–2798, C_box ≥ 4A_E); the tail rate per unit νσ is the split of IT-1 §3a(6) as map l.1531 re-runs it, with θ° at V3's 1/16 and the complex 1/8, 𝔰³e^{(1/16 + 1/8 + 2 + 1 + 1/2)η𝔰} ≤ C(j+1)³e^{−(5/16)ηj}e^{4η𝔰} (map l.1531), which uses only that an insertion of tail index j has 𝔰 ≥ j (η_V = η = ν). The ledger is complete at k = 1: map l.1531 lists the users of V3's e^{4νσ} (the reserve and extent weights of (ii-w), §3j(6)'s 2νσ, θ° ≤ νσ/16, the tail), and no other clause of the map or of this closure draws on it. *Consumers of the lowered rates (rechecked).* (13)(a)/(S): ν_eC_box = ν/2 > η_w = (5/16)η_V (map l.1593); the factors e^{η_w} in (S) and w_t ≤ e^{η_w}w_{t+1} only get smaller, and the tail sums over t gain a factor ≤ 6/5 in 1/η_w; (12)(d)'s L₀ = 5000 uses ν_e ≤ ν/8, which holds for C_box ≥ 4 (map l.1531, l.1549); the box reductions (map l.1559, l.1576) give constants C(M/ν_e²), larger by 16, absorbed in ε^{old}_c, ε^{new}_t; (ii-t)'s carry k → k+1 has the coefficient L^{−1}(ρ̃_k/ρ̃_{k+1}) (map l.1357), free of the rates; §5's S_R = Σ_me^{−(25/8)(ν_e/L)R_{m+1}} stays small for small g_* (R_m ≥ (log g_m^{−2})^r, r ≥ 2); §2.1(a)'s c_* = ν_e/8 and 7.1's c are stated with ν_e. Map l.170, l.270, l.336, l.1529–1532, l.1593 and l.1614 carry these rates. **Derived** given §3g(2)'s factorisation of χ_Λ (a reduction, §3g(3)), §3j(1)–(6) with their count-check inputs (LFR II pp.384–385, §3f(2) Minor 4), 6.1's complex bound and the budget amendment; the acceptance of the merged activities is OPEN (O2′).

## §7. The payment (R10, R11)

*7.1 A_j(Z) ≤ e^{c_W}C_A(d_j′(Z)+1) (derived as a count; at complex data over the ω-polydiscs the att(j, Z) and att(j, coll) bounds for j ≥ 2 rest on O6, and the step-1 t > 0 species on O4).* The three totals of map l.383:
- att(j, Z) (and wrap(j)): by (ii′-a), Σ_{W∈att(j,Z)}e^{ν_rσ}e^{(3/8)ν_e·ext^{coll}}e^{θ}‖Y_W‖ ≤ Σ_nCρ̂_je^{−cd^Z_j(n)/M} over the step-j traversals n (the carried weights inside, 5.0; c := min(c^{(1)}, c_*′) (c_*′ ≤ c_*, §2.2: one rate in every N_j(·), as map (5)(f) states it) with c^{(1)} the step-1 rate of V3's totals (entering at j = 1 only) and c_* = ν_e/8 the rate of §2.1(a), §3 and §4.3; each insertion counted at its first traversal). A traversal with d^Z_j(n) ∈ [D, D+1) lies within D+1 of Z; the contour has at most c_CMR_j step-j bonds, hence traversals, per MR_j-cube, and the (D+1)-neighbourhood of Z has at most (2(D+1)/(MR_j)+5)^d#cubes_j(Z) cubes. *At j = 1.* The att(1, Z) parts decay from the target set E(B_ξ) ∪ (I∖D(Λ)) of IT-1 §3d(4), E(B_ξ) = E(Λ₁ᶜ) (V3 L2835). Its bonds outside D^{(0)}∖D(Λ) lie in Λ₁ᶜ or within c_J of it (6.2(a)–(b)); those assigned to Z, with Z's own bonds, lie in the set 𝔅_Z of the inputs bullet below, within 101ℓ of Z, so at j = 1 d^Z_1(n) := d(n, 𝔅_Z), and the (D+1)-neighbourhood of 𝔅_Z has at most (2(D+1)/(MR₁)+5)^d·205^d#cubes₁(Z) cubes: a_1 at j = 1 carries the extra constant factor 205^d. So Σ_ne^{−cd^Z_j(n)/M} ≤ c_CMR_j#cubes_j(Z)Σ_{D≥0}(2(D+1)/(MR_j)+5)^de^{−cD/M} ≤ c_CC_cM²R_j#cubes_j(Z), C_c depending on c and d (the cube side is M_VR_j and the decay length is the π-side M; in general the sum is ≤ c_CM_VR_j#cubes_j(Z)·C_cM(1 + M/(M_VR_j))^d, which is the displayed bound for M = M_V and a constant times M_VMR_j in any case, since M and M_V are constants), and Cρ̂_jN_j(Z) ≤ a_1(d_j′(Z)+1), a_1 := Cc_CC_cM²R_jρ̂_j·3·2^{d−1} (times 205^d at j = 1).
- att(j, coll): B_j(Z) ≤ C_Bρ_{t,j}R_j^d(d_j′(Z)+1) for j ≥ 2 (§2.2, the 0/1 collar marks on Coll_j). At j = 1 the species are V3's marks (closure §1), and §2.2 does not apply. B₁(Z) is the total of the marks anchored at Z by the anchor rule of the inputs bullet's *Step-1 marks*. Their anchors lie within c_J ≤ 101ℓ of Z, in at most 205^d#cubes₁(Z) ℓ-cubes, each carrying ≤ Cρ_{t,1}(r^{d_G}+1)ℓ^d·c_CC_FLM. So B₁(Z) ≤ C_{B,1}(d₁′(Z)+1), C_{B,1} := Cρ_{t,1}(r^{d_G}+1)ℓ^dc_CC_FLM·205^d·3·2^{d−1}, which is g₀ times a polylog (ρ_{t,1} = C′_χg₀(1+2r)/u_*).
- lw: [j = 1]·(2C_{lw}g₀(r+1) + C_{lw}^{III})M_VR₁(d₁′(Z)+1) (§6.3's weighted production total, the class-(I) part doubled by §6.5's label factor). The class-(III) part is a polylog, not small in g₀; the retreat slots cost nothing here (6.5).
- the inputs term of (ii′-b) at j = 1 (map (5)(f)'s display: the ω′-translation of the step-1 insertions, Cρ_{t,1}C′_χg₁Σ_{b→Z}h(m̂_b)N_1(b), N_1(b) := Σ_ne^{−cd(b,n)/M} over the traversals): the att bounds are complex-data bounds on 𝒟^{sub} (§5.0), so the sup is §6.3's complex one, sup_bh_ℂ(m̂(𝐔)_b) ≤ C_m^ℂϑ_Vr (6.3 *Complex bound*; no factor (r+1)κ_ℂ). b → Z is the nearest-component assignment of 2.0's marks (each b to the component of Z₁ nearest to it, ties by a fixed order), for the b within R_t + c_J ≤ 101ℓ of Z₁ (6.2); h(m̂_b) ≠ 0 only within R_t of J₁(Λ) at real data and of J_col(Λ) at complex data (6.3(c)–(d)), so in both cases within R_t + c_J of Λ₁ᶜ ∪ Ret(Λ) = Λᶜ = Z₁ (6.2 *Units*; J_col(Λ) ⊆ J(Λ)). *One anchor per term.* (a) The inputs term is exactly a sum of pieces, one per pair (W, b), all those of an input b anchored at b. Its source difference is ∫₀¹∂_s(·)ds along the shift sm̂, and by the chain rule ∂_s of each s-dependent word (the link word at the shifted argument and the fixed factor N_s of V3's source slot, L2783) is Σ_bh(m̂_b)∂_{m̂_b/h(m̂_b),b}(·). So the (W, b)-th piece obeys V3 Lemma c5-5-7z(b)'s second assertion for the link-word part, and its first assertion with the fixed insertion N′_{s,b} := −π(P^s)^{−1}∂_{e_b,b}π(T_W)(sm̂)π(P^s)^{−1}π(h), e_b := m̂_b/h(m̂_b), whose norm (a) bounds by N_trC_linkg₀ (V3 L2744–2747), ≤ u_t^{|𝖩|}C′_χg₀h(m̂_b). It is anchored at b, at the component b → Z (d(b, Z₁) ≤ R_t + c_J holds, since Ret(Λ) ⊂ Z₁ = Λᶜ; no retreat-anchored case remains). (b) A group W that is not good and whose Q_W lifts (IT-1 §3a(4)(i), §3d(4); a Q_W that does not lift gives wrap(1), §1) has a target bond, of E(B_ξ) ∪ (I∖D(Λ)), with an endpoint in Q_W: a bond of E(B_ξ) at a vertex of B_ξ = Λ₁ᶜ in Q_W when Q_W ⊄ Λ₁ (§3a(4)(i)), or else the free input b ∉ D(Λ) of §3d(4), which lies in Q_W. Its background part is anchored at the first such bond b_W in a fixed order. Q_W contains Γ_n for every n ∈ ℋ(W) (V3 L5024), so d(n, b_W) ≤ diam Q_W + 1 ≤ C_boxLM𝔰(W) + 1. This lower bound on 𝔰(W) is the only input of §3d(4)'s per-traversal bound (§3a(6)'s *Other groups* step uses only 𝔰 ≥ s_n), which therefore holds in the weighted form Σ_{W∋n, lifting}K_χe^{θ°}𝔡⁰_We^{c(d(n,b_W)−1)/(LM)} ≤ Cρ̂; so for every bond set B the sum restricted to b_W ∈ B is ≤ Cρ̂e^{−c(d(n,B)−1)/(LM)}, and B = 𝔅_Z gives the first bullet's d^Z_1(n). b_W is booked by the rule of the booking paragraph below: every anchor goes to the nearest component of Z₁ = Λᶜ (an anchor in D^{(0)}∖D(Λ) has an endpoint in Ret(Λ) ⊂ Z₁). *The four step-1 masses anchored in Ret(Λ).* Since Z₁ = Λᶜ ⊇ Ret(Λ) (6.2 *Units*), the four step-1 masses anchored in Ret(Λ) are attached to the component X of Z₁ containing their anchor, as att(1, X), B₁(X), the inputs term, and the exposed-segment term of X (WILSON_IT1_O2PRIME_CLOSURE_2026_10_26.md §4): the att parts anchored by rule (b) at bonds b_W ∈ D^{(0)}∖D(Λ) (IT-1 §3d(4), case D2(d), l.866–869), the E^∂ cluster terms meeting D(Λ)ᶜ only in Ret(Λ), the inputs term's pieces (rule (a)) at inputs b within R_t + c_J of Ret(Λ) only, and the step-1 marks anchored in D^{(0)}∖D(Λ). *Step-1 marks.* The step-1 att(1, coll) species are V3's marks (closure §1; IT-1 §3a(7)–(8)):
  - bond marks 𝔪_b ≤ 2C_ϖr^{d_G}Σ_{e∈J₁(Λ)}e^{−μ_xd(b,e)} (V3 L2713, at the rate μ_x of IT-1 §3j(5) at m̂);
  - host marks equal to the constant m̄_h := 2ω̄̂e^{ω̄̂}e^{κ(B_h+4)}e^{1/8}/ω_* (ω̄̂ ≤ 1 of IT-1 §3j(4)(c) at m̂, in place of V3's ω̄) when a near primitive of the host has an input in J(Λ), and of the h(m_b) form, with prefactor C′_bdg₀(1+3r)², otherwise (V3 L2716–2718; IT-1 §3a(8));
  - the tilt marks 𝔪^{δ̂}_Y ≤ Ĉ^δ_bd rΣ_{b:Y_b=Y}h(δ̂(b)) and the complex δℓ tilt marks (IT-1 §3j(4)–(6)), which have the host-mark form; the real tilt marks are nonzero only for hosts within R_N + ρ_L of J₁(Λ) (the complex δℓ tilt marks are not so restricted; neither support statement is used below).

  Each mark term is one insertion (V3's telescoping gives one differenced factor per term). So it gets one anchor, and its whole weight is carried by one species' rule. *Host cutoff parts.* V3 bounds a host term's cutoff part (1−Θ⁰_{E(Y)})∫𝐏₀e^{t_Y𝐏₀} through the inequality 1−Θ⁰_{E(Y)} ≤ Σ_{b∈E(Y)}(1−χ⁰_b) (V3 L2732), which is not a decomposition. Here it is telescoped exactly: with E(Y) = {b_1, …, b_m} in a fixed order, 1 − Π_kχ⁰_{b_k} = Σ_k(Π_{i<k}χ⁰_{b_i})(1−χ⁰_{b_k}), and on the host term's support χ¹_{b_k} = 1, so the k-th piece is ≤ 𝟏_{S_{b_k}}(B_{b_k}), V3's per-bond bound. The k-th piece is a separate term, anchored at e(b_k); with ≤ N_host hosts per bond (V3 L2633, L2732) the pieces at b total ≤ ϖ_b, the host share of 𝔪_b = 2ϖ_b. No constant changes.
  - A bond mark at b is anchored at a nearest point e(b) of J_col(Λ) (ties by a fixed order; at complex data m̂(𝐔) is supported near J_col(Λ) and m̂(𝐔) − m̂(U₁) decays from J_col(Λ), 6.3(c)–(d), so a J₁-anchored bound does not hold there; J₁(Λ) ⊆ J_col(Λ), so the J_col form holds at real data too, and the host-cutoff pieces below, anchored at e(b_k), move with it; the bond-mark bound at complex data in the J_col form is part of O4, as 7.1's label states). Since Σ_{e∈J_col(Λ)}e^{−μ_xd(b,e)} ≤ C_{μ/2}e^{−(μ_x/2)d(b,e(b))}, with C_{μ/2} := sup_bΣ_ee^{−(μ_x/2)d(b,e)} (e over all bonds, so the same constant serves J₁(Λ) and J_col(Λ)), a quarter of the decay pays 5.6's extent weight from the anchor, e^{(3/8)ν_e d(b,e(b))/(LM)} ≤ e^{(μ_x/4)d(b,e(b))} under (3/2)ν_e ≤ μ_xLM. The bond marks anchored at one e then total ≤ 2C_ϖr^{d_G}C_{μ/2}C_{μ/4}, C_{μ/4} := sup_eΣ_be^{−(μ_x/4)d(b,e)}.
  - *Host marks (a host has side up to 4M, V3 L2827).* A constant host mark is anchored at a nearest input in J(Λ) of its near primitives (ties by the fixed order). At most N_host = 4⁴·20⁴ hosts share such an anchor (V3 L2633), and the host lies within 4M + c_st of it (c_st the stencil range), i.e. within (4M + c_st)/(LM) + 1 ≤ 3 π₁-sides, so 5.6's extent weight costs the constant e^{3ν_e}.
