An h-form host mark is ≤ C′_bdg₀(1+3r)²Σ_{b∈E(Y)}h(m̂_b) ≤ C′_bdg₀(1+3r)²·2C_mϑrΣ_{b∈E(Y)}Σ_{e∈J₁(Λ)}e^{−μ_xd(b,e)} (IT-1 §3j(5), pointwise form); a real tilt mark has the same form with Ĉ^δ_bdC_sh′e^{3μ_Hρ_L/20}ϑg₀r² (IT-1 §3j(5) *real tilt*, (4), with 6.4 *Shift*'s C_sh′); a complex δℓ tilt mark has it with C^δ_bdρ₁ϑr² and with J_col(Λ) in place of J₁(Λ), 𝔪^δ_Y ≤ C^δ_bdρ₁ϑr²Σ_{b:Y_b=Y}Σ_{e∈J_col(Λ)}e^{−μ_xd(b,e)}, with C^δ_bd built from C_δ at (2C_m, μ_x) (IT-1 §3j(6) *Thresholds*, l.1757: at m̂₀ "each of the two difference terms has (c5-5-1)'s form at rate μ_x and carries ρ₁; C_δ is taken with (2C_m, μ_x) for them"; its A^{Λ,Ψ}-dependence is through J_col(Λ), 6.3(d)). This is the mark of the frozen-centre representation m̂₀ = m̂(U₁) of IT-1 §3j(6); if O4 closes by its stated route (a host tilt at the centre tm̂(𝐔)), this term of C_h and K₁'s condition (7.2) are to be re-checked. J₁(Λ) ⊆ J_col(Λ) ⊆ J(Λ) (V3 L2336), so each J₁ form implies the J_col form. *One anchor per host term.* A host's smooth part is one term, and its bound is the sum of the h-form, real tilt and complex δℓ tilt forms. All three are taken in the J_col form, and the term is anchored at one nearest point e(Y) of J_col(Λ), ties by a fixed order. It is paid exactly as the bond marks: d(b, e) ≥ d(Y, e(Y)) for b ∈ E(Y), so μ_x/2 gives C_{μ/2}e^{−(μ_x/2)d(Y,e(Y))}; μ_x/4 pays the extent, max_{□∈Y}dist(□, e(Y)) ≤ d(Y, e(Y)) + 4M, with e^{(3/8)ν_e(d(Y,e(Y)) + 4M)/(LM)} ≤ e^{3ν_e/(2L)}e^{(μ_x/4)d(Y,e(Y))}; μ_x/4 pays the sum over the hosts with a given anchor, at most N_host per bond, ≤ N_hostC_{μ/4}. So the h-form and tilt marks anchored at one point total ≤ C_h := (C′_bdg₀(1+3r)²·2C_mϑr + Ĉ^δ_bdC_sh′e^{3μ_Hρ_L/20}ϑg₀r² + C^δ_bdρ₁ϑr²)·max_Y|E(Y)|·N_hostC_{μ/2}C_{μ/4}e^{ν_e}, g₀ times a polylog. J_col(Λ) ⊆ J(Λ), so 6.2(a)–(c) and the booking below hold for these anchors unchanged. The constant host marks anchored at one input total ≤ N_hostm̄_he^{3ν_e}.

  By V3 L2336 and L2978, J₁(Λ) ⊆ J(Λ) = J ∪ (I∖D(Λ)). By 6.2(a)–(c), an anchor outside D^{(0)}∖D(Λ) lies in Λ₁ᶜ or within c_J of it, while one in D^{(0)}∖D(Λ) has an endpoint in Ret(Λ). Since Ret(Λ) ⊂ Z₁ = Λᶜ (6.2 *Units*), every mark, the ones anchored in D^{(0)}∖D(Λ) included, is the att(1, coll) species of the component of Z₁ nearest to its anchor (2.0's rule), counted in B₁(Z) (second bullet). No term is split. The per-ℓ-cube count of the masses anchored in Ret(Λ), which B₁(X) and the other three terms of X absorb, is the following (Q an ℓ-cube of Ret(Λ), each anchor assigned to the nearest Q, ties by a fixed order). Per Q: the att parts are ≤ Cρ̂₁c_CC_cM²R₁ (the first bullet's decay count with #cubes = 1); the E^∂ terms are ≤ (ℓ/ℓ₀+2)⁴C_rτ² per ℓ-cube (§3i(7)'s rooted form at step 1, which is the step-1 bound; the 7.4(a) form m₁ is the form of the steps k+1 ≥ 2); the inputs term is ≤ C_ing₀ϑ_VrM_VR₁ (this bullet's count with #cubes = 1); the marks anchored in Q are ≤ Cρ_{t,1}(2C_ϖr^{d_G}C_{μ/2}C_{μ/4} + N_hostm̄_he^{3ν_e} + C_h)#E(Q)·c_CC_FLM ≤ Cρ_{t,1}(r^{d_G} + 1)ℓ^d·c_CC_FLM. Here #E(Q) ≤ 2dℓ^d counts the possible anchors in Q, bonds with an endpoint in Q (the factor 2 is inside the C), and C_h (Host marks above) bounds the h-form and tilt marks per anchor, g₀ times a polylog, ≤ 1 for small g₀ (the same per-cube bound holds for the marks anchored at Z, second bullet). m̄_h is the constant host mark, N₁(Δ) is bounded by the displayed c_CC_FLM (the first bullet's decay count would give c_CC_cM²R₁ instead; either constant suffices), and ρ_{t,1} = C′_χg₀(1+2r)/u_* (map D(k)(ii)). Each is g₀ or τ² times a polylog. Each is g₀ or τ² times a polylog per ℓ-cube, inside the per-component terms att(1, X), B₁(X), the inputs term, and the exposed-segment term of X that 7.2 pays out of κ₁(X); their later-step carry is §5's, as for every att(1, X) species (the separate factor 2 per ℓ-cube, its merge with the label activity into 4^{#ℓ-cubes(X)}, and the share 3·2^dlog 2·(d₁′(X)+1) of κ₁(X) are no longer used, l.160). So the set 𝔅_Z := {b : d(b, Z₁) ≤ R_t + c_J and Z is the component of Z₁ nearest to b (ties by the fixed order)} ∪ E(Z) of the bonds assigned to Z, with Z's own bonds, lies in the 101ℓ-neighbourhood of Z, at most 205^d#cubes_1(Z) ℓ-cubes. With the swapped sum Σ_nΣ_{b∈𝔅_Z}e^{−cd(b,n)/M} ≤ C_dM^dΣ_ne^{−(c/2)d(n,𝔅_Z)/M} and the decay count of the first bullet applied to that neighbourhood, the term is ≤ C_in g₀ϑ_VrM_VR₁(d₁′(Z)+1) with C_in := C·C_m^ℂC′_χ·C_dM^d·c_CC_{c/2}M·205^d·3·2^{d−1} ∝ M^{d+1} (ρ_{t,1}g₁ ≤ g₀ for small g). For j ≥ 2 the inputs term is absent: §2.2 bounds the ω′-dependence pointwise on its support, with no translation; this rests on §2.2's reading R-src. Map (5)(f)'s display is amended to list the term at j = 1 only.
With 5.0's normalisation the bullets enter multiplied by e^{c_W}, a constant; below C_A(j) is written for the bullets' sum and e^{c_W}C_A(j) is meant wherever A_j(Z) is bounded. The factor covers every species 5.0 normalises: att(j, Z), the att(j, coll) marks (B_j(Z), C_{B,1}) and lw(Z) (6.4's total, 5.6's step-1 production), all of which enter C_A(j). So A_j(Z) ≤ e^{c_W}C_A(j)(d_j′(Z)+1) with C_A(j) := a_1 + [j ≥ 2]C_Bρ_{t,j}R_j^d + [j = 1](C_{B,1} + (2C_{lw}g₀(r+1) + C_inϑ_Vg₀r + C_{lw}^{III})M_VR₁) (C_{B,1} of the second bullet; C_I″g₀(r+1) in C_lw). For j ≥ 2 each term is g_j or g_j² times powers of R_j and log g_j^{−2}; at j = 1 the class-(III) term C_lw^{III}M_VR₁ is a polylog without a small factor (6.3). The later steps n of the lifetime use C̄_A := e^{c_W}sup_{j≤n≤K}C_A(n), so A_n(·) ≤ C̄_A(d_n′(·)+1) for j ≤ n ≤ K: R_n ≤ LR_j for n ≥ j (p.385 l.9 uses R_n^{d+1} ≤ L^{d+1}R_j^{d+1} over the lifetime), so C̄_A is g_* times powers of LR_j for j ≥ 2, and a constant times M_VLR₁ plus such terms for j = 1 (R_j ≤ (log g_j^{−2})^{r′} by CRE (2.5)). The comparisons the ledgers use: ε_A(j+1) ≤ K_{j+1}/8 = γ₀A₁²p₀²(g_{j+1})/(32(14)^d) (7.3) for small g_*; ε_A(1) ≤ K₁/(8C_X) (7.2, the c₄ form, per label polymer), where ε_A(1) is a polylog of degree ≤ 2r′ + 1 in log g₁^{−2} plus g₀ × polylog terms (C_{B,1}, the inputs term, the class-(I) part of lw; these tend to 0) against K₁ ∝ (log g₁^{−2})^{2p₀}, so it holds for small g₁ under the condition of p.386 and (1.88) (p₀(g), not its square, beats R^{d+2}, i.e. p₀ > r′(d+2), which gives 2p₀ > 2r′ + 1; p.385's own condition is in p₀²). *Which M.* B₁, B_{j+1}, B_j(Z) and the MR_j-cubes use Bałaban's M = M_V (the π-side M below is the same constant), fixed before the coupling (CRE p.245; §6 counts in the same ℓ-cubes, 6.2). The π-side M is Bałaban's constant M as well: the R₁, M₁ of (C-M) are VAR's, fixed before the coupling (VAR p.277; RG2 p.3), so M ≥ 2LM₁ + 2R₁M₁ is a condition on constants, not a polylog in g₁. V3's order admits these lower bounds on M = M_V: M is chosen after L, the group, M₁ and M₂, as an odd power of L that is large, as CRE p.245 asks (V3 L3967). So M ≥ 2LM₁ + 2R₁M₁, LM ≥ 4ℓ_s + 4ρ_L (ℓ_s fixed before M₁ and M, §8), M ≥ 4LM₂ (implied by V3's M ≥ 8LM₂) and LM ≥ 5/(2μ_H) can be imposed there. 7.4(a)'s ratio M/M_V is then 1. *Names.* B₁ (no argument) is the ledger constant of 7.2, B_j(Z) the att(j, coll) total of the second bullet, and 𝔅_Z the bond set of the inputs bullet. So a_1, C_B, m_n, C_in and the shell factors are constants times powers of R_j, every comparison is a polylog of fixed degree against powers of log g^{−2} of order 2p₀, and the comparisons at j ≥ 2 are uniform in K. For §6.4 this is O5, treated there and in 5.6 (the pieces Y_{i,X}, 6.4 *Extent and reserve count*; the extent of every step-1 species from 𝒩₁, 5.6). At j = 1 the carried weights inside A_1 rest on 6.5's budget amendment, and the extent of every step-1 species is measured from 𝒩₁ (5.6).
*7.2 The per-component cost and the enlarged induction.* At every step of its lifetime the carried mass of Z's species is ≤ 2A_j(Z) ≤ 2C̄_A(d_j′(Z)+1) (§5, §6.4). At j = 1 every step-1 species (att(1, Z), the inputs term, lw, att(1, coll)) is carried with the factor C_lin of 5.6 in place of 2. wrap(1) stays in (ii), with growth L per step (5.0, 2.1(e), map (c)(ε)). C_lin is a constant, so below the factor 2 is read as C_lin at j = 1, and no comparison changes (ε_A(1) remains a polylog of the same degree). The words are expanded multilinearly in the species, so the total of Z's attached insertions on a labelled term is ≤ Π(1+‖Y‖) ≤ e^{2C̄_A(d_j′(Z)+1)}. Each later step n of the lifetime also makes the species att(n, Z_n) for the component Z_n ⊇ Z of Z_n (2.0's convention; Z ⊆ Λ_jᶜ ⊆ Λ_nᶜ), of mass ≤ 2C̄_A(d_n′(Z_n)+1), and the exposed segments cost Cc_CMR_nτ̂_n²#cubes_n(Z_n) per step, n ≥ j (7.4(a), with τ̂_n² := max(τ_n², Cρ̂_n); map (5)(f) *Per-step cost*). So the costs enlarge Bałaban's per-step summand O(1)M^dR_n^{d+1}d_n′(S^{n−j}(Z)) of (1.80) by terms of the same shape: a constant times the current size, plus a constant per step. **The enlarged induction (derived: a re-run of pp.384–389, with the per-step summand of (1.80)–(1.81) enlarged by the costs above; the steps checked:)** (1.82) at j = 1 with the constant 2(64)^d → 4(1 + C_X)(64)^d (the per-X ledger below; map (5)(f) *The condition*; within "p₀ large, g small", p.385; p.386 and (1.88) need p₀(g), not its square, to beat R^{d+2}, the same condition); (1.83) verbatim; the merging (1.84)–(1.87) (pp.385–386) with the new summand subadditive: #cubes_n(Z) ≤ #cubes_n(X) + #cubes_n(Y) and d′(Z) ≤ d′(X)+d′(Y)+2d for Z ⊂ X ∪ Y (p.386), so (d′+1) is subadditive up to the slack 2d and the extra costs 4dC̄_A^{(j+1)} + the per-step constants, C̄_A^{(j+1)} := e^{c_W}sup_{n≥j+1}C_A(n) at step-(j+1) merges and small domains (no class-(III) term enters, since that term is C_A(1)'s only), are paid by the merging surplus 2(1+β₀)^{−1}p₀(g_{j+1}) ≥ O(1)·6(100M)^dR_{j+1}^{d+2} ((1.88) with its constant doubled, the same smallness); p.387's future sums: S^m(Z) ⊆ S^m(X) ∪ S^m(Y) by p.384's S^m(Z) = ∪_{□⊂Z}S^m(□) with Z ⊂ X ∪ Y (the 20MR_n-cube in S^{n−j−1}(X) ∩ S^{n−j−1}(Y) is what p.387 uses for the subadditivity of d_n′ without the +2d), so the cube counts and d_n′ of the merged region are bounded by the sums, with the extra 2n₁ + R_{j+1} steps (K ≤ K₂ + n₁ + R_{j+1}, n₁ < 10, X's and Y's n₁ each) each costing ≤ 2C̄_A(100^d+1) + Cc_CML²R_jτ̂̄²100^d (S^{K₁}(X) lies in a cube of size 100MR_{j+1+K₁}, p.387, as (1.88) charges these steps), paid from the same surplus; new large-field regions inside the lifetime enter through (1.84)–(1.85) (p.386; small domains pp.385–386) and bring their own fundamental factors, from which their costs are paid as at j (7.3); (1.89) verbatim. The n = j+1 scaling step and the 64^d bound over the ≤ R_j later steps are used in (1.81)'s form, as the map applies them (*Lifetime sum*), not as printed on p.385. At j = 1 the fundamental factor is RC_PAYMENT_PASS §7's ((W₁), (R₁), (P₁,Q₁) with (1+β₀)^{−2}g₁^{−2}, and the volume terms), in the form of the modified (1.76) of IT-1 §3c(4‴): κ₁(Z) ≥ ¼c₄(434)^{−d}ϑ_V²A₁²(log g₁^{−2})^{2p₀}(d₁′(Z)+1) − O(1)M^dR₁^{d+1}d₁′(Z) (§3j(6)). RC §7's two losses are inside this form (checked against IT-1 note (D2), l.1266–1276): (i) p.380's P₁, Q₁ factors at j = 1 give ≥ 4m_Aa per cube with g₁^{−2} (m_A := ¼min{½B₃^{−2}A₀²A₁^{−2}, 2}, l.1174), and RC §7's coefficient (1+β₀)^{−2}g₁^{−2} costs a factor (1+β₀)^{−2} ≥ ¼ (β₀ ≤ 1, p.255 l.51), which the slack ¼ of m_A absorbs, so ≥ m_Aa per cube remains, as c₄ := ½min{γ₀m_A, c_b/(4·10^d)} uses; (ii) the step-1 volume terms ((2.47)–(2.49) at k = 1, at most 2B₁ per point of Ω₁^{(1)}∖Λ⁰ by Pass 4 (4) (IT-1 note l.712), plus the normalisations at O(log g₀^{−2}) per site, l.1266) are inside the factor exp{O(1)log g₁^{−2}|𝒳|} ≤ exp{O(1)M^dR₁^{d+1}(d₁′(𝒳)+1)} of (D2) *Together* (l.1270). RC §7 is not reviewed. *Ledger at j = 1 (per label polymer: §3j(6) attributes the fundamental factor e^{−κ₁(X)−2p₀(g₁)} to the label polymers X, the components of Λᶜ, and 6.5's retreat factor is per X).* Put K₁ := ¼c₄(434)^{−d}ϑ_V²A₁²(log g₁^{−2})^{2p₀} and B₁ := O(1)(64)^dM^dL^{d+1}R₁^{d+2}. The components of Z₁ = Λᶜ are the label polymers X (6.2 *Units*, corrected 26 Oct 2026; the Z ⊆ X bookkeeping below stays valid with Z = X, and 6.5's share 3·2^dlog 2·(d₁′(X)+1) with the factor 4^{#ℓ-cubes(X)} is no longer needed, the retreat slots being lw(X) insertions; the printed condition on K₁ is stronger than needed and stays valid). The label polymers are the ∗-components of Λᶜ (ℓ-cubes sharing a point; IT-1 §3c(4⁗)(d) and its (2.19) regions), the weakest adjacency, so a connected Z lies in one X. And Σ_{Z⊆X}(d₁′(Z)+1) ≤ C_X(d₁′(X)+1), C_X := √d·3·2^{d−1}. Z is a connected union of MR₁-cubes, so a spanning tree of its cube adjacency graph has #cubes₁(Z) − 1 edges of length ≤ √d cube sides. RG1 p.257 measures d_j(X) as the length, in unit cubes, of a shortest tree graph in the continuous space contained in X and meeting all its cubes. The segment between the centres of two ∗-adjacent cubes lies in their union and has Euclidean length ≤ √d, which gives d₁′(Z) + 1 ≤ √d·#cubes₁(Z); the Z ⊆ X are disjoint, so the sum is ≤ √d·#cubes₁(X) ≤ √d·3·2^{d−1}(d₁′(X)+1) (6.5 *Absorption*'s count). Require K₁ ≥ 4(1 + C_X)B₁ + 12·2^dlog 2 and ε_A(1) ≤ K₁/(8C_X) (7.1's comparison with 8C_X in place of 8: the same polylog against (log g₁^{−2})^{2p₀}). Then κ₁(X) ≥ K₁(d₁′(X)+1) − B₁(d₁′(X)+1) (the absorption term, O(1)M^dR₁^{d+1}(d₁′+1) ≤ B₁(d₁′+1), with the +1 of (D2)'s volume factor). Of this: the future sums (1.81) take ≤ Σ_{Z⊆X}B₁(d₁′(Z)+1) ≤ C_XB₁(d₁′(X)+1) (charged per component Z; a sum for X itself, B₁(d₁′(X)+1), is smaller, C_X ≥ 1); the factors e^{E_1(Z)} take Σ_{Z⊆X}ε_A(1)(d₁′(Z)+1) ≤ (K₁/8)(d₁′(X)+1); the reserves Res(Z) := (K₁/(8C_X))(d₁′(Z)+1) are set aside, Σ_{Z⊆X}Res(Z) ≤ (K₁/8)(d₁′(X)+1) (carried to D2.7, *Use of Res* below); the share 3·2^dlog 2·(d₁′(X)+1) is carved out next to them for the retreat factor 4^{#ℓ-cubes(X)} of §6.5 and 7.1 (carved out, not paid from Pol); and Pol(X) := (K₁ − (1 + C_X)B₁ − K₁/4 − 3·2^dlog 2)(d₁′(X)+1) ≥ (K₁/2)(d₁′(X)+1) is carried (κ₁(X) ≥ Σ_{Z⊆X}Res(Z) + Pol(X) + the share ≥ 0). The branches below run per region of Bałaban's induction; how X's Pol and the Res(Z), Z ⊆ X, pass to the regions of Z₂ at step 2 is the acceptance of the pair (Λ₁, Λ) by the later steps, derived on 26 Oct 2026 in WILSON_IT1_O2_CLOSURE_2026_10_26.md §4.2 (formerly OPEN as O2; for review): the step-2 construction carries 𝐓₁ = T₁^{Λ₁,Λ} whole ((3.24) p.270), and LFR II's induction (1.80)–(1.88) takes from step 1 only κ₁(X), j(X) = 1 and (1.76) per component X of Λᶜ, with no Λ₁-entropy to pay, Λ₁ being a function of CRE's labels (Ω₁, S₁). This rests on §3j(6) and §3c(4‴) (listed in §8).
*The reserve through the induction.* The induction of pp.384–389 is run for κ̂_j(Z) := κ_j(Z) − Res(Z) − Pol(Z), branch by branch (LFR II pp.385–387, in the transcription):
  - (1.85)'s new regions: at j = 1 per component Z of Z₁, the per-Z share κ₁(Z) := (B₁ + ε_A(1) + K₁/(8C_X))(d₁′(Z)+1) of κ₁(X) (the ledger above pays Σ_{Z⊆X}κ₁(Z) from κ₁(X)), with Res(Z) as above and Pol(Z) := 0, Pol(X) being carried per label polymer X under O2; as in 7.3 for j ≥ 2.
  - (1.83), Z = S(Z₀) with no new field: Res(Z) := Res(Z₀), Pol(Z) := Pol(Z₀), so κ̂_{j+1}(Z) = κ̂_j(Z₀) − O(1)M^dR_{j+1}^{d+1}d′_{j+1}(Z), and the enlarged (1.80) for Z₀ gives it for Z, as on p.385.
  - p.385–386's small-domain branch, Z = S(Z₀) with Z₀ satisfying (i), (ii) and a new large field: Bałaban discards κ_j(Z₀) ≥ 0 ("we do not have a better bound for it, but we have the new factor exp(−p₀(g_j))", p.386 l.1) and sets κ_{j+1}(Z) = p₀(g_j) − O(1)M^dR_{j+1}^{d+1}d′_{j+1}(Z). The integrand still carries exp(−κ_j(Z₀)), so here κ_{j+1}(Z) := κ_j(Z₀) + ¾p₀(g_j) − O(1)M^dR_{j+1}^{d+1}d′_{j+1}(Z) (the other ¼p₀(g_j) pays the new region's costs, 7.3), with Res(Z) := Res(Z₀) + ¼p₀(g_j) and Pol(Z) := Pol(Z₀) + ¼p₀(g_j). Then κ̂_{j+1}(Z) = κ̂_j(Z₀) + ¼p₀(g_j) − O(1)M^dR_{j+1}^{d+1}d′_{j+1}(Z) ≥ ¼p₀(g_j) − O(1)M^dR_{j+1}^{d+1}100^d, which covers Bałaban's future sum over K = R_{j+1} steps (p.386's condition with the constant 4, within "p₀ large"); κ̂_j(Z₀) ≥ 0 is dropped, as Bałaban drops κ_j(Z₀). So κ̂_{j+1}(Z) satisfies Bałaban's (1.80) for Z, and κ̂_{j+1}(Z) + ¼p₀(g_j), with the separate ¼p₀(g_j) of 7.3 that pays the summand's extra costs over these steps, satisfies the enlarged (1.80).
  - (1.86)–(1.88), Z joined from X, Y: Res(Z) := Res(X) + Res(Y) + Res_merge and Pol(Z) := Pol(X) + Pol(Y), with Res_merge := C(n₁ + R_{j+1} + 1)(100^d + 1)m̄_{j+1}, m̄_{j+1} := sup_{n≥j+1}m_n (the extra steps run past j+1), carved from the merging surplus 2(1+β₀)^{−1}p₀(g_{j+1}) (m_n the rooted link mass per MR_n-cube of 7.4(a); Res_merge is g_* times a polylog, so the surplus still pays (1.88)'s O(1)3(100M)^dR_{j+1}^{d+2} with its constant doubled). (1.88) spends κ̂(X) + κ̂(Y) on Z's future sum, as printed.
  - (1.89) verbatim: κ̂_k(X) ≥ 0 implies κ_k(X) ≥ Res(X) + Pol(X) ≥ 0.
  *Use of Res (for D2.7).* Within D2.6 (k+1 ≤ N, no R-operation, R-h) Res is not spent: the factor e^{E^∂} is paid pointwise on each labelled term by 7.2's exposed-segment term (7.4(b)(ii)). Res is carried, with the induction clause 4·mass(Z) ≤ Res(Z), to the exponentiated cluster expansion at the R-operation (LFR II (1.90)–(1.97)), which is D2.7's. Here mass(Z) := Σ_n m_n·13^d#cubes_n(Z_n) over Z's lifetime, Z_n its lineage. The clause holds at creation: by 7.2's chain (Σ_n#cubes_n(S^{n−j}(Z)) ≤ C_d′(LR_j+1)(d_j′(Z)+1)) and m_n ≤ C_νc_CMR_nτ̂_n² (7.4(a)), mass(Z) ≤ 13^dC_νc_CML C_d′R_j(LR_j+1)τ̂̄²(d_j′(Z)+1), g_* times a polylog, so ≤ Res(Z)/4 for small g_*. At n = 1, m₁ is the step-1 form 205^d(ℓ/ℓ₀+2)⁴C_rτ₁² per ℓ-cube of 7.4(a), which adds 205^d(ℓ/ℓ₀+2)⁴C_rτ₁²·13^d·3·2^{d−1}(d₁′(Z)+1), also g² times a polylog, so the clause holds against Res(Z) = (K₁/(8C_X))(d₁′(Z)+1). It survives the branches: S and the small-domain branch keep it (S^m(Z) ⊆ S^m(Z₀)'s continuation; ¼p₀(g_j) ≥ 4m̄_{j+1}·13^d100^d(n₁ + R_{j+1} + 1)); at a merge, S^m(Z) ⊆ S^m(X) ∪ S^m(Y) (7.2) gives mass(Z) ≤ mass(X) + mass(Y) + (the extra 2n₁ + R_{j+1} steps, X's and Y's n₁ each, each step with ≤ 100^d cubes) ≤ (Res(X) + Res(Y) + Res_merge)/4 for C ≥ 8·13^d, with Res_merge's factor (n₁ + R_{j+1} + 1) ≥ (2n₁ + R_{j+1})/2. p.387's arbitrary-region claim is accepted for j ≥ 2 only (RC_PAYMENT §7 *Use*), where the factor is LFR II's own. **Derived** (the re-run above; the enlarged summand's form and size), with LFR II's own steps **accepted** where they are used verbatim ((1.83), (1.89), p.387's structure).
*7.3 The condition at general j, with (1.85).* For a component created at step j+1 ≥ 2, (1.85) is stated in d′(Z^{(i)}) for the region Z^{(i)} of the step; for Z ⊂ (Z^{(i)})^{∼2} the conversion to d′(Z) costs p.385's factor 2(14)^d (for ∼3), which goes into the leading term. So κ_{j+1}(Z) ≥ K_{j+1}(d′(Z)+1) − B_{j+1}d′(Z), K_{j+1} := ½γ₀A₁²p₀²(g_{j+1})/(2(14)^d), the second term (1.85)'s absorption term O(1)M^dR_{j+1}^{d+1}d′ ≤ B_{j+1}d′. *Ledger at j+1* (the same as at j = 1, each cost paid once): with B_{j+1} := O(1)(64)^dM^dL^{d+1}R_{j+1}^{d+2} and K_{j+1} ≥ 8B_{j+1} (γ₀A₁²p₀²(g_{j+1}) ≥ 32(14)^d·O(1)(64)^dM^dL^{d+1}R_{j+1}^{d+2}: Bałaban's p.385 condition with a larger constant, uniform in j for g_{j+1} ≤ g_*, within "p₀ large": p₀(g) beats the polylog R^{d+2}), from K_{j+1}(d′+1) the absorption term takes B_{j+1}d′ ≤ B_{j+1}(d′+1) (this is the −B_{j+1}d′ above, not a second payment) and Bałaban's future sum B_{j+1}(d′+1), e^{E_{j+1}(Z)} takes ε_A(d′+1) ≤ (K_{j+1}/8)(d′+1) for small g_*, Res(Z) := (K_{j+1}/8)(d′+1) is the reserve carried to D2.7 (7.2 *Use of Res*), and Pol(Z) := (K_{j+1} − 2B_{j+1} − K_{j+1}/4)(d′+1) ≥ (K_{j+1}/2)(d′+1) is carried; (1.89)'s T′_k(X)1 ≤ exp(−2(1+β₀)^{−1}p₀(g_k)) stays Bałaban's. Small domains (p.386: κ_{j+1}(Z) = p₀(g_j) − O(1)M^dR_{j+1}^{d+1}d′, d′ ≤ 100^d, K = R_{j+1}): the costs are ≤ ε_A(100^d+1) + R_{j+1}·2C̄_A^{(j+1)}(100^d+1) ≤ ¼p₀(g_j) (C̄_A^{(j+1)} of 7.2) for small g_* (p₀(g), not squared, against the polylog: p₀ large), leaving ¾p₀(g_j): ¼p₀(g_j) is added to Res, ¼p₀(g_j) to Pol, and ¼p₀(g_j) pays Bałaban's own future sum over K = R_{j+1} steps (p.386's condition with the constant 4, within "p₀ large"), with κ_j(Z₀) kept (7.2, small-domain branch). **Derived** given 7.2.
*7.4 The exposed segments (R11).*
- (a) *E^∂_{k+1}(ℓ) as attached local terms.* By D2.3(8) l.1198, E^∂_{k+1}(ℓ) is the sum of the cluster terms of D2.2(5)–(6)'s expansion that see ℓ's data: the clusters of ℓ₀ that ℓ does not have, those only ℓ has, and the changed activities near ℓ's data. By the locality of the step (the s-localisation RG2 (1.9)–(1.10); the collar rule D2.4(4) *Scope*; §2.1's rule) a cluster X whose polymer does not meet Coll_{k+1} ∪ Z_{k+1} has the same activity on ℓ and on ℓ₀ (this rests on D2.4(4) *Scope* and D2.3, which are not supplied; it stays listed as given in §8), and the vacuum clusters cancel between the numerator and the normaliser. So E^∂_{k+1}(ℓ) = Σ_Xe^{(k+1)}_X, e^{(k+1)}_X := Φ_ℓ(X) − Φ_{ℓ₀}(X), over the loop-dependent clusters X meeting Coll_{k+1} ∪ Z_{k+1}; e^{(k+1)}_X depends on the data in X only (a cluster term is a function of its own activities), and it is attached to the component Z of Z_{k+1} whose collar X meets first (a fixed order), as a local term of the scalar, never bounded globally. The anchored bound: Σ_{X∋n}|e^{(k+1)}_X|e^{θ(X)} ≤ 2Σ_{X∋n}|Φ(X)|e^{θ(X)} ≤ Cτ̂_{k+1}² per traversal n, the Kotecký–Preiss tree bound of (S-w)/(12)(e) (D2.4's (T) is not used, D2.3(9)) with D(k+1)(ii)'s per-traversal activity sum Cρ̂_{k+1} ≤ τ̂_{k+1}² := max(τ_{k+1}², Cρ̂_{k+1}) (ρ̂ ≤ τ² would need q₁ < 3p₀, which is not imposed; τ̂ is used throughout, still g² times a polylog); and ≤ Cτ̂_{k+1}²e^{−c″d_j} for a segment j at distance d_j from the collar (the weight e^{θ} spent on the cubes between). This is IT-1 §3i(7)'s form at step k+1 (the thin sets X there are the clusters here). *Per cube.* The weight θ(X) is the reference-gas weight a°(X) = Σ_𝒢(ν/32)σ_{k+1}(𝒢) of D2.4(12)(f), which satisfies a°(X) ≥ (ν/32)#cubes_{k+1}(X) and a°(X) ≥ (ν/(64M))diam(X) (map D2.3(4)(Z-f)); every e_X is rooted at a traversal (the loop-independent clusters cancel). So for an MR_{k+1}-cube Q, splitting a° in halves and summing the roots by the decay count of 7.1's first bullet, m_{k+1} := Σ_{X∩Q≠∅}|e^{(k+1)}_X|e^{(ν/64)#cubes_{k+1}(X)} ≤ C_νc_CMR_{k+1}τ̂_{k+1}². The clusters attached to Z meet Z's collar, inside Z^{∼6} (d₀ = 6), whose MR-cube count is ≤ 13^d#cubes(Z). The per-step cost per MR_{k+1}-cube of Z_{k+1} is then ≤ 13^dm_{k+1} ≤ Cc_CMR_{k+1}τ̂_{k+1}² (13^d inside C; C_ν absorbs the decay length M and the ratio M/M_V of the cube sides), the summand of 7.2. *At n = 1.* The step-1 clusters attached to Z meet the band within 101ℓ of Z (6.2, IT-1 §3d(4)), so their cube count is ≤ 205^d#cubes₁(Z) and the factor 13^d becomes 205^d, with §3i(7)'s rooted form (ℓ/ℓ₀+2)⁴C_rτ² per ℓ-cube in place of m₁; still a constant times τ² times a polylog per cube. **Derived** given D2.3(8), (S-w)/(12)(e), (f) with (Z-f)'s a°-content, and the locality of D2.2's construction, with D2.4(4) *Scope* and D2.3 for the same-activity step (given, §8).
- (b) *The per-cube comparisons of §3i(7) Against (0) (step 1).* (i) (a₂/ℓ₀+2)⁴C_rτ² against κ_{a₂}: the rooted bound per step-2 cube Q, Σ_{X∩Q≠∅}|e_X|e^{κ_{a₂}#cubes(X)} ≤ (a₂/ℓ₀+2)⁴C_rτ², is the Kotecký–Preiss condition for the defect polymers with the per-cube weight κ_{a₂} once (a₂/ℓ₀+2)⁴C_rτ̂² ≤ κ_{a₂}/2 (§3i(0)'s first form: the mass per cube ≤ κ_{a₂}/2); with κ_{a₂} ≥ κ(2ℓ₀+2)^{−4} fixed before the coupling, a₂ ≤ CMR₂ (a step-2 cube) and τ̂² ≤ Cg₀²(log g₀^{−2})^{max(6p₀, 4p₀+(q₁−p₀)₊)}, the left side is g₀² times a polylog and the condition holds for small g₀ (D2.3(9)'s note amended to τ̂²). **Checked** for the count checks of §3i(7) (comparisons of printed constants; the Kotecký–Preiss statement behind them is (S-w)/(12)(e)'s). (ii) *The large-field side within D2.6.* For k+1 ≤ N no R-operation has acted (R-h), and D2.0(5)(f) requires a bound per labelled term (7.4(d)); no polymer expansion of the large-field sum is made in D2.6. On a labelled term ℓ the e_X are numbers (D2.3(8), D(k+1)(v)), each attached to one component (7.4(a)), so |e^{E^∂(ℓ)}| ≤ exp(Σ_X|e_X(ℓ)|) ≤ Π_Z exp(Σ_n 13^dm_n#cubes_n(Z_n)), the product over the components with their lineages (at n = 1 with 205^d and §3i(7)'s rooted form, 7.4(a)), (the E^∂ terms anchored in Ret(Λ) ⊂ Z₁ = Λᶜ belong to their component's exposed-segment term, 7.1; no separate factor). This is the exposed-segment term of 7.2's E_j(Z), paid by ε_A ≤ K/8 out of κ. So the factor e^{E^∂} is paid once, by 7.2, and the link masses of the record below are the same factor; no second payment is made. *The joint linking expansion (D2.7's).* At the R-operation (k > N) Bałaban exponentiates the large-field sum: Mayer expansion of (1.72)'s bracket, components connected through the localisation domains Y, activities (1.91), bounds (1.92)–(1.97) with (1.89)'s factor exp(−2(1+β₀)^{−1}p₀(g_k) + 1) per T′-region and the volume factor exp(−(6·2^d)^{−1}βκM^{−d}|X′∖∪Y_i|) (LFR II pp.387–389). The links e_X then couple the T′-regions and must be expanded jointly with them: the position factor is spent only where something is summed, the link set keeps its connectedness, and p.388's tree count is used with Bałaban's factor, not with Pol. Route recorded for D2.7: (1) minimal connecting link trees T, the non-tree links paid by Π_Z e^{2mass(Z)} ≤ Π_Z e^{Res(Z)/2} (the clause 4·mass ≤ Res of 7.2); (2) per tree link, e^{−(ν/64)#} split into a quarter that, with the per-cube component mass Σ_{Z∋Q}(shape count)e^{−Pol(Z)} ≤ ν/256 (Pol ≥ (K/2)(d′+1), the number of components through Q with d′ = D at most C_d^{D+1}), pays the components each link reaches, and a part kept as volume decay for (1.93)–(1.96) on the link cubes; (3) the root cubes of each component summed by the other half of Res. Step (2) uses Pol ≥ (K/2)(d′+1) at the R-operation; Pol is carried additively through S and the merges while d′ changes (the +2d slack, the change of scale), so that bound is not shown at the later steps, and it is part of D2.7's route. Whether the kept link decay pays (1.94)'s volume cost O(1)α^{1/3} per M-cube of the link cubes (Bałaban's α, κ against the link rate ν/64) is not checked; this is D2.7's, with the k > N terms. **Derived** for (ii) within D2.6 (the pointwise per-term payment, given 7.2 and 7.4(a)); the joint expansion is now the D2.7 note's item 15.3 (the loop-linked part of (1.72)'s bracket as the ratio of two polymer gases with the incompatibility factor, the links among the activities) with 15.5–15.7, derived there given the reading R-far, for review; the route above is the record.
- (c) *The label weight a_lab·n_t of §3j(6):* §6.5.
- (d) *𝔼[N_exp].* The bound 𝔼[N_exp] ≤ 4(2c_st+1)⁴C_Ln_C(q + N_retq_ϑ) of §3d(5) is not used: the payment is per component on each labelled term, (a)–(b) and 7.2, as D2.0(5)(f) requires (a per-term bound; no expectation of an exponential is taken).

## §9. The carrier item on the band (R14; CRE pp.245, 246 (1.3), 257, 265–269 read in full)

*9.1 What is needed.* D2.4(5) (T-dev) "(2) The global induction" (map l.1355), the downward induction of (T-dev): for V_{i+1}^* in the full step-(i+1) support, V_i^* := M^iU_{k+1}, which is V^{(i)}(V_{i+1}^*) on Γ_{i+1} and V_i off Γ_{i+1} (on Γ_{i+1} by (3.10), p.266: V^{(i)} = M^i(U_{i+1}) with the induction hypothesis on V_{i+1}^*; on the step-(k+1) Γ_i = Ω_i^{(i)}∖Ω_{i+1}^{(i)} by (2.10)–(2.12), U_{k+1}'s constraints there being V_i's; on Ω_iᶜ by R-avg, the composition M^iU = average of M^{i−1}U, derived: M^k(U) = Ū^k is the k-fold average (RG1 (0.21), p.256), defined by iteration, Ū^{k+1} = (Ū^k)‾ (CMP98 (42)–(43), pp.23–24, the one-step average of RG1 (0.12) having "all results of [12]", RG1 p.254), and Ū^k_c depends only on the bonds of B^k(c_−) ∪ B^k(c_+) (CMP98 p.24, after (43)); p.267's "M^{k+1}(U_{k+1}) = V_{k+1} = M^{k+1}(U_{k+1,□′}) on □′^∼" being an analogue at the top scale only, with (1.3)'s locality, p.246: (Q^{s*}V)(b) is 1 inside blocks and V(c) on B(c), so it depends on V at one block bond, and p.246's "The function in (1.2) depends on the field V restricted to □′^{∼4}"; where □^{∼4} meets Ω_{i−1}ᶜ as well, R-avg is iterated down the scales), must lie in the full step-i support: χ_i(□)(V_i^*) = 1 for every LM₂R_i-cube □ ⊂ Ω_i ((2.16)–(2.17), p.257), and χ_Ax = 1. Map D2.0(5)(c)(β) derives this for □ ⊂ □′ ⊂ Ω_{i+1}^{∼−1}, □′ a cube of (3.16), from (3.19). Left there: the band cubes □ ⊂ Ω_{i+1}^∼ in no such □′, and the cubes □ ⊂ Ω_i∖Ω_{i+1}^∼ with □^{∼4} ∩ Ω_{i+1} ≠ ∅.
*9.2 The second class is empty (derived).* A cube □ ⊂ Ω_i∖Ω_{i+1}^∼ is at distance ≥ LMR_{i+1} from Ω_{i+1} (Ω_{i+1}^∼ adds one layer of LMR_{i+1}-cubes, p.265), and □^{∼4} extends at most 4LM₂R_i ≤ 4L²M₂R_{i+1} (p.245's rule gives M₂R-layers, so 4LM₂R_i is an upper bound; R_i ≤ LR_{i+1} by (2.9)'s second line with (2.5), as in D2.2(3)(b)9). So □^{∼4} ∩ Ω_{i+1} = ∅ once M ≥ 4LM₂, which is within p.245's choice (l.34–37: M = L^m, M₂ = L^{m₂}, M₁ < M₂ < M, "M will be chosen much larger than M₂"). On □^{∼4}, V_i^* = V_i: on the part in Ω_i∖Ω_{i+1} because U_{k+1}'s constraints there are V_i's, by (2.10)–(2.12); where □^{∼4} meets Ω_iᶜ by R-avg with (2.10)–(2.12) at i−1 and the step-i δ-function, which fixes V_i = V̄_{i−1} there, with (1.3)'s locality. So χ_i(□)(V_i^*) = χ_i(□)(V_i) = 1 on the support. That these small-field factors are present in the integrand at this point is the reading R-ζ(a) (map D2.4(5)(2), region 3), given R-h (no R-operation has removed them). **Derived given** R-ζ(a), R-h and M ≥ 4LM₂ (R-avg is derived, 9.1).
*9.3 The band cubes (derived by route (a), given R-ζ(b), R-h and R-ζ(a) beyond Ω_{i+1}^∼, for re-review).* Let □ ⊂ Ω_{i+1}^∼ and □′ ⊃ □ the L²M₂R_{i+1}-cube of pp.265–266 ("This cube determines a cube □′ of the next partition, such that □ ⊂ □′ ⊂ Ω_{i+1}^∼"). Bałaban's (3.6)–(3.8) at V_i = V_i^*: (3.6) is an identity for any V_i; (3.7)–(3.8) need (a) U_{i+1,□′} passing (3.2)'s test, true on the support since □′ ⊂ Ω_{i+1}^{∼2}, where "there are only the small field characteristic functions from the above decompositions of unity" (p.265, after (3.5)); for □′ ⊄ Ω_{i+1} these small-field factors sit in ζ(Ω_{i+1}ᶜ): this is nearly printed (every resummed term has Q′ and P′ at least 2 and 4 layers outside Ω_{i+1} by (3.5), so □′ ⊂ Ω_{i+1}^∼ is small-field in every term: p.265's Ω^{∼2} sentence with p.267's resummation), given R-h (no R-operation has acted on these factors); this content is R-ζ(b), distinct from region 3's R-ζ(a) used in 9.2; and (b) the argument of 𝐇_{i,□}, (1/i)log[V_i^*(V^{(i)}_{□′})^{−1}] off the 2M₁ boundary layer, bounded by 4δ_i (with p.266's boundary-layer input: it holds at V_i^* where V_i^* = V_i; on the Γ_{i+1}-blocks it rests on [14]'s (1.65) as p.266 cites it: REG (1.65), p.87, is REG Lemma 1 (p.79, (1.25), |V′ − 1| < 4d²α₀ + α₁ for two regular configurations with α₁-close averages, local by p.80) applied down the scales). On the blocks of □^{∼4} off Γ_{i+1} that lie in □′^{∼2}, V_i^* = V_i and (3.3)'s small-field function on □′, in force on Ω_{i+1}^{∼2}, gives |V_i(V^{(i)}_{□′})^{−1} − 1| < 2δ_i, hence a logarithm < 4δ_i, p.266's "hence it can be bounded by 4δ_k". At i < k, V_{i+1}^* = V_{i+1} on □′^{∼4}: on Γ_{i+1} by (2.10)–(2.12), on Ω_{i+1}ᶜ by R-avg with the δ-function δ(V̄_iV_{i+1}^{−1}) of (3.1) (p.264) and p.267's display, and □′^{∼4} ∩ Ω_{i+2} = ∅ by (G3) with (3.20) and M ≥ 4LM₂. So (3.2), (3.3) and, on the ring, (3.9) are evaluated at V_{i+1}. On the blocks of Γ_{i+1}, V_i^* = V^{(i)} and the bound would be (3.19) for □′, whose printed domain is b ∈ (□′^{∼2})^{(k)*} (the first printed line of p.269), the intersection with Ω_{i+1}^{(i)} coming from (3.10)'s domain of V^{(i)}; p.268 derives it from the localisation identity (3.17) for the minimiser U_{i+1} relative to U_{i+1,□′}, whose constraints on □′^{∼4} are those of the scale-(i+1) blocks 𝔹_{i+1}(□′^{∼4}). Where this stands:
- For every □′ with □′^{∼4} ⊂ Ω_{i+1}: this covers □′ ⊂ Ω_{i+1}^{∼−1} (4L²M₂R_{i+1} ≤ LMR_{i+1} under M ≥ 4LM₂) and the inner band cubes whose □′^{∼4} stays in Ω_{i+1}. U_{i+1}'s constraints on □′^{∼4} are the scale-(i+1) ones, so (3.17) is Bałaban's identity there and map (β)'s argument applies (p.268). **Derived** from R-avg (9.1), [15] (VAR Theorem 1, p.279, uniqueness of the critical orbit; (190), p.308), [12] (CMP98 (106)–(108), p.33, and (159)–(163), p.42: block-local bounds on the k-fold averages of U′U₀ against U₀, no condition on the domain) and [14] (1.65)'s reasoning applied at V^{(i)} (p.266 applies it at V_k), each read at the cited place (RG1's bibliography, p.299, gives CRE's numbers: [12] CMP98, [14] REG, [15] VAR, [16] UV3); this extends p.268's printed scope □′ ⊂ R_{k+1}ᶜ to these cubes.
- For □′ with □′^{∼4} ⊄ Ω_{i+1} (the crossing cubes), □′^{∼4} meets Ω_{i+1}ᶜ, where U_{i+1}'s constraints are the scale-i ones ((2.10)–(2.12)). (3.17) with 𝔹_{i+1}(□′^{∼4}) is then not an identity for U_{i+1}, and route (a) treats these cubes.
- *Route (a) (derived).* Bałaban prints mixed-set localisation. p.257 (2.14)–(2.15) defines the localised determining set 𝔹 ∪ 𝔹_j(Ω) := (𝔹 ∩ Ω^{∼−2}) ∪ (𝔹_j(Ω) ∩ (Ω∖Ω^{∼−2})) in two cases: (single layer) under the conditions that ∂Ω ⊂ Ω_j∖Ω_{j+1}, Ω is a union of M₁-cubes, and the distance between ∂Ω and the boundary of Ω_j, Ω_{j+1} contained in Ω is ≥ 2M₁; (several layers) when ∂Ω lies in several Ω_j∖Ω_{j+1}, by the sentence "we construct the corresponding determining sets restricted to the domains", which prints no condition. At a point where ∂□′^{∼4} crosses ∂Ω_{i+1} the single-layer distance is 0, so that clause does not cover the crossing cubes; and p.269's domain Λ_{k+1}ᶜ ∩ Λ_k is not a crossing instance (∂Λ_{k+1} lies ≥ 2 LMR_{k+1}-layers inside Ω_{k+1}, (3.20), and ∂Λ_k ≥ 6 layers outside it, (G1), so ∂Ω_{k+1} is interior to the domain and the single-layer condition holds there). p.269 takes the mixed function on Λ_{k+1}ᶜ ∩ Λ_k (determining set 𝔹_{k+1}(Λ_{k+1}ᶜ)|_{Ω_{k+1}} ∪ 𝔹_k(Λ_k)|_{Ω_{k+1}ᶜ}) and writes "We use again the formula (3.17), but with U_{k+1,□′} replaced by the above function. It yields the representation (3.18) with the corresponding changes … and with similar bounds"; (3.22) bounds the resulting 𝐇^{(k)} on S_{k+1} ⊇ Ω_{k+1}∖Ω_{k+1}^{∼−1} by O(1)ε_k exp(−R_k). Route (a) does the same for each crossing cube: (3.17) relative to Û_{□′} := U(𝔹_{i+1}(□′^{∼4})|_{Ω_{i+1}} ∪ 𝔹_i(□′^{∼4})|_{Ω_{i+1}ᶜ}, ·) with p.269's similar bounds. The two steps this needs are derived as follows.
  (1) *𝔹_mix is a determining set of VAR's class.* VAR p.277 (1): nested domains Ω′₀ ⊃ Ω′₁ ⊃ …, Ω′_n a union of M₁L^nη-blocks, with dist(Ω′_nᶜ, Ω′_{n+1}) bounded below at scale n, Λ′_n = Ω′_n∖Ω′_{n+1}, 𝔹 = ∪Λ′_n. CRE p.256 builds 𝔹_j(Ω) as the maximal such sequence Ω = Ω₀(Ω) ⊃ … ⊃ Ω_j(Ω) with distances ≥ L^nξM₁ (Bałaban's constant, the same in his single-layer case) and Ω^{∼−2} ⊂ Ω_j(Ω). p.256 states this with ξ = L^{−j}, so 𝔹_i(Ω) and 𝔹_{i+1}(Ω) as printed live on different fine lattices. A determining set for the one field U_{i+1} on T_ξ, ξ = L^{−(i+1)}, can only use one lattice. This is already so in p.269's 𝔹_{k+1}(Λ_{k+1}ᶜ)|_{Ω_{k+1}} ∪ 𝔹_k(Λ_k)|_{Ω_{k+1}ᶜ}, a set for U_{k+1}. So all sets here are on T_ξ, ξ = L^{−(i+1)}, and "𝔹_i(Ω)" means 𝔹_{i+1}(Ω) with its level-(i+1) constraints replaced by the level-i constraints of the same blocks. The recursion for Ω_n(Ω) on a fixed ξ does not depend on the top level, so the two share Ω_n(Ω), n ≤ i, and 𝔹_mix's constraints imply 𝔹_{i+1}(□′^{∼4})'s by the induction P(n) of (2)(i), which covers the bonds whose blocks lie in different layers. Put Ω′_n := Ω_n ∩ Ω_n(Ω), Ω_n the global domains, Ω = □′^{∼4}. This is p.257's several-layer set: inside Ω^{∼−2} ⊂ Ω_{i+1}(Ω) it is the global 𝔹 (2.14), and on the collar over Ω_j∖Ω_{j+1} it is 𝔹_j(Ω) (Ω′_n = Ω_n(Ω) for n ≤ j, Ω′_{j+1} = ∅ there); it equals 𝔹_{i+1}(□′^{∼4})|_{Ω_{i+1}} ∪ 𝔹_i(□′^{∼4})|_{Ω_{i+1}ᶜ} where □′^{∼4} ⊂ Ω_i (and the analogous three-layer set where it meets Ω_iᶜ). Nesting: Ω′_nᶜ = Ω_nᶜ ∪ Ω_n(Ω)ᶜ and Ω′_{n+1} lies in Ω_{n+1} and in Ω_{n+1}(Ω), so dist(Ω′_nᶜ, Ω′_{n+1}) ≥ min(dist(Ω_nᶜ, Ω_{n+1}), dist(Ω_n(Ω)ᶜ, Ω_{n+1}(Ω))), the smaller of two distances Bałaban's constructions already satisfy. These are of different printed forms: (G2) transcribes VAR (1) as > RM₁ in level-n units, while p.256 gives L^nξM₁ for 𝔹_j(Ω); that p.256's sets are admissible for VAR's theory is accepted from Bałaban's use of them (pp.257, 269), not derived here. The crossing of ∂□′^{∼4} with ∂Ω_{i+1} imposes nothing further: the condition involves only Ω′_nᶜ, which near the crossing is Ω_n(Ω)ᶜ on both sides. Ω′_n is a union of M₁L^n-blocks, as an intersection of two such unions (compatible partitions with side ratios powers of L, p.245).
  (2) *The localisation identity (3.17) for 𝔹_mix.* Let W on □′^{∼4} have M_{𝔹_mix}(W) = M_{𝔹_mix}(U_{i+1}), and glue it to U_{i+1} outside. (i) The glued field has U_{i+1}'s global constraints. *Conventions.* A domain that is a union of level-n blocks contains the level-n bonds that intersect it (RG1 Sect. 0, p.251), i.e. those with at least one endpoint in it (VAR p.277, "sets of bonds with at least one end-point belonging to Ω_j"); CRE (2.2) uses this convention ("Sect. 0 [I]"), and the layer sets are Λ′_n = Ω′_n^{(n)}∖Ω′_{n+1}^{(n)} (VAR p.277). The one-step average (RG1 (0.4), p.253) of a level-n bond c is a function of the level-(n−1) field on the bonds with both endpoints in B(c₋) ∪ B(c₊) (the contours Γ, Γ′ inside the two blocks and the transports [x,x′] of c between them), and M^n = M∘M^{n−1} (R-avg, 9.1). Ω′_n is a union of level-n blocks (an intersection of two unions of M₁L^n-blocks, (1)), and Ω′_n ⊆ Ω_n. *Claim P(n).* For every level-n bond c with no endpoint in Ω′_{n+1}, the glued field and U_{i+1} have the same level-n average on c. P(0): a fine bond with no endpoint in Ω′₁ either has an endpoint in Ω′₀, so it lies in Λ′₀, where 𝔹_mix fixes the field itself (M⁰ = id; VAR (3)) to U_{i+1}'s value, or lies outside □′^{∼4}, where the glued field is U_{i+1}. P(n−1) ⇒ P(n): if c has an endpoint in Ω′_n, then c ∈ Λ′_n, a constraint of 𝔹_mix, and its two blocks lie in □′^{∼4}: the block of that endpoint lies in Ω′_n ⊆ Ω_n(□′^{∼4}) (a union of level-n blocks), and the other block lies within L^nξ of it, while p.256's local chain gives dist(Ω_n(□′^{∼4}), (□′^{∼4})ᶜ) ≥ dist(Ω_n(□′^{∼4}), Ω_{n−1}(□′^{∼4})ᶜ) ≥ L^nξM₁ ≥ L^nξ (neither the global distance nor M₁ ≥ 2 is needed). There the glued field is W, so its average is the datum M_{𝔹_mix}(U_{i+1})(c). Otherwise neither endpoint is in Ω′_n; Ω′_n being a union of level-n blocks, B(c₋) ∪ B(c₊) ⊂ Ω′_nᶜ, so every level-(n−1) bond with both endpoints in B(c₋) ∪ B(c₊) has no endpoint in Ω′_n, and P(n−1) with (0.4) gives P(n). *Use.* A global constraint of level m is a bond c ∈ Γ_m = Ω_m^{(m)}∖Ω_{m+1}^{(m)} (CRE (2.2)); it has no endpoint in Ω_{m+1} ⊇ Ω′_{m+1}, so P(m) applies. This covers blocks over several local layers, bonds with their two blocks in different layers and the bonds crossing ∂□′^{∼4} or ∂Ω_{i+1}, with no condition on the width of Λ′₀. The same induction, with the sequence Ω_n(□′^{∼4}) of 𝔹_{i+1}(□′^{∼4}) in place of the global one (Ω′_{n+1} ⊆ Ω_{n+1}(□′^{∼4})), shows that 𝔹_mix's constraints imply 𝔹_{i+1}(□′^{∼4})'s, which (B) below uses. (ii) A plaquette with a bond outside □′^{∼4} has its inside bonds in Λ′₀ (Ω′₁ is at distance ≥ LξM₁ ≥ 2 fine units from Ω′₀ᶜ, CRE p.256), so A^η(glued) − A^η(U_{i+1}) is the difference of VAR's local functional (5) on Ω′₀ = □′^{∼4}. Hence U_{i+1} critical for the global problem gives U_{i+1}|_{□′^{∼4}} critical for the 𝔹_mix-problem. (iii) U_{i+1}|_{□′^{∼4}} lies in VAR's space (6) = (2) ∩ (3) for the local sequence, the space in which p.279 states uniqueness ((3) holds with its own averages as data); for (2): its regularity at a point of global level m is the level-m bound, and the local level there is n ≤ m, whose threshold ε₀L^{−2n} is weaker. Its data satisfy VAR (7) with O(ε₀) (VAR p.278, Prop. 2 of [4]). VAR Theorem 1 (p.279) then gives a unique critical orbit, the minimal one, so U_{i+1}|_{□′^{∼4}} = U(𝔹_mix, M_{𝔹_mix}(U_{i+1}))^{u^{−1}} with u = 1 on 𝔹_mix (VAR (4)). This is the first equality of (3.17) for 𝔹_mix, by the argument that gives Bałaban's own (3.17). The second equality, the 𝐇̂-representation with its decay, is VAR Prop. 9 (p.309) with (190) (p.308), whose B-range (172) has the hypothesis |V′ − 1| < C₁^{VAR}ε₁ (|B′| < 2C₁^{VAR}ε₁ its consequence), for ε₁ ≤ ε̄₁ (VAR Sect. G's threshold, §4.2); since |e^{iB} − 1| ≤ |B|, it covers (A)'s boundary layer (< 44d²B₃ε_{i+1} on the Ω_{i+1}-blocks, p.268, < 30d²L²B₃(1+β₀)ε_i on the Ω_{i+1}ᶜ part, and < C_{3.14}ε_i on the level-i constraints straddling ∂Ω_{i+1} described in (B)) and (B)'s data difference X with its layer (bound in (B)) once ε₁ := (C₁^{VAR})^{−1}max(2·44d²B₃ε_{i+1}, 2·30d²L²B₃(1+β₀)ε_i, 2C_{3.14}ε_i, 2sup|X|, C₁^{VAR}·(the data regularity)) ≤ ε̄₁, C_{3.14} the O(1) of (3.14) at step i; every entry is O(ε_i), so this is a g-threshold (§8). p.269's "similar bounds" are exactly this application. (B)'s statement that U_{i+1,□′} is the 𝔹_mix-minimiser with its own averages needs the inclusion alone: 𝔹_mix's constraints imply 𝔹_{i+1}(□′^{∼4})'s by (i)'s induction, and a minimiser over the larger competitor set that lies in the smaller set minimises there. *The remaining step ((3.7)'s requirement is |𝐇_{i,□}| < (1/10)ε_i on □^∼, reached "for A₁/A₀ and γ sufficiently small"; 4δ_i is the bound available there, not the threshold).* Keep Bałaban's reference U_{i+1,□′} in (3.6), so that (3.8) is used exactly as printed (its first term is U_{i+1,□′}'s regularity with coefficient 1, p.267, from (3.2)'s test, which holds on the support for □′ ⊂ Ω_{i+1}^{∼2}). What changes is the argument of 𝐇_{i,□} on the Γ_{i+1}-blocks of □^{∼4}, (1/i)log[V^{(i)}(V^{(i)}_{□′})^{−1}], which is split at M^iÛ_{□′}: (A) V^{(i)} against M^iÛ_{□′}: by (1)–(2), U_{i+1} = (exp iL^{−1}η𝐇̂(·)Û_{□′})^{û^{−1}} with 𝐇̂'s argument (1/i)log[M·(U_{i+1})(M·(data of Û))^{−1}] equal to 0 off the boundary layer of □′^{∼4} (on the Ω_{i+1}-blocks M^{i+1}(U_{i+1}) = V_{i+1}; on the Ω_{i+1}ᶜ-blocks M^i(U_{i+1}) = V_i, (2.10)–(2.12)), so p.269's "similar bounds" give |V^{(i)}(M^iÛ_{□′})^{−1} − 1| < O(1)44d²B₃²(1+β₀)ε_ie^{−R_i} on □′^{∼2}, the analogue of (3.19). The O(1) absorbs a factor L² from U_{i+1}'s scale-i regularity on the Ω_{i+1}ᶜ part of the boundary layer ([15] Theorem 1 with p.256's |∂V_i − 1| < O(L²)ε_i), and the step from 𝐇̂ to the averages uses [12] (106)–(108), (159)–(163). (B) M^iÛ_{□′} against V^{(i)}_{□′}: U_{i+1,□′} minimises under 𝔹_{i+1}(□′^{∼4}), hence also under the finer set 𝔹_mix(□′^{∼4}) with its own averages as data (adding constraints that a minimiser satisfies does not change the minimum; uniqueness by [15]); so Û_{□′} and U_{i+1,□′} are minimisers for the same set 𝔹_mix with data that agree on the Ω_{i+1}-blocks (V_{i+1}) and differ on the Ω_{i+1}ᶜ-blocks (V_i against V^{(i)}_{□′}). By (3.6)'s two-data representation for 𝔹_mix (VAR Prop. 9 with (1)), Û_{□′} = (exp iη𝐇_mix(X)U_{i+1,□′})^{u^{−1}} with X := (1/i)log[V_i(V^{(i)}_{□′})^{−1}] on the Ω_{i+1}ᶜ-blocks of □′^{∼4} and 0 on its Ω_{i+1}-blocks, off a boundary layer. *Data convention and the layer.* Û's data are M·(Q^{s*}_{i+1}V_{i+1}) on the constraints of 𝔹_mix that are constraints of 𝔹_{i+1}(□′^{∼4}) meeting Ω_{i+1}, and M·(Q^{s*}_iV_i) on the others (p.269's restriction; p.269 prints the data as "M·(Q_k^{s*}V_k)|_{Λ_k∩Ω_{k+1}}", read here as |_{Λ_k∩Ω_{k+1}ᶜ}, consistent with the determining set printed just above it, "𝔹_{k+1}(Λ_{k+1}ᶜ)|_{Ω_{k+1}} ∪ 𝔹_k(Λ_k)|_{Ω_{k+1}ᶜ}"; the printed Ω_{k+1} is a likely misprint, and the transcription carries no note on it). This differs from "the Ω_{i+1}-blocks" only at level-i bonds straddling ∂Ω_{i+1} with their outer endpoint in Ω_{i+1}(□′^{∼4}) and their inner endpoint outside it: a constraint of Λ′_n meeting Ω_{i+1} that is not in Λ_n(□′^{∼4}) has an endpoint in Ω_{n+1}(□′^{∼4})∖Ω_{n+1}, which for n < i is impossible next to Ω_{i+1} ⊆ Ω_{n+2} ((G2)'s distances). These bonds lie in the collar Ω∖Ω_{i+1}(Ω), i.e. in the boundary layer. Û's data are taken with Q^{s*}_iV_i^* (equal to Q^{s*}_iV_i off Ω_{i+1}^{(i)}; V_i itself, for i < k, is integrated at step i+1, p.267, and is not a variable of the term). The exceptional bonds have an endpoint in Ω_{i+1}, so they lie in Ω_{i+1}^{(i)}, where V_i^* = M^iU_{k+1} = V^{(i)}(V_{i+1}^*) (9.1), and (A)'s argument there is exactly 0. In (B), X at these bonds is (1/i)log[V^{(i)}(V^{(i)}_{□′})^{−1}], covered by the layer bound below with V^{(i)}'s regularity from U_{i+1}'s (p.268). (ε₁'s entry 2C_{3.14}ε_i is kept; it is harmless.) On the layer, with X(c) the logarithm of Û's datum against U_{i+1,□′}'s own average: X = 0 on the constraints carrying M·(Q^{s*}_{i+1}V_{i+1}), since they are constraints of 𝔹_{i+1}(□′^{∼4}), where U_{i+1,□′}'s averages are M·(Q^{s*}_{i+1}V_{i+1}) by (2.16) (p.257, at scale i+1). On the others X is the pair M·(Q^{s*}_iV_i), M·(U_{i+1,□′}) of (3.6)'s argument. p.266 bounds that pair on a boundary layer by the reasoning of [14] (1.65), whose output 11d²α₀ + α₁ is linear in the regularity α₀ of the two configurations and in the closeness α₁ of their top averages (REG p.87). On □′^{∼4}'s layer U_{i+1,□′} and V_i are regular, as for V^{(i)}_{□′} below (V_i's regularity from p.256's printed |∂V_i − 1| < O(L²)ε_i on Γ_i, as in (A)), and the i-th averages differ by < O(1)ε_i, as below, so |X| < O(1)L²B₃(1+β₀)ε_i on the layer. This is p.266's boundary-layer reasoning ("or rather by an application of the reasoning" of [14] (1.65)) transferred to □′^{∼4}'s layer, accepted from Bałaban at the status of p.266's sentence, not derived from the supplied REG pages: REG (1.65) (p.87) is stated under (1.33)–(1.35), for configurations in 𝔘_k({Ω_j}, α₀) with U′U₀ ∈ Ax_k(𝔅_k, U₀) (p.82, (1.34)), so the reasoning presupposes the axial gauge, as Bałaban's (3.9) does ("Using the axial gauge for V^{(k)}_{□′}"). That input is χ_Ax at V_i on □′^{∼4}∖□′^{∼2}: by (3.9) on the ring under R-ζ(b), and by region 3 (R-ζ(a)) where □′^{∼4} leaves Ω_{i+1}^∼. U_{i+1,□′} lies in the 𝔹_mix regular space by (iii)'s level comparison with Ω′_n ⊆ Ω_n(□′^{∼4}). V^{(i)}_{□′} on □′^{∼4}, beyond (3.4)'s printed domain (□′^∼)^{(k)}, means M^i(U_{i+1,□′}). This enters (2)(iii)'s ε₁ as 2sup|X|. On (□′^{∼2})^{(i)*}, |X| < 4δ_i by (3.3)'s small-field function on □′ (in force on Ω_{i+1}^{∼2}, p.265) and |(1/i)log x| ≤ 2|x − 1|. On □′^{∼4}∖□′^{∼2}, |X| < O(1)ε_i: V_i and V^{(i)}_{□′} are both regular there (V_i by the step-i small-field functions; V^{(i)}_{□′} by [15] Theorem 1, VAR p.279, with V_{i+1}'s regularity, p.256 and p.264, since (3.2) tests only p ⊂ □′^∼) and have the same (i+1)-averages (V_{i+1} = V̄_i on Ω_{i+1}ᶜ by the δ-function δ(V̄_iV_{i+1}^{−1}); M^{i+1}U_{i+1,□′} = V_{i+1} on every level-(i+1) bond with both blocks in □′^{∼4}: p.267 prints it on □′^∼ only. On the rest, the 2LM₁-collar of □′^{∼4} included, U_{i+1,□′} and Q^{s*}_{i+1}V_{i+1} have the same 𝔹_{i+1}(□′^{∼4}) data by (2.16)'s definition (p.257, at scale i+1). (2)(i)'s induction P(n), run with the sequence Ω_n(□′^{∼4}) and for bonds with both blocks in □′^{∼4}, then gives M^{i+1}U_{i+1,□′} = M^{i+1}(Q^{s*}_{i+1}V_{i+1}) there, since Ω_{i+2}(□′^{∼4}) = ∅. The induction's three cases are: a fine bond of Λ₀(□′^{∼4}) is a datum; a level-n bond with an endpoint in Ω_n(□′^{∼4}) and none in Ω_{n+1}(□′^{∼4}) is a constraint; otherwise its blocks avoid Ω_n(□′^{∼4}) and (0.4) hands it down. Finally M^{i+1}(Q^{s*}_{i+1}V_{i+1}) = V_{i+1}. By induction on n with (0.4) (RG1 p.253) and (1.3) (p.246, at scale i+1), M^n(Q^{s*}_{i+1}V_{i+1}) is 1 on level-n bonds inside one (i+1)-block and V_{i+1}(c̄) on those between B(c̄₋) and B(c̄₊). The reason is that level-n blocks lie inside (i+1)-blocks, so in (0.4)'s loop Γ ∪ [x,x′] ∪ (−Γ′) ∪ (−c) only [x,x′] and c cross the (i+1)-block interface, once each in opposite directions. The loop's value is 1, and Ū(c) = U(c). U_{i+1,□′} and U_{i+1} share only the level-(i+1) data: their lower-level data differ on the 2LM₁ boundary layer, where p.268 bounds (3.17)'s argument by 44d²B₃ε_{k+1}, which is the situation of Lemma 1 [14] in (3.13). *Geometry.* p.245: "∼" adds one layer of the family's cubes, M₂R_i-cubes for □ (an LM₂R_i-cube), and for □′ (an L²M₂R_{i+1}-cube) the LM₂R_{i+1}-cubes (p.245's rule applied by analogy, in T_η-units; not printed on p.265; both conclusions below hold under either reading). R_i and R_{i+1} are powers of L ((2.5)) with R_{i+1} ≤ LR_i and R_i ≤ LR_{i+1} ((2.9)), so R_i/R_{i+1} ∈ {L^{−1}, 1, L}. If R_i = LR_{i+1}, □ and □′ have the same size, so □ = □′ (compatible partitions) and □^{∼m} = □′^{∼m}; otherwise □^{∼4} extends 4M₂R_i ≤ 4M₂R_{i+1} < LM₂R_{i+1} beyond □ ⊂ □′ (L > 4), so □^{∼4} ⊂ □′^∼. In both cases dist(□^∼, □′^{∼4}∖□′^{∼2}) ≥ M₂R_i. *The estimate.* Write the argument of 𝐇_{i,□} on the blocks of □^{∼4}, off the 2M₁-layer, as Y₁ + Y₂ + Y₃ (to first order; the BCH corrections are quadratic in quantities below (1/10)ε_i and are absorbed by |log(gh) − log g − log h| ≤ C|log g||log h|): Y₁ = (1/i)log[V_i(V^{(i)}_{□′})^{−1}] < 4δ_i on the Ω_{i+1}ᶜ-blocks of □′^{∼2} (Bałaban's case, (3.3) with p.266's 4δ_k; when □ = □′, the Ω_{i+1}ᶜ-blocks of □^{∼4}∖□′^{∼2} are treated with the far data below); Y₂ = the (A)-term, < O(1)44d²B₃²(1+β₀)ε_ie^{−R_i} on the Γ_{i+1}-blocks of □′^{∼2} (on Γ_{i+1}-blocks of □^{∼4}∖□′^{∼2}, which occur only when □ = □′ and then lie in □^{∼4}∖□^{∼2}, one M₂R_i-layer from □^∼, the whole argument is < O(1)ε_i, both configurations being regular with the same (i+1)-averages as in (3.13), and it is treated with the far data below); Y₃ = the i-th average of η𝐇_mix on the Γ_{i+1}-blocks, |Y₃(b)| ≤ c₁₂B₃(4δ_i + O(1)ε_iΣ_{b′ far}e^{−δ dist(b,b′)}) by (190) [15] and the passage from 𝐇_mix to the ratio of i-th averages, (3.18)–(3.19)'s step with [12]'s constant c₁₂ (CMP98 (159)–(163), p.42), the sum over the blocks b′ of □′^{∼4}∖□′^{∼2}. By (190) for 𝐇_{i,□} (which bounds ∇𝐇_{i,□} by the same expression, as p.266 uses it; (3.8) needs the gradient) and the same decay for 𝐇_mix, the far data reach □^∼ only through the product of the two decays, over a total distance ≥ dist(□^∼, □′^{∼4}∖□′^{∼2}) ≥ M₂R_i: their contribution to |𝐇_{i,□}| on □^∼ is ≤ C_volB₃²O(1)ε_ie^{−(δ/2)M₂R_i} ≤ (1/40)ε_i for γ small, for any fixed δM₂ > 0, since R_i ≥ (log g_i^{−2})^r (C_vol the polynomial count of the blocks; (3.7) itself needs 2δM₂ ≥ 1). So (3.7) becomes |𝐇_{i,□}| ≤ B₃C_9δ_i + O(1)e^{−R_i}ε_i + (1/40)ε_i + B₃e^{−δ2M₂R_i}30d²L²B₃(1+β₀)ε_i < (B₃C_9A₁/A₀ + O(1)e^{−R_i} + 1/40)ε_i < (1/10)ε_i on □^∼ for A₁/A₀ < 1/(20B₃C_9) and γ small, with C_9 := 4 + 4c₁₂B₃ (Y₁ and the near part of Y₃, with the logarithm's factor 2 and [12]'s constant). C_9 depends on d, L and B₃ only, not on A₁/A₀ or g, so this is (3.7)'s own condition with 4B₃ replaced by B₃C_9 (and the O(1)e^{−R_i} terms, small for γ small since R_i ≥ (log g_i^{−2})^r): A₁/A₀ is chosen after B₃, as (3.7)'s 4B₃A₁/A₀ and (3.19)'s e^{−R_k}A₀/A₁ already require. (3.8) then follows as printed, with (1.43) [14]. **Derived** from (1)–(2) (used twice: in (A) for U_{i+1} against Û_{□′}, in (B) for the two 𝔹_mix-minimisers; (2)(i)'s gluing by the induction P(n) on RG1 Sect. 0's bond convention (p.251), as VAR p.277 and CRE (2.2) use it, and RG1 (0.4) (p.253); for re-review), R-avg, CRE p.256's distances L^nξM₁, VAR Theorem 1, Prop. 9 with (172) and (190), REG Lemma 1 and (1.65) for the boundary layers, and CMP98 (159)–(163) for the averages in (A), each read at the page given, under the g-threshold on ε₁ in (2), and given R-ζ(b) and R-h (used in (a), in (B)'s (3.3) bound and in (3.9) on the ring). So O1 is derived by route (a), given R-ζ(b), R-h and, where □′^{∼4} leaves Ω_{i+1}^∼, R-ζ(a), and p.266's layer reasoning transferred (above), for re-review; route (b) is not needed.
- *Route (b) (not needed; route (a) is derived):* pose (LPG′) on Bałaban's multi-scale regularity class, D2.4(5).
So χ_i(□)(V_i^*) = 1 is derived for □ in the cubes □′ with □′^{∼4} ⊂ Ω_{i+1} (first bullet, by (3.7)–(3.8)) and for 9.2's class (by 9.2's own argument); for □ in the crossing cubes □′ ⊂ Ω_{i+1}^∼ with □′^{∼4} ⊄ Ω_{i+1} it is derived by route (a), given R-ζ(b), R-h and R-ζ(a) beyond Ω_{i+1}^∼. χ_Ax = 1 at V_i^* is derived on Γ_{i+1} and on the ring: on Γ_{i+1}, V_i^* = V^{(i)} satisfies (3.10)'s printed constraint "V_k(y,x) = 1 for y ∈ Γ_{k+1}, x ∈ B(y)" (with Bałaban's gauge representative of the minimum and U_{i+1}(V_{i+1}^*) = U_{k+1}, the induction hypothesis with map (α)); for y ∈ (Ω_{i+1}^∼)^{(i+1)} off Γ_{i+1}, V_i^* = V_i by (2.10)–(2.12) and (3.9) applies verbatim. Further out the χ_Ax factors hold at V_i through R-ζ(a) (map D2.4(5)(2), region 3, which lists χ_Ax). χ_Ax's test form is taken from p.266's use; its definition (1.5) is not read. (O1) is derived (route (a)), given R-ζ(b), R-h and R-ζ(a) beyond Ω_{i+1}^∼, for re-review. (3.13) cannot replace (3.19) (A₁/A₀ is small, p.266).
*9.4 Consequence.* (T-dev)'s induction (D2.4(5)(2)) has its D2.6 input on every band cube: of R14's two exclusions the second class is closed (9.2, given R-ζ(a), R-h and M ≥ 4LM₂), the cubes with □′^{∼4} ⊂ Ω_{i+1} are derived (9.3, from R-avg, [15], [12] and [14] (1.65)'s reasoning at V^{(i)}), and the rest are derived by route (a) given R-ζ(b) and R-h (9.3; O1 derived given R-ζ(b), R-h and R-ζ(a) beyond Ω_{i+1}^∼, for re-review). (T-dev)'s consumers are D2.4(5) *It carries*, D2.4(6), SW §3(b4) and the further users at map l.1418, l.1424, l.1430–1431; D2.4(12)(f)'s c_{{W}} = 0 (the reference activity of the trivial group) at the reference point is by f(0) = 0 (D2.3(3)), not by (T-dev). Since O1 is derived (given R-ζ(b), R-h and R-ζ(a)), (T-dev) has its D2.6 input on the whole support at that status, so the consumer list need not be restricted. The other entries of R14's row: p.256's unlisted "other conditions" enter this closure through (G1)–(G3) of §1, each derived from the printed constructions (p.265 (3.5), p.269 (3.20), and p.256's 2MR_j distance for the RG domains Ω_j, Λ_j as printed, with L^nξM₁ for 𝐁_j(Ω), as (G2) reads); whether (T-dev) itself uses further unlisted conditions is D2.4(5)'s question, not checked here. The extra outer label is D2.2(3)(b)'s item (O2, OPEN as recorded in §8); §6.5's merged activities are (O2′).
